The denominator1+v_plus+r_plus^2 is at least3/2, so

    |r_plus| <= L0 |r|, L0=1/4.

For a level j>=1 step b_j=1+a c^j, one has b_j>=2-epsilon>1.76,
whereas1+v+r^2<=1.76. The numerator is therefore negative, and

    |r_plus| <= Lj |r|, Lj=(2/7)c^j.               (19)

Indeed its absolute value is less than a c^j and its denominator is at
least3/2, while a<3/7. These bounds use the current state in (18) but
do not require the next state to be in that region.

Let P_j be the product of these scalar bounds over the full word H_j,
with P_0=1, and put A0=2/7 and beta=c^2/14<1/2. For j>=1,

    P_j=beta^(2^(j-1))/(A0 c^(j+1)),
    theta_j=P_j Lj=beta^(2^(j-1))/c<1.             (20)

This follows either by multiplying the known counts of each ruler level
or by P_(j+1)=P_j^2 Lj and P_1=1/4. Every prefix product in every H_j
is at most1: it is true for H_0; in the right child the left-child and
central-step prefactor is P_j Lj=theta_j<1, and the induction applies
inside both children. At j=0 that prefactor is1/4.

For the accumulated vertical drift, let E_j be the weighted prefix sum

    E_j=sum_(i in H_j) h_i [product_(t<i)L_(level(t))]^2.

Then E_1=b0<3/2 and, for j>=1,

    E_(j+1)=(1+theta_j^2)E_j+P_j^2 b_j.            (21)

The two needed infinite bounds are elementary. Since c>2,beta<1/2,

    sum_(j>=1)theta_j^2
        <(1/4)sum_(j>=1)2^(-2^j)<=1/8.

Also b_j<c^j for j>=1: both a and c^(-j) are less than3/7. Therefore

    sum_(j>=1)P_j^2 b_j
       <sum_(j>=1)49*2^(-2j-5)=49/96.

For this estimate insert(20), use A0^(-2)=49/4, c>2,
beta^(2^j)<2^(-2^j), and2^j>=j+1. Finally
product(1+theta_j^2)<=1/[1-sum theta_j^2]<8/7,
as follows by expanding the finite products and bounding their elementary
symmetric sums by powers of the total sum. Passing to the limit preserves
the bound. Equations(21) consequently imply

    E_j<(3/2+49/96)(8/7)=193/84<3                 (22)

for every j.

In the suffix of H_m after its first step, every prefix product is at
most4, because reinserting that first L0 multiplies it by1/4. The suffix
weighted drift sum is at most16E_m<48. Hence, as long as(18) has held,

    |r_i|<=4r_1<8/m<=1/16<1/10,
    v_i<=v_1+48r_1^2<5/8+192/m^2<3/4.

The lower bound v_i>=v_1>1/2 follows from v_plus=v+h r^2. These strict
bounds prove(18) throughout by a first-exit argument: the same estimates
control a proposed first exiting step, which is impossible.

At the terminal sample the entire suffix product is4P_m. From(17),(20),

    r_n^2<(64/m^2)P_m^2.

The numerator of(8) is less than2r_n^2 by(18), and the initial denominator
sigma(1+sigma)^2 exceeds98/125>3/4. Thus

    P_par<3r_n^2<(192/m^2)P_m^2
          <(2352/m^2)2^(-2^m),

which proves(15). For the exact benchmark, accepted factb98bb807d514e80d
gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3), so this particular parabolic witness divided
by U_n^ZJ tends to zero. Only this fixed starting rule is excluded.

The responsible state variable is visible in(17): matching the geometric
ratio makes the critical first step almost annihilate the horizontal
coordinate. The subsequent normal state remains near its post-first-step
value, and the complete ruler-product/drift bounds above prevent recovery.
This is a statement about the entire actual legal trajectory, not a
one-pair dual test or a comparison with an independently reset right child.

## 7. The concrete remaining critical-word problem

For the actual critical word fix, within each horizon m,

    c_m=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), a_m=(1-1/m)/c_m,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j).

Let T_h denote the explicit two-state transition (7). The full block map
is defined EXACTLY by

    Phi_0=identity,
    Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j composed with T_(1+a_m c_m^j)
                                     composed with Phi_j.

The central task in this branch is to select explicit r_0(m),v_0(m), or
prove a useful uniform upper bound for this mechanism, while controlling
the exact payoff (8) under Phi_m. The model is already an actual legal
function for every such selection, so no separate interpolation optimization
is required. What is missing is the QUANTITATIVE estimate on that nonlinear
recursion. Neither (11), the small-amplitude calibration, nor (13) fills it.

An open subgoal is to compare the increase in v across both copies of a
dyadic block with the horizontal sign and gain at its central bridge.
The state v must remain shared between the copies; restarting it, freezing
the curvature, or replacing the two copies by independent instances would
change the function and lose the construction.

## 8. Source grounding and verification scope

Jiang, Yue and Zhou, arXiv:1911.12545v2 (2021), Theorem2.2, gives the
Euclidean projection onto S={(x,y):||x||^2<=y}: inside S it is the point,
and outside it is(x/(1+mu),y+mu/2), where mu>=max(0,-2y) is the unique
solution of ||x||^2/(1+mu)^2=y+mu/2. The paper's surrounding definition
of S and full proof were read. It supplies a closely matched projection
mechanism, not a GD complexity result for the present function. Its
cubic-regularization convergence theorem is not invoked.

Sections1--6 derive the two-dimensional specialization and all required
properties directly. No external theorem is used as a black box, and no
unverified earlier project result is a premise. The finite C59 data merely
motivated seeking dense turning gradients. Every new statement here remains
a draft until accepted by the verifier; no claim is made that this family
solves the all-schedule exact-profile problem.

# C61: projection polytopes retain the unrestricted objective supremum

Status: complete self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate. No numerical evaluation
or verifier submission. Accepted predecessor1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the
finite smooth-convex interpolant. The concrete final application also uses
accepted facte9a55f57461f253c. All additional claims are proved below rather
than importing earlier unverified projection-lift findings as premises.

The new point is normalization by the ACTUAL closest minimizer. Moving far
in a direction of minimizers need not increase initial distance. The earlier
primitive-energy epigraph lifts did not establish this objective comparison.

## 1. Statement and exact scope

Fix any integer n>=0 and finite nonnegative physical word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)).
Use the definition of W_n(h) from accepted1252b8823bb5a290, with smoothness1.
For a nonempty compact convex set K containing0 define

  PFun61_K(X)=max_(G in K)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).

Let ProjPoly61_n(h) be the supremum of

  2 PFun61_K(X_n)/dist(X_0,argmin PFun61_K)^2,

over polytopes K=conv(0,G_0,...,G_n) in dimension at most n+1 and actual
GD X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i grad PFun61_K(X_i), with positive initial distance.
The symbols G_i in this display are the actual sampled gradients, including
the terminal gradient; repetition and redundant vertices are allowed.

The claim is

                       ProjPoly61_n(h)=W_n(h).                 (1)

This is an equality of SUPREMA; attainment in the projection subclass is
not asserted. Every n+2-vertex polytope is permitted, with arbitrary real
Gram matrix. There is no fixed dimension, conditioning, face-angle, jump,
normal-support, or correlation restriction independent of n. The closest
minimizer is generally NOT the origin, even though0 is a minimizer.

Before taking the compact hull, a quantitative construction uses one
polyhedral epigraph in one extra coordinate. Its minimizer set is exactly
a vertical ray, its closest-minimizer distance is EXACTLY the old distance,
and its normalized objective loss has an explicit O(Lambda^(-2)) bound.
Every queried step and every ordered interpolation pair are retained.

Equation(1) reformulates the full lower-witness problem. It supplies no
choice of K meeting the Zhang--Jiang bound for arbitrary h, and no scalable
description of such a polytope on the near-critical ruler.

## 2. Elementary projection and GD bounds used below

For any nonempty closed convex K containing0, completing the square shows
that the unique maximizer defining PFun61_K is the Euclidean projection
P_K(X). The minimizer of the squared distance exists by coercivity and
closedness, and is unique by strict convexity. Its normal inequality is

  <X-P_K(X),G-P_K(X)><=0 for every G in K.                     (2)

Adding the inequalities for two points gives

  ||P_K(X)-P_K(Y)||^2<=<P_K(X)-P_K(Y),X-Y>.                   (3)

Evaluating the two defining suprema at their maximizers shows
0<=PFun61_K(Y)-PFun61_K(X)-<P_K(X),Y-X>
 <=<P_K(Y)-P_K(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.
Thus PFun61_K is finite, convex, differentiable,1-smooth and has gradientP_K.
It is nonnegative and has minimum0 because0 belongs to K.

For any convex1-smooth function F, the necessary two-point interpolation
inequalities in accepted1252b8823bb5a290 imply, on adding them,

  <grad F(x)-grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2.

Consequently T_h(x)=x-h grad F(x) is Lipschitz with constant

  nu(h)=max(1,h-1), h>=0.                                    (4)

Indeed the squared difference of the outputs is at most
||x-y||^2+h(h-2)||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2. If h<=2 this is
at most||x-y||^2. If h>2, use the1-Lipschitz gradient bound to obtain
(h-1)^2||x-y||^2. Formula(4) includes h=0 and is also valid for PFun61_K.

Put S=sum_i h_i and A=product_i nu(h_i), with empty product1.
Every partial product and every consecutive product of the nu(h_i) is
at most A because every factor is at least1. If F has a minimizer x_star
and R=||x_0-x_star||, then ||x_i-x_star||<=R A at EVERY queried or terminal
sample. These are elementary finite bounds, not an asymptotic estimate.

## 3. A finite max-affine conjugate core

For now assume a finite nonnegative convex max-affine function psi61 on R^d
is identically0 on a neighborhood of0. Write

  psi61(p)=max(0,max_(j in J){<y_j,p>-delta_j}),
  B=max_(j in J)||y_j||,                                     (5)

where J is finite; take B=0 for the empty family. It is sufficient that
all nonzero y_j have delta_j>0. Terms with y_j=0 and delta_j>=0 can be
removed. Thus psi61 is globally B-Lipschitz, psi61(0)=0, and
0<=psi61(p)<=B||p||. Every subgradient of psi61 has norm at most B: it is a
convex combination of slopes of the active affine functions.

Set

  F61_core(x)=max_p{<x,p>-||p||^2/2-psi61(p)}.                      (6)

The maximizing p is the unique minimizer of
||p-x||^2/2+psi61(p). The strong-subgradient and maximizing-difference
arguments from accepted1252b8823bb5a290 give that F61_core is convex1-smooth
with this maximizer as its gradient. The same arguments also prove these
properties directly for(6). Since psi61>=0 and psi61(0)=0,0 is a minimizer
of F61_core with value0. The flat neighborhood makes it the UNIQUE minimizer:
if x!=0, choose a sufficiently small positive multiple p of x in that
neighborhood; then <x,p>-||p||^2/2>0.

Fix x_0!=0 and run actual GD for F61_core with the supplied full word h.
Use z_i for these original iterates, z_0=x_0, and R=||x_0||.

## 4. The exact epigraph trajectory and its closest minimizer

For any Lambda>0, put e=Lambda^(-2) and define the closed convex polyhedron

  K61_Lambda={(p,s) in R^(d+1): Lambda*s>=psi61(p)}.               (7)

Run actual GD for PFun61_(K61_Lambda) from

  X_0=(x_0,-Lambda).

Write X_i=(x_i,-Lambda*tau_i), with tau_0=1. At any point with tau>=1,
the projection onto(7) has the form

  G=(p,psi61(p)/Lambda),
  p=argmin_q {||q-x||^2/2+tau*psi61(q)+e*psi61(q)^2/2}.           (8)

For fixed q the optimal vertical coordinate is its lowest permitted
coordinate psi61(q)/Lambda, because the point's vertical coordinate is
negative and psi61>=0. Substitution into squared distance proves(8).
The objective in(8) is strongly convex and coercive: psi61 and psi61^2 are
convex, the latter because psi61 is nonnegative. Hence there is one p.
In particular this is a definition of an actual projection, not a relaxed
interpolation or numerical feasibility prescription.

The actual GD recursion is EXACTLY

  x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i p_i,
  tau_(i+1)=tau_i+e*h_i*psi61(p_i).                             (9)

Thus every tau_i>=1. The same K61_Lambda is retained at all steps.

Its function has precisely the minimizer set

  argmin PFun61_(K61_Lambda)={(0,t):t<=0}.                        (10)

For such a point every inner product with(p,s) in K61_Lambda is nonpositive,
so0 maximizes. Conversely, if the horizontal part x is nonzero, a small
p in the flat neighborhood of psi61, of the sign/direction of x, with s=0,
makes <x,p>-||p||^2/2 positive. If the vertical coordinate t is positive,
the permitted vector(0,s) with small s>0 makes ts-s^2/2 positive. These
arguments prove(10) in all cases.

It follows that the closest minimizer to X_0 is EXACTLY

  X_star=(0,-Lambda),  dist(X_0,argmin PFun61_(K61_Lambda))=R.    (11)

No Lambda^2 enters the initial squared distance. The initial norm relative
to the OTHER minimizer0 would be R^2+Lambda^2 and is not the normalization
used here.

## 5. Every interpolation pair, including the closest minimizer

Let psi61_i=psi61(p_i). Optimality in(8) gives a subgradient
s_i in partial psi61(p_i) with

  x_i-p_i=alpha_i s_i, alpha_i=tau_i+e*psi61_i>0.

For a finite max-affine psi61 this chain rule follows, for example, by taking
directional derivatives: the derivative of t->tau*t+e*t^2/2 at psi61_i is
the positive number alpha_i, so the subdifferential of its composition is
alpha_i partial psi61(p_i). Hence

  X_i-G_i=(alpha_i s_i,-Lambda alpha_i).

Use the actual value f_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2. Direct expansion of the
signed interpolation residual gives

  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2
      =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>
      =alpha_j[<s_j,p_i-p_j>-(psi61_i-psi_j)]<=0.              (12)

The last sign is the full convex subgradient inequality for psi61. It is
valid for EVERY ordered pair, irrespective of their temporal order.
For the optimum sample in(11), set p_star=0,psi_star=0,tau_star=1,s_star=0.
Flatness makes0 the subgradient there. Formula(12) still applies, giving
in particular Q_(i,star)=-psi61_i<=0. The lifted data therefore retain all
queried, terminal and optimum comparisons with the actual normalization.

## 6. An explicit finite-horizon error bound

All quantities in this section refer to the same fixed h and psi61.
The full GD distance estimate(4), applied about X_star, gives
||X_i-X_star||<=R A and hence ||x_i||<=R A. The projection optimality(8)
also gives ||p_i||<=||x_i||. To see this, write x_i-p_i=alpha_i s_i;
the subgradient inequality at0 gives <s_i,p_i>>=psi61_i>=0, and so
<x_i,p_i>>=||p_i||^2. Thus

  psi61_i<=B R A,
  0<=tau_i-1<=e*S*B R A.                                   (13)

Let p^0=grad F61_core(x_i), so x_i-p^0=s^0 in partial psi61(p^0).
Subtract the two optimality conditions and use subgradient monotonicity:

  ||p_i-p^0||^2
    <=-(alpha_i-1)<s_i,p_i-p^0>
    <=(alpha_i-1)B||p_i-p^0||.

If the difference vanishes there is nothing to divide; otherwise divide
by its positive norm. Since alpha_i-1=(tau_i-1)+e psi61_i, (13) implies

  ||p_i-grad F61_core(x_i)||<=e B^2 R A(S+1).                  (14)

Compare(9) with z_(i+1)=T_(h_i)(z_i) for the same F61_core. Formula(4)
and(14), iterated from x_0=z_0, give at every sample

  ||x_i-z_i||<=e B^2 R A^2 S(S+1).                          (15)

Explicitly each error contribution h_j times the right side of(14) is
multiplied by a subsequent product of nu(h_t), at most A; summing h_j
gives(15). No assumption of uniformly small A or S is made.

At X=(x,-Lambda*tau), formula(8) also yields the value identity

  PFun61_(K61_Lambda)(X)
    =max_p{<x,p>-||p||^2/2-tau psi61(p)-e psi61(p)^2/2}.         (16)

This is at most F61_core(x). Evaluating its maximum at p^0=grad F61_core(x)
gives a reverse lower bound differing by at most
(tau-1)psi61(p^0)+e psi61(p^0)^2/2. At the terminal point, ||p^0||<=||x_n||
<=R A, so(13) bounds this loss by e B^2 R^2 A^2(S+1/2).
Both x_n and z_n lie in the radius R A ball; the gradient of F61_core has
norm at most R A throughout their line segment. Therefore(15) gives

  PFun61_(K61_Lambda)(X_n)
     >=F61_core(z_n)-e R^2 C61_lift(h),

  C61_lift(h)=B^2 A^2[A S(S+1)+S+1/2].                        (17)

Combining with(11), the normalized objective loss is at most

                         2 C61_lift(h)/Lambda^2.              (18)

Every constant is explicit and finite for every finite nonnegative word,
including zeros and the empty word. It is not claimed to be economical.

## 7. Every legal objective can be approximated by such a flat core

Take any original convex differentiable1-smooth F with minimizer0,
F(0)=0 and initial x_0!=0, R=||x_0||. It is harmless if0 was not its
closest minimizer: this is still one of the admissible normalizations in
the definition of W_n(h). For 0<beta<1 set

  F_beta(x)=(1-beta)F(x)+(beta/4)||x||^2.                    (19)

It is beta/2-strongly convex, has UNIQUE minimizer0, and its gradient is
L_beta-Lipschitz with L_beta=1-beta/2<1. It approaches F with its gradient
uniformly on bounded sets. The following explicit finite bound avoids any
unquantified exchange of limits. Let z_i and z_i^beta be their actual
trajectories for h. Both have norm at most R A. On this ball,

  ||grad F_beta-grad F||<=3 beta R A/2,
  |F_beta-F|<=3 beta R^2 A^2/4.

The first inequality follows from ||grad F(x)||<=||x|| and the second
from 0<=F(x)<=||x||^2/2. Iterating(4) as in(15) gives
||z_n^beta-z_n||<=3 beta R A^2 S/2. Consequently the normalized terminal
objective for F_beta differs from that for F by at most

                  beta[3 A^3 S+(3/2)A^2].                 (20)

Use its complete finite jets, including sample n and the zero optimum.
At each finite sample let y_i=z_i^beta-grad F_beta(z_i^beta) and
delta_i=F_beta(z_i^beta)-||grad F_beta(z_i^beta)||^2/2.
The smooth descent inequality with step1/L_beta gives
F_beta(z_i^beta)>=||grad F_beta(z_i^beta)||^2/(2L_beta).
Thus delta_i>0 unless the gradient vanishes. In the latter case strong
convexity gives z_i^beta=0 and y_i=0.

It follows that the finite function

  psi61(p)=max(0,max_(0<=i<=n){<y_i,p>-delta_i})                (21)

has exactly the properties in(5), in particular a flat neighborhood of0.
For example take a positive radius smaller than every
delta_i/||y_i|| with y_i!=0; if none exist, psi61=0 everywhere.
By accepted1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraph2 applied to the complete
finite index set, F61_core interpolates ALL these jets. Its actual GD for
the unchanged word therefore has the same trajectory and terminal value
as F_beta. This invocation uses only the accepted all-pair construction,
not a reduced or conjecturally legal prefix.

Given any desired normalized loss eta>0, choose beta in(0,1) with the
right side of(20) below eta/2. Then form(21) and choose Lambda>0 with
2C61_lift(h)/Lambda^2<eta/2. Equations(18)--(21) give a real projection-function
instance of normalized payoff at least the original payoff minus eta.
Its exact closest-minimizer distance remains R.

## 8. Compactification and dimension compression prove(1)

For the lifted instance just constructed let G_0,...,G_n be its ACTUAL
gradients and put

  K_fin=conv(0,G_0,...,G_n).

This is a compact polytope contained in K61_Lambda. For every sample i,
G_i belongs to K_fin and was already the unique maximizer over K61_Lambda.
It is consequently also the unique maximizer over K_fin, with the SAME
function value. Induction on the actual GD recursion proves that replacing
K61_Lambda by K_fin preserves every sample, gradient and value in the entire
word, not just its endpoint.

The point X_star=(0,-Lambda) remains a minimizer for the compact hull:
its inner product with each G_i is -psi61_i<=0 and with0 is0. Therefore

  dist(X_0,argmin PFun61_(K_fin))<=||X_0-X_star||=R.            (22)

Shrinking this denominator cannot decrease a nonnegative objective ratio.
If the terminal value is positive, initial distance is positive, since
starting at a minimizer would make the actual GD stationary.

Finally let V=span(G_0,...,G_n), of dimension at most n+1, and orthogonally
project all X_i onto V. The function PFun61_(K_fin) depends only on this
projection, every gradient is already in V, and the same GD recursion and
values hold there. Its full minimizer set is the minimizer set inside V
plus V-perp, so its true initial distance is EXACTLY preserved by this
compression. Choose an orthonormal basis of V to realize it as R^dim(V).
The compact hull still has at most n+2 listed vertices.

This proves ProjPoly61_n(h)>=W_n(h) by arbitrarily accurate approximation of
each positive-payoff legal instance. Conversely every member of this
projection subclass is convex1-smooth with an attained minimum and positive
initial distance, so its ratio is at most W_n(h) by definition. This proves(1).
The positive value of W_n(h), including n=0, is part of the accepted fact;
it also follows by choosing a sufficiently small scalar quadratic curvature.

## 9. Concrete consequence for the now-verified C59 word

Accepted e9a55f57461f253c provides the actual63-step H6 word with epsilon1/6,
and an explicit function F59 in R66 whose conjugate core is the finite
maximum of0 and the planes <Y_i,p>-delta_i. Its proof establishes every
nonstar delta_i>0, so it ALREADY satisfies(5); no perturbation(19) is needed.
Let B=max_i||Y_i||, let A,S have their actual finite values for this H6,
and define C=C61_lift(h59) by(17). All are specified by that accepted fact.

Choose the explicit positive parameter

                    Lambda^2=1+(271100/89)C.

Then the loss in(18) is strictly smaller than89/135550, and

                    56/2711-89/135550=1/50.

Thus the epigraph projection function in R67 has the same true initial
distance as C59 and an actual H6 payoff>1/50>4/M59. Compactification and
span compression give a projection polytope with at most65 vertices in
dimension at most64 with true-distance payoff>1/50>4/M59.

This corollary remains UNVERIFIED pending a separately allocated gate.
It inherits the EXACT epsilon1/6 scope of its accepted input. It does not
assert that the compressed projection function has a unique minimizer,
or that its gradients are the original C59 gradients. No claim about its
six-jump geometry or changed-parameter H_m subwords is made here.

## 10. What this changes and what remains missing

The projection-function route itself is not too small for the objective
problem when true closest-minimizer distance is allowed: arbitrary compact
polytopes already have the full supremum, in the sense of(1). Earlier scoped
failures of cones, distance-squared functions, incidence relays, six-jump
coordinates, or special parabolas do not cover this class.

The target requires an explicit useful K or a lower bound on the entire
polytope game for each physical word. In the actual critical sequence
c_m=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/m), a_m=(1-1/m)/c_m,
H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j), parameters remain fixed within
each full horizon. This construction cannot replace them by the finite
C59 parameters or reset the vertical state in the right child.

For a chosen near-optimal full transcript, all the information about the
unknown solution is still present in psi61's planes and their joint Gram.
Equation(18) makes any desired finite accuracy attainable, but is not an
analytic policy for choosing those planes as m grows. The compactification
is performed only after the whole actual trajectory, retaining all its
normal comparisons. Treating vertices or copies independently would lose
the load-bearing joint constraints.

Matlas and web background searches were performed for epigraph projection
and smooth interpolation. No retrieved paper theorem is used as a premise.
The proof above is elementary apart from the explicitly cited accepted
finite interpolation construction. Prior shared primitive-lift candidates
motivated the closest-distance check but are not correctness sources.

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010-full-evidence.zip!/project/research/max3_c62_anchored_simplex_normalization.md

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000720 -->
# C62: anchor the simplex conversion at the actual minimum

Status: UNVERIFIED complete refinement, saved before switching to the new
C60 trajectory TASK. No solver or verifier call. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290
supplies necessary interpolation inequalities; accepted e9a55f57461f253c
supplies the final finite example. Earlier C27 and C61 are not premises.

## Exact independent-gradient conversion

Suppose a legal convex1-smooth GD trajectory has minimizer0, value0 there,
jets(x_i,g_i,f_i), and linearly independent sampled gradients g_0,...,g_m.
Its steps are any supplied nonnegative finite steps for which the samples
are distinct; zero-step repetitions can subsequently be restored. Set

  I_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=0,
  A_ij=<g_i,g_j>,
  w=-sum_j(A^(-1)I)_j g_j,
  X_i=x_i+w, C=conv(0,g_0,...,g_m).

The Gram A is positive definite and <w,g_i>=-I_i. For every i,j, expansion
using the displayed definition of I gives

  <X_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>
    =f_j-f_i+<g_j,x_i-x_j>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
    =Q_(j,i)<=0.

For the extra vertex0, the normal comparison is
<X_i-g_i,-g_i>=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2=Q_(star,i)<=0.
Consequently P_C(X_i)=g_i, by expanding squared distances to each vertex
and then using convex combinations. The fixed function

  F_C(X)=max_(g in C)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2)

has gradient P_C: projection normal inequalities give firm nonexpansiveness,
and comparing the maximizing expressions gives differentiability. Thus
F_C is globally convex1-smooth and its actual GD visits exactly X_i.
Its values are EXACTLY the original values:

  F_C(X_i)=<x_i+w,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2=f_i.

Moreover w is an actual minimizer because <w,g_i>=-I_i<=0 for all vertices,
and0 is a vertex. It follows that

  dist(X_0,argmin F_C)<=||X_0-w||=||x_0||.                   (1)

Hence the TRUE normalized objective cannot decrease. All gradients, all
their inner products and jumps, every function value, and every physical
step are preserved EXACTLY. No common value shift was made. Projection
onto V=span(g_0,...,g_m) preserves all these data and true closest distance:
F_C depends only on V, and its minimizer set is its minimizer set in V plus
V-perp. The resulting simplex has dimension m+1 and m+2 vertices.

This is compatible with C27's dilution examples: arbitrary translations
with a common added value need not preserve a normalized objective. The
specific translation here uses the slacks relative to the original TRUE
minimum0 and keeps every f_i unchanged. The known minimizer w in(1) is
the missing normalization control.

## Uniform relative approximation by strictly independent gradients

For an arbitrary legal trajectory with R=||x_0||>0, first compress all zero
steps, leaving m strictly positive steps. In R^(m+1) choose

  theta_l=(l+1)/(2(m+2)), 0<=l<=m,
  D=diag(theta_l), z_0=(1,...,1)/sqrt(m+1), q(z)=<z,Dz>/2.

Run that SAME compressed schedule on q. Its gradients at indices0,...,m
are linearly independent: their l-th coordinates are
theta_l product_(t<i)(1-h_t theta_l)/sqrt(m+1); the polynomials of degrees
0,...,m in this expression form a basis, since all compressed h_t>0, and
evaluation at the m+1 distinct nonzero theta_l is invertible. For m=0
the single gradient is nonzero and the same conclusion holds.

Every finite pair of distinct samples has strict interpolation slack:
Q_ij^q=-(z_i-z_j)^T D(I-D)(z_i-z_j)/2<0. The positions are distinct
because the difference of the corresponding polynomials has degree at
most m and cannot vanish at all m+1 nodes. Every z_i is nonzero for the
same root-counting reason, giving strict slacks in both optimum directions.

For any t>0 use the direct-sum function F(x,z)=f(x)+q(z), with initial
point(x_0,tR z_0). This is convex1-smooth and has a minimizer0. Its exact
jets are(x_i,tR z_i),(g_i,tR D z_i),f_i+t^2R^2 q(z_i).
All compressed gradients are independent, and all distinct finite/optimum
interpolation inequalities are strict. Its initial squared norm is
R^2(1+t^2), whereas its terminal value is at least the original f_n.
Applying the anchored conversion just proved therefore gives a strict
simplex projection trajectory with true normalized objective at least

              [2 f_n/R^2]/(1+t^2).                         (2)

The relative factor is independent of the number, magnitudes and order of
steps. Restoring zero steps repeats the corresponding samples and changes
nothing. Taking t to0 proves that the supremum over STRICT simplex vertex
projection trajectories of dimension m+1 is the full W_n(h). This remains
a supremum assertion; finite t perturbs the original function and can make
the Gram extremely ill-conditioned. No conditioning restriction is implied.

## Correct closest-distance Gram game

For an independent simplex let A be its gradient Gram and b_i=<g_i,X_0>.
Let T_ii=1,T_ij=h_j for j<i, and T_ij=0 for j>i, using compressed indices.
Every projection normal comparison is exactly

  b_j-b_i<=(TA)_ij-(TA)_ii,  b_i>=(TA)_ii.                   (3)

The final objective is

  2F_C(X_m)=2b_m-2sum_(i<m)h_i A_im-A_mm.                   (4)

The TRUE initial squared distance to the polar minimizer cone is

  R_C^2=max_(u>=0)[2b^T u-u^T A u].                        (5)

Here u is a vector of nonnegative coefficients, not a physical iterate.
To prove(5), minimize ||X_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2 over u>=0. Positive
definiteness makes this coercive and strictly convex. At its minimizer,
Au-b>=0 and u_i(Au-b)_i=0, by the one-sided coordinate derivatives. Put
p=sum_i u_i g_i and z=X_0-p. Then z is in the polar cone and <p,z>=0.
For any polar point y, expansion gives
||X_0-y||^2=||p||^2+||z-y||^2-2<p,y>>=||p||^2,
with equality at y=z. Thus the distance is ||p||^2=u^TAu=2b^Tu-u^TAu,
which proves(5). Orthogonal components of X_0 were discarded as above.

Together(2)--(5) give a lossless objective supremum over the COMPLETE
normal-inequality simplex game with the polar distance denominator. The
older expression b^T A^(-1)b is distance to the chosen minimizer0, and
cannot replace(5) in this objective formulation. No inequality comparing
this full game to the benchmark has been proved.

## Exact stronger finite interpretation of accepted C59

In accepted e9a55f57461f253c the rational core gradients are rows1,...,64
of the lower triangular65by65 integer matrix T divided by10^15. All64
diagonal entries T_ii,1<=i<=64, are strictly positive; their minimum is
8124849466. This follows by reading the frozen defining integers in the
accepted proof. A read-only extraction matched the frozen JSON SHA256
7376e26ca81edc6438c0563e008c845ce439e424bd42f9f65c37a07867d6b113.
The triangular submatrix on columns1,...,64 is therefore invertible.
The64 core gradients are independent, and adding their auxiliary coordinate
does not destroy that independence.

Apply the exact conversion with the actual C59 values and gradients. After
span compression this gives a65-vertex simplex projection function in R64
for the SAME actual H6 epsilon1/6 word, with exactly the C59 full gradient
Gram, exactly its function values, and true initial distance at most R59.
Its payoff is therefore>56/2711>1/50>4/M59. Its jump Gram is unchanged,
so the accepted six-jumps obstruction also holds for this trajectory.
All C59 original ordered residuals were strictly negative, so every sampled
vertex normal is strict against every distinct simplex vertex including0.

This new corollary and the general reduction remain UNVERIFIED; only the
input C59 fact is accepted. No new verification or numerical solve is used.
The conclusion is still finite epsilon1/6; it supplies no analytic simplex
geometry for the changing-parameter H_m family.

## DOC-000722 — archive_full_20261010/project/research/max3_c63_parabolic_shared_state_bound.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010-full-evidence.zip!/project/research/max3_c63_parabolic_shared_state_bound.md

原始字节数：17936；原始 SHA-256：6c4a7b12eb91adc0bdfdf79b0d9839d1a64f8b00afa4617476719122ab88f785

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000722 -->
# C63: a shared-state block bound closes the large horizontal parabolic regime

Status: complete UNVERIFIED analytic candidate following the bounded direct
C60 experiment. No new fact_submit. Accepted b98bb807d514e80d is used for
the exact benchmark definitions; every further estimate below is derived
internally. C60's unverified formulas are reproduced and proved here.

The result is uniform over an explicit domain of initial states and, in
Section8, over EVERY trajectory with at most one horizontal sign change.
It is NOT an upper bound on every parabolic start or on unrestricted W_n(h).

## 1. Result, with the actual physical parameters

For m>=5 put

  lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
  n=2^m-1, S=sum H_m, M=1+S.

All these parameters remain fixed throughout the complete word H_m.
Consider the ONE function and initial state defined in Section2. Its
parameters are r_0!=0,v_0>=0. Let

  R=|r_0|(1+v_0+r_0^2),
  E_par(S)=(2S+3)^2/[8(S+1)^2(2S+1)].

If

                     R >= S sqrt(c-2),                     (1)

then every r_i has the same strict sign as r_0, and its actual normalized
last-iterate objective satisfies

  P_par(H_m;r_0,v_0)<=E_par(S)<1/(2S+1).                    (2)

In particular(1) holds uniformly for ALL initial states

                     r_0^2>=M^(2/3), v_0>=0.               (3)

For the EXACT Zhang--Jiang benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n from the accepted fact,

  B_(2^m-1) sup_(states satisfying(1)) P_par
       <=lambda^(m+1) E_par(S),
  limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup_(states satisfying(1)) P_par
       <=lambda/[4 exp(1)]<1/4.                            (4)

No asymptotic equality for B_n is assumed. Section6 proves the scalar upper
envelope needed in(4) directly from its exact finite recurrence.

The payoff bound in(2) also holds for ANY finite nonnegative schedule of
total mass S>=1/2 whenever its whole parabolic trajectory keeps one sign
(allowing absorption at zero). Thus the candidate bound is not inferred
from the finite sample portfolio. What is particular to the ruler is the
proved sufficient initial-state condition(1).

## 2. One global legal function, every pair, and true distance

In R2 define

  K_par={(t,s):s>=t^2/sqrt(2)},
  F_par(X)=max_(g in K_par)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2).

The maximizer is the unique Euclidean projection onto this nonempty closed
convex set: complete the square, use coercivity for existence and strict
convexity for uniqueness. The normal inequalities at two projection points
give firm nonexpansiveness. Comparing the defining maxima shows that F_par
is differentiable with gradient equal to the projection; it is globally
convex and1-smooth. In detail, for the projection p(X),
||p(X)-p(Y)||^2<=<p(X)-p(Y),X-Y>, and
0<=F_par(Y)-F_par(X)-<p(X),Y-X>
 <=<p(Y)-p(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.

For r real and v>=0 put

  u=v+r^2,
  X(r,v)=(r(1+v+r^2),-v/sqrt(2)),
  G(r)=(r,r^2/sqrt(2)).

One has X-G=u(r,-1/sqrt(2)). For any(t,s) in K_par,
<X-G,(t,s)-G><=-(u/2)(t-r)^2<=0. Thus G(r) is the actual gradient
at X(r,v), and substitution gives

  F_par(X(r,v))=r^2(1+v)/2+3r^4/4.                          (5)

For EVERY ordered pair of such samples the complete interpolation residual is

  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2
      =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>
      =-(v_j+r_j^2)(r_i-r_j)^2/2<=0.                       (6)

The identity follows by writing f_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2 and expanding.
There is no adjacent-only interpolation condition in this construction.

The minimizer set of F_par is exactly{(0,y):y<=0}. Such a point gives a
nonpositive inner product with every feasible gradient, and0 is feasible.
A point with positive vertical coordinate has a positive value by testing
(0,s) with small positive s. A point with nonzero horizontal coordinate
has a positive value by testing(t,t^2/sqrt(2)) with small t of the same
horizontal sign; the linear term dominates the quadratic and quartic terms.
Hence the closest minimizer to X(r_0,v_0) is(0,-v_0/sqrt(2)), and the
true squared distance is R^2=r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2.

For every physical step h>=0 actual GD is described by

  v_plus=v+h r^2,
  r_plus^3+(1+v_plus)r_plus=r(1+v+r^2-h).                  (7)

The cubic's derivative is1+v_plus+3r_plus^2>=1, so it has exactly one real
solution. The horizontal and vertical coordinates in(7) are exactly those
of X-hG. This proves actual GD for the same global F_par at every step.
The normalized payoff is

  P_par=[r_n^2(1+v_n)+(3/2)r_n^4]/R^2.                    (8)

Reflection of the horizontal coordinate sends r to-r and leaves all values
and v unchanged. Thus it suffices to prove the remaining statements with
r_0>0. If r ever becomes zero it remains zero by(7) and the payoff is zero.

## 3. Quantitative block map with one shared vertical state

First suppose a consecutive actual block of total mass T has positive
r-values throughout. At one step, the sign is retained exactly when
h<1+v+r^2. The increasing cubic in(7), evaluated at the OLD positive r,
exceeds its right side by h r(1+r^2)>=0. Consequently

                       0<r_plus<=r.                       (9)

Write the entry state(r_A,v_A), exit state(r_B,v_B), and horizontal
coordinate x=r(1+v+r^2). Telescoping the two ACTUAL recursions gives

  Delta_x=x_A-x_B=sum_(block)h_i r_i,
  Delta_v=v_B-v_A=sum_(block)h_i r_i^2.

Because r_B<=r_i<=r_A, the following complete finite bounds hold:

  T r_B<=Delta_x<=T r_A,
  r_B Delta_x<=Delta_v<=r_A Delta_x,
  T r_B^2<=Delta_v<=T r_A^2,
  Delta_x^2<=T Delta_v.                                   (10)

The final inequality is weighted Cauchy--Schwarz; at T=0 both sides vanish.
In particular the nonlinear block map obeys

  r_B[1+v_B+r_B^2+T]<=r_A(1+v_A+r_A^2).                   (11)

These estimates compose along H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), where b_j=1+a c^j,
by using the ACTUAL exit(r_B,v_B) of the left block as the bridge input,
then the bridge's exit as the right block input. The right copy's v is
never restarted or frozen. Equations(10)--(11) therefore control full
nonlinear blocks with all finite masses and all inherited v explicitly
present; no limiting differential equation is used.

## 4. The first possible turn has a quantitative initial-radius cost

Consider a first step whose output r is nonpositive. All preceding states
were positive and decreasing by(9). Let P be the total earlier step mass,
h this step, r its positive input, v its input vertical state, q=r^2,
and u=v+q. Equation(7) forces h>=1+u. Also the prefix bounds(10) give

  v-v_0>=P q,  x_0-x<=sqrt(P(v-v_0)),  x=r(1+u).

Therefore v>=Pq, u<=h-1, and

  x_0<=h sqrt(q)+sqrt(Pv).

For fixed u with v=u-q, the condition v>=Pq says 0<=q<=u/(P+1).
On this interval h sqrt(q)+sqrt(P(u-q)) is nondecreasing in q. For P>0
this follows by differentiation, because h>=1 and u-q>=Pq imply
h^2(u-q)>=Pq. For P=0 it follows directly. Its maximum is therefore
(P+h)sqrt(u/(P+1)). We have proved the necessary condition for a first
turn OR a first annihilation:

  x_0<=(P+h) sqrt[(h-1)/(P+1)].                             (12)

This estimate retains all previous gradient energy in v-v_0; it does not
replace the old trajectory by an independent block instance.

For the actual ruler let S_j=sum H_j and M_j=1+S_j, retaining the SAME
c,a from the enclosing horizon m. Directly from the concatenation,

  M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j,
  M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j, A_mass=a/(c-2).             (13)

Here 0<a<c-2. Indeed, writing epsilon=1/m and using lambda^2=2lambda+1,

  c(c-2)-(1-epsilon)
    =epsilon(2lambda+3)+lambda^2 epsilon^2>0.

Thus0<A_mass<1, and for every level j

                      a c^j<(c-2) M_j.                    (14)

Before any level-j step the elapsed prefix mass P is at least S_j: the
first occurrence of that level is immediately after its left H_j, and
every later occurrence is later in the positive word. Since h-1=a c^j,

                (h-1)/(P+1)<c-2,  P+h<=S.                 (15)

Combining(12)--(15) makes x_0<S sqrt(c-2) necessary for any first turn or
annihilation. This contradicts(1), proving strict sign preservation for
the ENTIRE actual word, at every depth and every queried sample.

For m>=5, c-2<1: lambda<5/2 and1+1/m<=6/5 give c<3. If(3) holds,
R=|r_0|(1+v_0+r_0^2)>=|r_0|^3>=M>S sqrt(c-2). This proves the claimed
uniform inclusion without depending on any observed numerical state.

## 5. Exact terminal payoff bound for every one-sign trajectory

Apply(10)--(11) to the entire one-sign run, whose total mass is S>=1/2.
Let q=r_n^2 and w=1+v_n. The actual initial horizontal coordinate obeys

  R>=r_n(S+w+q),  w>=1+S q.

If r_n=0 the desired bound holds because the numerator vanishes. Otherwise
(8) gives

  P_par<=(w+(3/2)q)/(S+w+q)^2.                             (16)

Set t=w+q. The constraint on w gives t>=1+(S+1)q, so q<= (t-1)/(S+1).
Consequently the right side of(16) is at most

              [(2S+3)t-1]/[2(S+1)(S+t)^2], t>=1.

Its derivative has the sign of(2S+3)(S-t)+2. At S>=1/2 the maximizing
point t=S+2/(2S+3) lies in the stated domain. Substitution gives exactly

                 E_par(S)=(2S+3)^2/[8(S+1)^2(2S+1)].       (17)

Moreover E_par(S)<1/(2S+1), because
8(S+1)^2-(2S+3)^2=4S^2+4S-1>0 at S>=1/2. Finally
S E_par(S) tends to1/4. This proves(2) and a uniform bound on every
one-sign initial state, not only the sufficient domain(1).

## 6. Exact finite benchmark comparison

From accepted b98bb807d514e80d define P_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1.
Their recurrences, after substituting those shifts, are P_0=B_0=1 and

  P_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(P_k,P_l),
  B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(P_k,B_l),
  Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
  G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

Here is a full proof of the needed comparator, so the earlier unverified
C54 calculation is not used as a premise. Set alpha=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda)
and Llin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Both coefficients of Llin exceed1, so
Llin>=x+y. Direct expansion, using lambda^2=2lambda+1, gives

  alpha^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]
                   =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda y)^2>=0.

Thus Llin>=Omega(x,y), the positive root of z^2-(x+y)z-xy=0. At
z=lambda Omega(x,y), the polynomial with larger root G(x,lambda y) obeys

  z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2
      =x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha Omega(x,y)]>=0.

Also z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, so it lies to the right of that
quadratic's vertex. Therefore G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y) for all
x,y>=0. Monotonicity of G in its second variable and induction imply

                         B_n<=lambda P_n.                 (18)

Put rho=log_2 lambda>1, D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 and ell(z)=log D(exp z).
For t=exp z, differentiation yields

  ell''(z)=rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/D(t)^2>0.

The bracket vanishes at0 and has positive derivative for t>0. Convexity
of ell therefore gives D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=2. For x,y>0 let
Z=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho. Substitution gives
(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy and hence Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0. Also Z>=x+y, by integrating
the increasing derivative of the positive power. It follows that
Omega(x,y)<=Z; zero arguments follow directly. Induction in the P recurrence
now gives P_n<=(n+1)^rho, and therefore

                     B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1).              (19)

Balanced joins in fact attain P_(2^m-1)=lambda^m, although that equality
is not needed for this upper comparison.

For the ACTUAL mass(13), A_mass tends to1, c^m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m tends
to exp(1), and (2/lambda)^m tends to0. Hence S/lambda^m tends to exp(1).
Combine(17),(19) to obtain both the finite inequality and the limsup in(4).
The strict comparison lambda/[4 exp(1)]<1/4 follows from
lambda<5/2<exp(1), the latter by the exponential series. No decimal limit
for the benchmark was assumed or evaluated by the experiment.

## 7. What the bounded experiment changed

The CLOSED ledger research/max3_c60_trajectory_budget.json records78 full
state-schedule attempts and0.8192669660056708 measured seconds, including
diagnostic repair and a read-only closeout audit. The remaining18 calls
expired. This is separate from the closed656 conic/PEP calls. No optimizer,
software installation or verifier call was used.

Three exact calibrations checked h=(0),r0=1/2,v0=3/4; h=(2),r0=1,v0=0;
and h=(10/3,50/9),r0=1/2,v0=0. Their exact terminal payoffs are17/32,0,
and518/225. All passed numerically, with scaled cubic residuals below
4e-16. The root formula used was
2sqrt(A/3)*sinh(asinh((3B/(2A))*sqrt(3/A))/3 for r^3+A r=B, A>0.

The first50 actual runs used ten initial recipes at depths4,6,8,10,12:
r0^2 in{1/16,1,m,sqrt(M),M/16,M/4,M,4M} with v0=0, plus
(r0^2,v0)=(1,sqrt(M)) and(sqrt(M),sqrt(M)). They suggested the intermediate
horizontal scale r0^2 proportional to M^(2/3), with many largest observed
payoffs occurring without a sign change. The next25 runs used
r0^2=k M^(2/3),v0=0, k in{1/16,1/4,1/2,1,2}, at the same five depths.

For the now-uniformly-excluded k=1 and2 recipes, the numerical M*payoffs
were respectively

  m4:  0.0746391227, 0.1281488193;
  m6:  0.0687903731, 0.1259430663;
  m8:  0.0684937234, 0.1263322527;
  m10: 0.0689648481, 0.1268277425;
  m12: 0.0694885381, 0.1272377691.

Those numbers motivated the proof; none is a premise. No exact B_n was
numerically evaluated or inferred from M. The proof instead uses(19).

Two depth10 runs completed with payoff underflow, and two depth12 runs
stopped at cubic RHS/root underflow; those are not exact zero trajectories.
One further payoff was subnormal. Raw states and original statuses remain
public. An underflow-sensitive turn diagnostic originally multiplied two
tiny r-values; saved-state companions correct it using separate sign tests,
without replaying any trajectory. The repair time is included in the ledger.
No original raw file was overwritten. All raw hashes were checked at closeout.

## 8. Every trajectory with just one turn is also uniformly excluded

This strengthening imposes NO initial-state bound(1). Suppose the actual
parabolic trajectory on H_m has exactly one horizontal sign change.
If it ever hits r=0 then all later values vanish, so only nonzero turns
need be considered. Reflect horizontally so its initial sign is positive.

The normal variable u_i=v_i+r_i^2 is nondecreasing for this entire physical
word, because(7) gives the exact identity

  u_(i+1)-u_i=(h_i-1)r_i^2+r_(i+1)^2>=0.                  (21)

Every h_i>1 here. Before the first turn, any previous step h_t preserved
positivity, so h_t<1+u_t. A later step h_i no larger than that previous
h_t then satisfies h_i<=h_t<1+u_t<=1+u_i and CANNOT turn. Consequently
the first turn occurs at a NEW RECORD step, necessarily the first occurrence
of some level j. In the actual recursive word that step is b_j=1+a c^j,
its prefix is exactly H_j with mass P=S_j, and its suffix mass is

  T=S_m-S_j-b_j>=S_(m-1).                                 (22)

The last inequality follows because S_j+b_j is increasing in j and the
top-level decomposition is S_m=2S_(m-1)+b_(m-1).

Let r>0,u be the state just before this first turn. The positive prefix
has decreasing r-values. Therefore its initial horizontal magnitude R
and the new magnitude R_after obey

  R=r(1+u)+sum_(t<i)h_t r_t>=r(P+1+u),
  R_after=r(h_i-1-u).

The numerator is positive precisely because this step turns. Hence

  R_after/R <=(h_i-1-u)/(P+1+u)
             <=(h_i-1)/(P+1)<c-2.                         (23)

The strict final inequality is(14)--(15), with the actual H_j prefix.
There is no restart of v: the tail starts at the ACTUAL state produced
by the turning step. By hypothesis there is no further turn, so Section5
applies to this suffix with its true initial distance R_after and mass T.
The function and its minimum0 remain identical. Thus

  2F_par(X_n)/R^2
    <=E_par(T)(R_after/R)^2
    <(c-2)^2 E_par(S_(m-1)).                               (24)

For monotonicity used here, logarithmic differentiation gives
E_par'(T)/E_par(T)=4/(2T+3)-2/(T+1)-2/(2T+1)<0; the first term is already
strictly smaller than2/(T+1). The suffix mass in(22) is at least1/2.

Since S_(m-1)/lambda^(m-1)->exp(1), the exact benchmark bound(19) gives

  limsup B_(2^m-1) sup_(all exactly-one-turn initial states) P_par
      <=lambda^2(lambda-2)^2/[4 exp(1)]
       =1/[4 exp(1)]<1/4.                                 (25)

The equality uses lambda(lambda-2)=1. Combining with the zero-turn estimate
(4), every initial state whose actual trajectory has at most one turn has
uniform benchmark limsup at most lambda/[4 exp(1)]<1/4. No initial norm,
vertical-state, amplitude, or smallness restriction was imposed on the
one-turn class. This is a proof about the full actual trajectory and its
retained normal state, not about a constant-gradient single-cut ansatz.

## 9. Remaining interface

Every parabolic start whose trajectory stays on one horizontal side is now
below even the global Huber lower witness by(17). Thus a useful parabolic
mechanism on this ruler must actually TURN. Condition(1) also quantifies
which initial horizontal scales cannot turn. The turning domain
0<R<S sqrt(c-2), with arbitrary coupled r0,v0, is not excluded here.

The k=1/4 finite starts had one turn and small observed M*payoff. Section8
now excludes the ENTIRE one-turn class, independently of those samples.
The k=1/16 starts had several turns; two or more turns remain open. A next
proof must retain how every turn transports BOTH the remaining horizontal
magnitude and the accumulated v into the actual suffix, with its unchanged
physical parameters. After the first turn, a later turn need not be a new
global record; applying the first-record argument afresh would be invalid.
Resetting v or treating the two H_j copies independently is also invalid.

This is a concrete uniform regime result with exact nonlinear block losses,
not a solution of the full parabolic family, the critical diagnostic, or the
original all-schedule exact-profile goal. All new statements remain drafts
until separately authorized independent verification.

# C64 extension: arbitrarily many turns below the first turning threshold

Status: UNVERIFIED. The public combined package appends the COMPLETE C63
proof, including the global function, every interpolation pair, the true
distance, the one-sign block estimates and the exact benchmark comparison.
These are reproduced internal arguments, not unverified predecessor facts.
The only accepted mathematical input is b98bb807d514e80d for its benchmark
definitions. No numerical evaluation or verification is used here.

## Statement

Use exactly the physical H_m, c,a,S_j,M_j and parabolic trajectory from the
attached complete proof, with m>=128. Put d_j=a c^j and u_i=v_i+r_i^2.
Suppose there is a first nonzero horizontal sign change, occurring at level j,
and suppose

                            u_n<=d_j.                      (A)

The first-turn level j is allowed to depend arbitrarily on m. There is no
initial-state bound, fixed turn count or ambient approximation. If absorption
at zero occurs at any time then the final payoff is zero and the bounds below
hold immediately. Otherwise there are exactly2^(m-j)-1 turns.

For j>=1 set

  k=m-j, N=2^k, L_j=d_(j-1)=d_j/c,
  C_j=d_j/(M_j+L_j), beta_j=c C_j.

Then the normalized actual payoff satisfies

  P_par<=E_par(S_j) beta_j^[2(N-1)] c^(-2k),
  B_(2^m-1) P_par<729/1600<1/2.                            (B)

For j=0 it satisfies

  P_par<=(1-1/m)^[2(2^m-1)] c^(-2m),
  B_(2^m-1)P_par<=lambda^(1-m)<1/2.                        (C)

Thus every trajectory satisfying(A) is uniformly below HALF the exact
benchmark for all m>=128, even when its number of turns grows with m.
Together with C63's complete one-turn estimate in the attached proof, the
remaining parabolic case must have AT LEAST TWO turns and u_n>d_j for its
first-turn level j. This does not settle that remaining case or unrestricted W.

## 1. The lower normal threshold persists after the first turn

The exact identity

  u_(i+1)-u_i=(h_i-1)r_i^2+r_(i+1)^2>=0                    (D)

holds because every physical step is greater than1. A sign change occurs
exactly when u_i<d_level(i), since the cubic's right side is r_i(u_i-d_level(i)).
As proved directly in the attached C63 argument, the first turn must occur
at a new record step, hence at the first occurrence of level j. Its preceding
word is exactly H_j, with no earlier turn.

For j>=1, the earlier first occurrence of b_(j-1)=1+d_(j-1) did NOT turn
or annihilate. At its input this implies u>d_(j-1). Monotonicity(D) gives
u>L_j at the first turn and at every later sample. For j=0 simply use u>=0.
Hypothesis(A) and(D) simultaneously give u<=d_j at every sample.

Consequently, after the first turn every lower-level step in H_j preserves
the sign, since its d_level<=L_j<u. Every intervening step of level at
least j reverses the sign, except that an equality can cause absorption,
which has already been separated as zero payoff. The same u and v are
inherited across all copies.

## 2. All complete blocks and all coarse bridges remain in order

The actual word H_m partitions into N=2^(m-j) copies of H_j, interleaved
with N-1 bridges. The excess of the bridge after block ell,0<=ell<N-1, is

                 d_j c^(nu_2(ell+1)),                     (E)

where nu_2 is binary valuation. No physical step is aggregated or replaced
in the actual dynamics; this partition is only for applying proved block
inequalities. The first H_j has one sign by the definition of the first turn.
Every later H_j has one sign by Section1. All finite pair inequalities are
already supplied by the one fixed function in the attached proof.

Let R_ell be the horizontal magnitude at the entry to an H_j block. Just
before its following bridge, let the actual gradient magnitude be |r| and
normal state u. The one-sign block telescope gives

  R_ell>=|r|(M_j+u).

The bridge is a turn, so its resulting horizontal magnitude is
|r|(d_j c^s-u), where s=nu_2(ell+1). Since u>=L_j for j>=1,

  R_(ell+1)/R_ell
    <=(d_j c^s-u)/(M_j+u)
    <=d_j c^s/(M_j+L_j)=C_j c^s.                         (F)

The first inequality also holds for the first bridge: its left H_j was
one-sign, and u>=L_j holds at the BRIDGE, regardless of any smaller u at
the original starting point. Thus no extra initial-band hypothesis was used.

The binary counts give

  sum_(ell=0)^(N-2)nu_2(ell+1)=N-1-k.

One direct proof counts multiples of2^s among1,...,2^k-1 and sums
sum_(s=1)^(k-1)(2^(k-s)-1)=2^k-1-k. Multiplying(F) therefore yields

  R_(N-1)/R_0<=C_j^(N-1)c^(N-1-k)
              =beta_j^(N-1)c^(-k).                       (G)

The final full H_j is one-sign and has its ACTUAL inherited vertical state.
Its true initial distance is R_(N-1), because the closest minimizer of the
fixed parabolic function has the same vertical coordinate. Apply the proved
E_par(S_j) suffix bound, and use(G), to obtain the first inequality in(B).

## 3. Explicit constants and the exact finite benchmark

From the exact mass formula in the attached proof,
d_j/M_j<c-2. Thus

  beta_j=c[d_j/M_j]/[1+(d_j/M_j)/c]
           <c^2(c-2)/(2c-2).                             (H)

For m>=128 one has c<73/30. To check this without decimal input, use
sqrt2<99/70 because9801>9800, and hence
c<(169/70)(129/128)=21801/8960<73/30.
The positive function c^2(c-2)/(2c-2) is increasing for c>2, since both
c^2 and(c-2)/(2(c-1)) are increasing and positive. Consequently

  beta_j<69277/77400<9/10.                                (I)

Also a>2/5: from1-1/m>=127/128 and c<73/30,
a>3810/9344>2/5. Because c-2<1/2, A_mass=a/(c-2)>4/5.
For j>=1, c>12/5 and the exact mass formula give

  S_j>(4/5)c^j-1>(23/60)c^j>c^j/3.                        (J)

The second inequality uses1<(5/12)c^j. The elementary one-sign objective
bound also gives E_par(S)<=3/(8S): bound w+(3/2)q by(3/2)(w+q) in its
proof and maximize t/(S+t)^2<=1/(4S). Thus(J) implies
E_par(S_j)<9/(8c^j).

The attached benchmark proof establishes B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1) from
the exact finite recurrence, without an assumed asymptotic constant.
Using k=m-j>=1, N-1>=1 and(I), we obtain

  B_(2^m-1)P_par
    <(9/8)(lambda/c)^m [lambda c^(-k)] beta_j^[2(N-1)]
    <(9/8)(1/2)(81/100)=729/1600.

Here(lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m)<=1/2 by the binomial theorem, and
lambda c^(-k)<1 for k>=1. This proves the stated finite uniform gap.

## 4. The smallest first-turn level

If j=0 then(A) says u_n<=a. By monotonicity u_i<=a at every query. Every
step has excess d_level>=a; all steps turn unless there is absorption.
Writing x_i=r_i(1+u_i), the exact horizontal update gives

  |x_(i+1)|/|x_i|=(d_level-u_i)/(1+u_i)<=d_level.

There are2^m-1 steps and the sum of their levels is2^m-1-m by the same
binary count. Since a c=1-1/m,

  |x_n|/|x_0|<=a^(2^m-1)c^(2^m-1-m)
                =(1-1/m)^(2^m-1)c^(-m).

At any parabolic state, 2F_par(X)<=x^2, where x is the horizontal
coordinate: by direct subtraction the difference is
r^2[u+u^2-r^2/2]>=0 because u>=r^2. Hence its normalized payoff is at
most the squared displayed ratio. The exact benchmark bound then gives
B P<=lambda^(m+1)c^(-2m)<=lambda^(1-m), proving(C).

## 5. The actual remaining charge, with exact identities

This result allows any number of turns but assumes the terminal normal state
has not passed the first turning excess. It cannot be restarted for free
when u crosses d_j: the remaining word is then only a partial recursive block,
and its first incoming state already contains the prior turn history.

Two exact identities preserve that history for a possible future charge.
Let x_i=r_i(1+u_i), q_i=r_i^2 and define

  Energy_i=x_i^2+v_i^2-v_0^2, Energy_0=R_0^2.

The two-coordinate recursions x_plus=x-h r, v_plus=v+h r^2 give

  Energy_(i+1)-Energy_i=h_i(h_i-2)q_i(1+q_i),
  u_(i+1)-u_i=(h_i-1)q_i+q_(i+1).                         (K)

For the first identity expand both squares and substitute
x_i r_i=q_i(1+v_i+q_i); the v terms cancel. Also Energy_i>=x_i^2 because
v_i>=v_0. This is an AUXILIARY identity; it does not assert that the
rescaled two-dimensional field is a Euclidean gradient, or that Energy
is monotone at steps above2. Its initial value is the TRUE initial squared
distance, so the free vertical starting coordinate is not charged as norm.

A useful next bound must combine the positive large-step terms in(K) with
the normal-level crossing they cause, retaining the partially completed
word. Merely declaring those terms nonpositive, resetting v, or multiplying
full-block bounds across an unproved cut would be invalid. No such bound
is yet established here.


# Complete internal preliminary proof (C63)

# C63: a shared-state block bound closes the large horizontal parabolic regime

Status: complete UNVERIFIED analytic candidate following the bounded direct
C60 experiment. No new fact_submit. Accepted b98bb807d514e80d is used for
the exact benchmark definitions; every further estimate below is derived
internally. C60's unverified formulas are reproduced and proved here.

The result is uniform over an explicit domain of initial states and, in
Section8, over EVERY trajectory with at most one horizontal sign change.
It is NOT an upper bound on every parabolic start or on unrestricted W_n(h).

## 1. Result, with the actual physical parameters

For m>=5 put

  lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
  n=2^m-1, S=sum H_m, M=1+S.

All these parameters remain fixed throughout the complete word H_m.
Consider the ONE function and initial state defined in Section2. Its
parameters are r_0!=0,v_0>=0. Let

  R=|r_0|(1+v_0+r_0^2),
  E_par(S)=(2S+3)^2/[8(S+1)^2(2S+1)].

If

                     R >= S sqrt(c-2),                     (1)

then every r_i has the same strict sign as r_0, and its actual normalized
last-iterate objective satisfies

  P_par(H_m;r_0,v_0)<=E_par(S)<1/(2S+1).                    (2)

In particular(1) holds uniformly for ALL initial states

                     r_0^2>=M^(2/3), v_0>=0.               (3)

For the EXACT Zhang--Jiang benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n from the accepted fact,

  B_(2^m-1) sup_(states satisfying(1)) P_par
       <=lambda^(m+1) E_par(S),
  limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup_(states satisfying(1)) P_par
       <=lambda/[4 exp(1)]<1/4.                            (4)

No asymptotic equality for B_n is assumed. Section6 proves the scalar upper
envelope needed in(4) directly from its exact finite recurrence.

The payoff bound in(2) also holds for ANY finite nonnegative schedule of
total mass S>=1/2 whenever its whole parabolic trajectory keeps one sign
(allowing absorption at zero). Thus the candidate bound is not inferred
from the finite sample portfolio. What is particular to the ruler is the
proved sufficient initial-state condition(1).

## 2. One global legal function, every pair, and true distance

In R2 define

  K_par={(t,s):s>=t^2/sqrt(2)},
  F_par(X)=max_(g in K_par)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2).

The maximizer is the unique Euclidean projection onto this nonempty closed
convex set: complete the square, use coercivity for existence and strict
convexity for uniqueness. The normal inequalities at two projection points
give firm nonexpansiveness. Comparing the defining maxima shows that F_par
is differentiable with gradient equal to the projection; it is globally
convex and1-smooth. In detail, for the projection p(X),
||p(X)-p(Y)||^2<=<p(X)-p(Y),X-Y>, and
0<=F_par(Y)-F_par(X)-<p(X),Y-X>
 <=<p(Y)-p(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.

For r real and v>=0 put

  u=v+r^2,
  X(r,v)=(r(1+v+r^2),-v/sqrt(2)),
  G(r)=(r,r^2/sqrt(2)).

One has X-G=u(r,-1/sqrt(2)). For any(t,s) in K_par,
<X-G,(t,s)-G><=-(u/2)(t-r)^2<=0. Thus G(r) is the actual gradient
at X(r,v), and substitution gives

  F_par(X(r,v))=r^2(1+v)/2+3r^4/4.                          (5)

For EVERY ordered pair of such samples the complete interpolation residual is

  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2
      =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>
      =-(v_j+r_j^2)(r_i-r_j)^2/2<=0.                       (6)

The identity follows by writing f_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2 and expanding.
There is no adjacent-only interpolation condition in this construction.

The minimizer set of F_par is exactly{(0,y):y<=0}. Such a point gives a
nonpositive inner product with every feasible gradient, and0 is feasible.
A point with positive vertical coordinate has a positive value by testing
(0,s) with small positive s. A point with nonzero horizontal coordinate
has a positive value by testing(t,t^2/sqrt(2)) with small t of the same
horizontal sign; the linear term dominates the quadratic and quartic terms.
Hence the closest minimizer to X(r_0,v_0) is(0,-v_0/sqrt(2)), and the
true squared distance is R^2=r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2.

For every physical step h>=0 actual GD is described by

  v_plus=v+h r^2,
  r_plus^3+(1+v_plus)r_plus=r(1+v+r^2-h).                  (7)

The cubic's derivative is1+v_plus+3r_plus^2>=1, so it has exactly one real
solution. The horizontal and vertical coordinates in(7) are exactly those
of X-hG. This proves actual GD for the same global F_par at every step.
The normalized payoff is

  P_par=[r_n^2(1+v_n)+(3/2)r_n^4]/R^2.                    (8)

Reflection of the horizontal coordinate sends r to-r and leaves all values
and v unchanged. Thus it suffices to prove the remaining statements with
r_0>0. If r ever becomes zero it remains zero by(7) and the payoff is zero.

## 3. Quantitative block map with one shared vertical state

First suppose a consecutive actual block of total mass T has positive
r-values throughout. At one step, the sign is retained exactly when
h<1+v+r^2. The increasing cubic in(7), evaluated at the OLD positive r,
exceeds its right side by h r(1+r^2)>=0. Consequently

                       0<r_plus<=r.                       (9)

Write the entry state(r_A,v_A), exit state(r_B,v_B), and horizontal
coordinate x=r(1+v+r^2). Telescoping the two ACTUAL recursions gives

  Delta_x=x_A-x_B=sum_(block)h_i r_i,
  Delta_v=v_B-v_A=sum_(block)h_i r_i^2.

Because r_B<=r_i<=r_A, the following complete finite bounds hold:

  T r_B<=Delta_x<=T r_A,
  r_B Delta_x<=Delta_v<=r_A Delta_x,
  T r_B^2<=Delta_v<=T r_A^2,
  Delta_x^2<=T Delta_v.                                   (10)

The final inequality is weighted Cauchy--Schwarz; at T=0 both sides vanish.
In particular the nonlinear block map obeys

  r_B[1+v_B+r_B^2+T]<=r_A(1+v_A+r_A^2).                   (11)

These estimates compose along H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), where b_j=1+a c^j,
by using the ACTUAL exit(r_B,v_B) of the left block as the bridge input,
then the bridge's exit as the right block input. The right copy's v is
never restarted or frozen. Equations(10)--(11) therefore control full
nonlinear blocks with all finite masses and all inherited v explicitly
present; no limiting differential equation is used.

## 4. The first possible turn has a quantitative initial-radius cost

Consider a first step whose output r is nonpositive. All preceding states
were positive and decreasing by(9). Let P be the total earlier step mass,
h this step, r its positive input, v its input vertical state, q=r^2,
and u=v+q. Equation(7) forces h>=1+u. Also the prefix bounds(10) give

  v-v_0>=P q,  x_0-x<=sqrt(P(v-v_0)),  x=r(1+u).

Therefore v>=Pq, u<=h-1, and

  x_0<=h sqrt(q)+sqrt(Pv).

For fixed u with v=u-q, the condition v>=Pq says 0<=q<=u/(P+1).
On this interval h sqrt(q)+sqrt(P(u-q)) is nondecreasing in q. For P>0
this follows by differentiation, because h>=1 and u-q>=Pq imply
h^2(u-q)>=Pq. For P=0 it follows directly. Its maximum is therefore
(P+h)sqrt(u/(P+1)). We have proved the necessary condition for a first
turn OR a first annihilation:

  x_0<=(P+h) sqrt[(h-1)/(P+1)].                             (12)

This estimate retains all previous gradient energy in v-v_0; it does not
replace the old trajectory by an independent block instance.

For the actual ruler let S_j=sum H_j and M_j=1+S_j, retaining the SAME
c,a from the enclosing horizon m. Directly from the concatenation,

  M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j,
  M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j, A_mass=a/(c-2).             (13)

Here 0<a<c-2. Indeed, writing epsilon=1/m and using lambda^2=2lambda+1,

  c(c-2)-(1-epsilon)
    =epsilon(2lambda+3)+lambda^2 epsilon^2>0.

Thus0<A_mass<1, and for every level j

                      a c^j<(c-2) M_j.                    (14)

Before any level-j step the elapsed prefix mass P is at least S_j: the
first occurrence of that level is immediately after its left H_j, and
every later occurrence is later in the positive word. Since h-1=a c^j,

                (h-1)/(P+1)<c-2,  P+h<=S.                 (15)

Combining(12)--(15) makes x_0<S sqrt(c-2) necessary for any first turn or
annihilation. This contradicts(1), proving strict sign preservation for
the ENTIRE actual word, at every depth and every queried sample.

For m>=5, c-2<1: lambda<5/2 and1+1/m<=6/5 give c<3. If(3) holds,
R=|r_0|(1+v_0+r_0^2)>=|r_0|^3>=M>S sqrt(c-2). This proves the claimed
uniform inclusion without depending on any observed numerical state.

## 5. Exact terminal payoff bound for every one-sign trajectory

Apply(10)--(11) to the entire one-sign run, whose total mass is S>=1/2.
Let q=r_n^2 and w=1+v_n. The actual initial horizontal coordinate obeys

  R>=r_n(S+w+q),  w>=1+S q.

If r_n=0 the desired bound holds because the numerator vanishes. Otherwise
(8) gives

  P_par<=(w+(3/2)q)/(S+w+q)^2.                             (16)

Set t=w+q. The constraint on w gives t>=1+(S+1)q, so q<= (t-1)/(S+1).
Consequently the right side of(16) is at most

              [(2S+3)t-1]/[2(S+1)(S+t)^2], t>=1.

Its derivative has the sign of(2S+3)(S-t)+2. At S>=1/2 the maximizing
point t=S+2/(2S+3) lies in the stated domain. Substitution gives exactly

                 E_par(S)=(2S+3)^2/[8(S+1)^2(2S+1)].       (17)

Moreover E_par(S)<1/(2S+1), because
8(S+1)^2-(2S+3)^2=4S^2+4S-1>0 at S>=1/2. Finally
S E_par(S) tends to1/4. This proves(2) and a uniform bound on every
one-sign initial state, not only the sufficient domain(1).

## 6. Exact finite benchmark comparison

From accepted b98bb807d514e80d define P_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1.
Their recurrences, after substituting those shifts, are P_0=B_0=1 and

  P_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(P_k,P_l),
  B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(P_k,B_l),
  Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
  G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

Here is a full proof of the needed comparator, so the earlier unverified
C54 calculation is not used as a premise. Set alpha=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda)
and Llin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Both coefficients of Llin exceed1, so
Llin>=x+y. Direct expansion, using lambda^2=2lambda+1, gives

  alpha^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]
                   =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda y)^2>=0.

Thus Llin>=Omega(x,y), the positive root of z^2-(x+y)z-xy=0. At
z=lambda Omega(x,y), the polynomial with larger root G(x,lambda y) obeys

  z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2
      =x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha Omega(x,y)]>=0.

Also z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, so it lies to the right of that
quadratic's vertex. Therefore G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y) for all
x,y>=0. Monotonicity of G in its second variable and induction imply

                         B_n<=lambda P_n.                 (18)

Put rho=log_2 lambda>1, D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 and ell(z)=log D(exp z).
For t=exp z, differentiation yields

  ell''(z)=rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/D(t)^2>0.

The bracket vanishes at0 and has positive derivative for t>0. Convexity
of ell therefore gives D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=2. For x,y>0 let
Z=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho. Substitution gives
(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy and hence Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0. Also Z>=x+y, by integrating
the increasing derivative of the positive power. It follows that
Omega(x,y)<=Z; zero arguments follow directly. Induction in the P recurrence
now gives P_n<=(n+1)^rho, and therefore

                     B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1).              (19)

Balanced joins in fact attain P_(2^m-1)=lambda^m, although that equality
is not needed for this upper comparison.

For the ACTUAL mass(13), A_mass tends to1, c^m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m tends
to exp(1), and (2/lambda)^m tends to0. Hence S/lambda^m tends to exp(1).
Combine(17),(19) to obtain both the finite inequality and the limsup in(4).
The strict comparison lambda/[4 exp(1)]<1/4 follows from
lambda<5/2<exp(1), the latter by the exponential series. No decimal limit
for the benchmark was assumed or evaluated by the experiment.

## 7. What the bounded experiment changed

The CLOSED ledger research/max3_c60_trajectory_budget.json records78 full
state-schedule attempts and0.8192669660056708 measured seconds, including
diagnostic repair and a read-only closeout audit. The remaining18 calls
expired. This is separate from the closed656 conic/PEP calls. No optimizer,
software installation or verifier call was used.

Three exact calibrations checked h=(0),r0=1/2,v0=3/4; h=(2),r0=1,v0=0;
and h=(10/3,50/9),r0=1/2,v0=0. Their exact terminal payoffs are17/32,0,
and518/225. All passed numerically, with scaled cubic residuals below
4e-16. The root formula used was
2sqrt(A/3)*sinh(asinh((3B/(2A))*sqrt(3/A))/3 for r^3+A r=B, A>0.

The first50 actual runs used ten initial recipes at depths4,6,8,10,12:
r0^2 in{1/16,1,m,sqrt(M),M/16,M/4,M,4M} with v0=0, plus
(r0^2,v0)=(1,sqrt(M)) and(sqrt(M),sqrt(M)). They suggested the intermediate
horizontal scale r0^2 proportional to M^(2/3), with many largest observed
payoffs occurring without a sign change. The next25 runs used
r0^2=k M^(2/3),v0=0, k in{1/16,1/4,1/2,1,2}, at the same five depths.

For the now-uniformly-excluded k=1 and2 recipes, the numerical M*payoffs
were respectively

  m4:  0.0746391227, 0.1281488193;
  m6:  0.0687903731, 0.1259430663;
  m8:  0.0684937234, 0.1263322527;
  m10: 0.0689648481, 0.1268277425;
  m12: 0.0694885381, 0.1272377691.

Those numbers motivated the proof; none is a premise. No exact B_n was
numerically evaluated or inferred from M. The proof instead uses(19).

Two depth10 runs completed with payoff underflow, and two depth12 runs
stopped at cubic RHS/root underflow; those are not exact zero trajectories.
One further payoff was subnormal. Raw states and original statuses remain
public. An underflow-sensitive turn diagnostic originally multiplied two
tiny r-values; saved-state companions correct it using separate sign tests,
without replaying any trajectory. The repair time is included in the ledger.
No original raw file was overwritten. All raw hashes were checked at closeout.

## 8. Every trajectory with just one turn is also uniformly excluded

This strengthening imposes NO initial-state bound(1). Suppose the actual
parabolic trajectory on H_m has exactly one horizontal sign change.
If it ever hits r=0 then all later values vanish, so only nonzero turns
need be considered. Reflect horizontally so its initial sign is positive.

The normal variable u_i=v_i+r_i^2 is nondecreasing for this entire physical
word, because(7) gives the exact identity

  u_(i+1)-u_i=(h_i-1)r_i^2+r_(i+1)^2>=0.                  (21)

Every h_i>1 here. Before the first turn, any previous step h_t preserved
positivity, so h_t<1+u_t. A later step h_i no larger than that previous
h_t then satisfies h_i<=h_t<1+u_t<=1+u_i and CANNOT turn. Consequently
the first turn occurs at a NEW RECORD step, necessarily the first occurrence
of some level j. In the actual recursive word that step is b_j=1+a c^j,
its prefix is exactly H_j with mass P=S_j, and its suffix mass is

  T=S_m-S_j-b_j>=S_(m-1).                                 (22)

The last inequality follows because S_j+b_j is increasing in j and the
top-level decomposition is S_m=2S_(m-1)+b_(m-1).

Let r>0,u be the state just before this first turn. The positive prefix
has decreasing r-values. Therefore its initial horizontal magnitude R
and the new magnitude R_after obey

  R=r(1+u)+sum_(t<i)h_t r_t>=r(P+1+u),
  R_after=r(h_i-1-u).

The numerator is positive precisely because this step turns. Hence

  R_after/R <=(h_i-1-u)/(P+1+u)
             <=(h_i-1)/(P+1)<c-2.                         (23)

The strict final inequality is(14)--(15), with the actual H_j prefix.
There is no restart of v: the tail starts at the ACTUAL state produced
by the turning step. By hypothesis there is no further turn, so Section5
applies to this suffix with its true initial distance R_after and mass T.
The function and its minimum0 remain identical. Thus

  2F_par(X_n)/R^2
    <=E_par(T)(R_after/R)^2
    <(c-2)^2 E_par(S_(m-1)).                               (24)

For monotonicity used here, logarithmic differentiation gives
E_par'(T)/E_par(T)=4/(2T+3)-2/(T+1)-2/(2T+1)<0; the first term is already
strictly smaller than2/(T+1). The suffix mass in(22) is at least1/2.

Since S_(m-1)/lambda^(m-1)->exp(1), the exact benchmark bound(19) gives

  limsup B_(2^m-1) sup_(all exactly-one-turn initial states) P_par
      <=lambda^2(lambda-2)^2/[4 exp(1)]
       =1/[4 exp(1)]<1/4.                                 (25)

The equality uses lambda(lambda-2)=1. Combining with the zero-turn estimate
(4), every initial state whose actual trajectory has at most one turn has
uniform benchmark limsup at most lambda/[4 exp(1)]<1/4. No initial norm,
vertical-state, amplitude, or smallness restriction was imposed on the
one-turn class. This is a proof about the full actual trajectory and its
retained normal state, not about a constant-gradient single-cut ansatz.

## 9. Remaining interface

Every parabolic start whose trajectory stays on one horizontal side is now
below even the global Huber lower witness by(17). Thus a useful parabolic
mechanism on this ruler must actually TURN. Condition(1) also quantifies
which initial horizontal scales cannot turn. The turning domain
0<R<S sqrt(c-2), with arbitrary coupled r0,v0, is not excluded here.

The k=1/4 finite starts had one turn and small observed M*payoff. Section8
now excludes the ENTIRE one-turn class, independently of those samples.
The k=1/16 starts had several turns; two or more turns remain open. A next
proof must retain how every turn transports BOTH the remaining horizontal
magnitude and the accumulated v into the actual suffix, with its unchanged
physical parameters. After the first turn, a later turn need not be a new
global record; applying the first-record argument afresh would be invalid.
Resetting v or treating the two H_j copies independently is also invalid.

This is a concrete uniform regime result with exact nonlinear block losses,
not a solution of the full parabolic family, the critical diagnostic, or the
original all-schedule exact-profile goal. All new statements remain drafts
until separately authorized independent verification.
