# C83: exact all-curvature repair at a cut

Status: complete joint UNVERIFIED analytical candidate. No new schedule,
optimizer/model evaluation, numerical replay, installation or verifier
call. This strengthens the shared unit-curvature cut/quad idea by using
every curvature kappa in(0,1], retaining its strictly negative original
interpolation residuals, and deriving the exact maximal repair weight.
The unit-curvature shared draft is motivation only; everything needed
is rederived here. Accepted facts used:1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation)
and04219554e41d9750 Section10 (full primitive certificate identity).

## 1. Data and statement of the necessary condition

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be any finite nonnegative word, let
s=sum h_i,A=1+s, and select an index j with h_j>1. Define

 P=sum_(i<j)h_i, R=sum_(i>j)h_i, H=P+h_j=s-R,
 eta=(h_j-1)/(1+R)>0, C0=eta(1+eta),
 V0=(1+P)^2-A^2 eta^2.

For kappa in(0,1] let

 p_0=1, p_i=product_(t<i)(1-kappa h_t) for0<=i<=j+1,
 E=1+kappa R, q=kappa p_(j+1)/E,
 t_kappa=(1-kappa)/(2kappa).

If R>0 put

 beta=max{0, max_(0<=i<=j)
       [q(kappa p_i-q)-t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2/R]}.

If R=0 put beta=0. Finally define

 V_kappa=
   kappa sum_(i=0)^j h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2
   -H[s+R+1+1/kappa]q^2.                               (1)

Every full primitive certificate for the ORIGINAL word necessarily
satisfies, for every such j and every0<kappa<=1,

             beta V0+C0 V_kappa >=0.                    (2)

For beta=0 the stronger explicit interpretation is V_kappa>=0,
which is equivalent to(2) because C0>0. No other step is assumed
positive, above1, symmetric, ordered or part of a primitive prefix.

## 2. The legal scalar cut coordinate

Use the scalar convex function with derivative clip(x,-eta,1),
minimum0 at0, and initial point1+P. Put
P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and R_i=sum_(t>=i)h_t. Its actual data are

 x_i^0=1+P-P_i,    g_i^0=1,             i<=j,
 x_i^0=-eta(1+R_i), g_i^0=-eta,         i>j,
 f_i^0=x_i^0 g_i^0-(g_i^0)^2/2.

The selected step sends1 to1-h_j=-eta(1+R), and the remaining
updates keep the points on the negative constant-gradient segment,
ending at-eta. All zero steps retain their original sample indices.
This verifies the full GD chronology on one actual function.

For this coordinate the ordered residual is

 Q_ik^0=(g_i^0-g_k^0)(x_k^0-g_k^0).

It is0 inside either block. From the prefix to a suffix target k>j,
it is exactly-C0 R_k. In the opposite direction it is
-(1+eta)(P-P_k)<=0. All optimum comparisons are nonpositive.
The norm-telescoping identity, or direct substitution, gives its
primitive energy V0 exactly as defined above.

## 3. A quadratic prefix and constant-gradient suffix

Define a second set of scalar data, not yet claimed to be legal:

 x_i^1=p_i, g_i^1=kappa p_i, f_i^1=kappa p_i^2/2,       i<=j;
 x_i^1=q(1/kappa+R_i), g_i^1=q,
 f_i^1=q x_i^1-q^2/(2kappa),                           i>j.

The point after the selected step is p_(j+1)=q(1/kappa+R),
so these data follow every original GD update and end at q/kappa.
Include the optimum sample (x_star^1,g_star^1,f_star^1)=(0,0,0).

Write Q_ik^1=f_i^1-f_k^1+g_i^1(x_k^1-x_i^1)
                  +(g_i^1-g_k^1)^2/2.
Direct substitution gives the COMPLETE list of residual types:

 * i,k<=j:
   Q_ik^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1-g_k^1)^2<=0.
 * i,k>j: Q_ik^1=0.
 * i>j,k<=j:
   Q_ik^1=-t_kappa(q-kappa p_k)^2<=0.
 * i<=j,k>j:
   Q_ik^1=R_k q(kappa p_i-q)
                    -t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2.
 * Q_(i,star)^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1)^2<=0 for all i.
 * Q_(star,i)^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1)^2 for i<=j, and
   Q_(star,i)^1=-(R_i+t_kappa)q^2 for i>j.

Thus only the fourth type can be positive. If R_k=0 it is
nonpositive. For R_k>0, its quotient by R_k is

 q(kappa p_i-q)-t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2/R_k,

which is nondecreasing as R_k increases. Since0<R_k<=R,
its largest possible value is at k=j+1, whose R_k equals R.
This proves that beta is the exact largest nonnegative ratio of
a possibly bad residual to its corresponding suffix mass.

In particular beta=0 makes ALL auxiliary data, including optimum
comparisons, legal. If R=0 there are no positive suffix masses,
so the convention beta=0 has exactly this same meaning.

## 4. Orthogonal repair gives an actual globally legal function

If beta>0 choose epsilon=C0/beta and set

 X_i=(x_i^0,sqrt(epsilon)x_i^1),
 G_i=(g_i^0,sqrt(epsilon)g_i^1),
 F_i=f_i^0+epsilon f_i^1,

with the zero optimum sample. GD still uses the same full word.
Every original residual is Q_ik^0+epsilon Q_ik^1. All types except
prefix-to-suffix are sums of nonpositive numbers. For that type
with R_k>0 the sum is at most

       -C0 R_k+epsilon beta R_k=0;

when R_k=0 the auxiliary residual itself is nonpositive. Therefore
all directed comparisons, including the optimum and original terminal
sample, hold. A maximizing i in beta and k=j+1 show that epsilon
is also the largest permitted weight of this fixed auxiliary data
against this fixed base coordinate. No residual was discarded.

By accepted1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraph2, these planar jets have
an everywhere finite convex differentiable1-smooth interpolant with
minimum value0 at0. Concretely, with I={star,0,...,n}, define

 c_i=<G_i,X_i>-F_i,
 J_i(v)=c_i+<X_i,v-G_i>+||v-G_i||^2/2,
 J(v)=max_(i in I) J_i(v),
 F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-J(v)}.

The cited fact proves F(X_i)=F_i and grad F(X_i)=G_i from exactly
the residual convention just verified. Thus these are actual GD data
of one function, not a sum of separately illegal functions.

If beta=0, apply the same accepted interpolation construction directly
to the auxiliary scalar jets, since their full residual list is legal.
No division by beta occurs in this case.

## 5. Exact energy calculation

For the auxiliary suffix, f_i^1-f_n^1=q^2 R_i and
f_n^1=q^2/(2kappa). The elementary finite identity

 2sum_(i>j) h_i R_i = R^2+sum_(i>j)h_i^2

follows by expanding R^2. Thus the combined suffix contribution of
the weighted value differences and gradient diagonals is R(R+1)q^2.
The prefix contribution is

 kappa sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2-H q^2/kappa.

Subtracting the terminal s(s+1)q^2 gives(1), because
s(s+1)-R(R+1)=H(s+R+1).

The primitive energy is linear in all values and Gram entries.
The repaired planar trajectory therefore has energy

          V0+(C0/beta)V_kappa

when beta>0, and the legal auxiliary trajectory has energy V_kappa
when beta=0. Accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10, says an original
full nonnegative flow/PSD certificate forces nonnegative energy on
every actual trajectory. This proves(2), including beta=0.

## 6. The unit-curvature slice and exact low-degree polynomial interface

At kappa=1, q=p_(j+1)/(1+R)=-eta p_j, t_kappa=0, and

 beta=max{0,max_(i<=j)q(p_i-q)}, V_1=1-A^2 q^2.

For the last identity, the prefix sum in(1) telescopes to
1-p_(j+1)^2, using p_(i+1)^2=(1-h_i)^2 p_i^2. Then
(1+R)^2+H(s+R+2)=A^2. This rederives the unit-curvature
repair without treating the shared max2 draft as a correctness premise.

For R>0 the entire curvature interval can be expressed through
finitely many POLYNOMIAL branches. Define

 d_i(kappa)=[E p_i-p_(j+1)]/kappa,                     0<=i<=j,
 T_i(kappa)=2R p_(j+1)d_i-(1-kappa)d_i^2,
 T_max(kappa)=max{0,T_0(kappa),...,T_j(kappa)},

 P_cut(kappa)=E^2 sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2
              -H[1+kappa(s+R+1)]p_(j+1)^2.

The numerator defining d_i vanishes at kappa=0, so d_i is a
polynomial, including at0. It has degree at most j. Consequently
T_i has degree at most2j+1, and P_cut degree at most2j+3.
The exact identities are

 beta=kappa^3 T_max/(2R E^2),
 V_kappa=kappa P_cut/E^2.

Since kappa,R,E are positive, (2) is equivalent to

 kappa^2 V0 T_max(kappa)+2R C0 P_cut(kappa)>=0,
                                             0<kappa<=1. (3)

At each branch where0 or a specific T_i is the maximum, (3) is
a polynomial inequality of degree at most2j+3. For a four-step word,
the two middle cuts j=1,2 therefore require degrees at most5,7.
Their branch selection inequalities are the explicit comparisons
T_i>=0 and T_i>=T_k, with the zero branch selected when all T_i<=0.
This gives a precise exact real-algebraic interface for further work;
no polynomial-root search or numerical interval scan is performed.

## 7. Scope and remaining task

The construction gives legal adversaries whenever(2) fails. Passing
all these tests gives no full certificate; no sufficiency is claimed.
The convex combination mechanism retains every original interpolation
pair and all function-value offsets. A favorable or unfavorable outcome
on any finite list of curvatures would not settle the universal interval.

No new word was selected, tested, rounded, or evaluated in deriving this
family. It neither constructs the missing four-step seed nor excludes
every h with sum>=168/25. The accepted conditional lift remains available
as fact04219554e41d9750; the seed existence problem is still OPEN.

# max3 final round19 handoff: C81--C83 analytical continuation

Original target OPEN. No four-step full-cone seed exists in this output.
No global seed-impossibility theorem is proved. No new fact was submitted
or accepted. The accepted seed-to-asymptotic implication remains precisely
the conditional fact04219554e41d9750.

This completes the second and final round of the eighth max3 launch,
cumulative19 at the explicit TASK.md cap19. TASK.md still says
"No verifier or installation." The latest read master_guidance is
5e2fd47b60222794; its additional allocation concerns max, not max3.
No further round, computation or gate is inferred.

## New complete joint proof drafts

1. C81 / finding618a43a2e5b13506. The already saved record100 word
   h=(8/5,2,471/250,309/250),sum168/25, is rejected by the actual rational
   triangle K=conv{(2,0),(-1,3/5),(-221/559,0)} and
   F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2), starting at(11/5,3/5).
   The five actual gradients are(2,0),(-1,3/5),(1,0),(-221/559,0),
   (-221/559,0). Every original projection normal is proved, and
   V=-(11908*73398)/(625*559^2)<0. This uses an interior-edge query.
   Shared artifact: max3_c81_edge_interior_triangle.md.

2. C82 / finding6d992d34120a0c16. The complete C81 construction is
   reproduced together with an exact elimination of every witness
   parameter. For a,b,c>1,d>=0 it gives a radical/piecewise-quadratic
   necessary test E_min(h)>=0, including zero-height boundary limits
   and unbounded-negative cases. This is no longer a parameter scan.
   Shared artifact: max3_c82_joint_triangle_and_elimination.md.

3. C83 / finding32380fa0da39d710. Every nonnegative word, every selected
   cut h_j>1, and every curvature0<kappa<=1 obey a new necessary
   inequality beta V0+eta(1+eta)V_kappa>=0. It repairs a quadratic
   prefix/constant-gradient suffix with an actual cut coordinate,
   retains all function values and original ordered residuals, and
   derives the exact maximum admissible orthogonal repair weight.
   For n4 and the two middle cuts, the whole curvature continuum is
   represented by polynomial branches of degree at most5 and7.
   Shared artifact: max3_c83_all_curvature_cut_repair.md.

All three are UNVERIFIED, self-contained joint proof candidates using
accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and04219554e41d9750 as indicated. No unverified
shared finding was used as a fact. No outside-paper theorem is imported;
the existing exact Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur source was reread as background.

## Direct screening and the remaining mathematical gap

Plan811c5d663de45479 was screened in proof_attempt06536c7f8be8f39e.
Failure synthesis b8ff515b5f904ad8 records the precise open interface.
The triangle minimization is finished, and the all-curvature repair is
finished, but no proof that their simultaneous necessary conditions
exclude every four-step schedule at or above the threshold was obtained.
Passing these tests is not a full flow/PSD certificate.

The saved record34 is from the kernel-dual model and does not contain
a primal Gram/value transcript. Its PSD multiplier was not promoted to
an actual legal function or treated as proof. A contemplated prefix
lifting argument was found already in max2's unit-curvature repair and
max4's older core-Huber-padding/saturated-prefix-heredity drafts. These
were recalled through shared memory and were not claimed as new results.

The exact next analytical interface is to prove global coverage across
all four schedule variables and all polynomial branches, or produce
the actual full20-multiplier certificate. The original full-space signs,
flow and PSD conditions remain authoritative. The C83 degree bound is
an interface for symbolic work, not authorization for a numerical scan,
root sweep or heavy algebra system.

## Allocation and accounting

Round19 deltas: ZERO optimizer/model evaluations, ZERO distinct proposed
words, ZERO exact numerical enclosure replays, ZERO verifier calls,
ZERO installations or subagents. Only mathematical derivation, retrieval,
text/file operations and artifact-integrity checks were performed.

The existing numerical ledger was read and LEFT UNCHANGED:
status closed_budget_audit_overrun_no_further_discovery;
125 charged entries (99 actual conic solves,1 earlier exact candidate
check,25 retrospectively disclosed manual considerations),120 distinct

# max3: C95 round26 handoff

This is the first of the two newly authorized rounds. Completed baseline25;
this return would complete26; completed ceiling27 and attempted ceiling28
including the historical interruption. No additional allocation is inferred.
The original target remains OPEN. No new accepted fact or verifier call.

The COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED draft is shared as `a966d8ce3986d7b2` and at
`research/max3_c95_weak_background_forward.md`. It contains the exact static
curvature/minimizer proof, finite H6/H7/H8 ray comparison, correct asymmetric
core capture criterion, its priced conditional scalable obstruction, and
the full interpretation of bounded discovery. Normalization finding
`6b4a1c61d19d924a`, scope counterexample `1cf3f3da242a776d`, and scoped
dead end `fa6c9c8ef0983ef9` index the main points. None is a graph fact.

Sixteen charged cases have completed:1 calibration,14 discovery,1 exact
reconstruction. All raw statuses are finite. There are zero timeouts,
stale locks, solver calls, installations, formal submissions, new benchmark
DP evaluations, or C59 replay checks. The persistent ledger reports
0.6265509749991907 seconds used. Remaining allocation is20 total cases,
at most18 discovery and two calibration/reconstruction reserves, and the
remainder of the ORIGINAL30-second cap. The ledger remains open across
round27. Always use the main-owned meter and launcher; do not reset it.

No gain appeared. Twelve discovery cases change queried gates. The largest
such floating payoff is case12 at approximately0.8972369014*K, below the
accepted scalar reference and far below accepted C59 on the same H6.
The complete accepted ray reference equals K for all three finite words,
as proved symbolically in the UNVERIFIED joint draft, without a DP replay
or enumeration of checkpoint sets. No m>=128 result was transplanted.

Case16 reconstructs the EXACT SAME static object as case10 in Q(sqrt(2)).
All physical parameters, points, gradients, values and gate labels are
recorded publicly. It reproduces the whole itinerary; at x60 both x60
and -a*x60 lie in the cores. The final H2 multiplier is exactly
-125/98+(125/147)*sqrt(2), and the exact check returns V<K/100000.
This is a finite exact arithmetic result and a joint proof draft, not a
verifier verdict or an original-target claim.

The important proof correction is that core membership alone is
insufficient for asymmetric radii. The actual two-point condition is
z,-a*z in the closed core intersection, when the total core matrix is I.
Under the explicit weak-background bound A<=2I/hmax and uniform clipped
gradient cap, capture at the final dyadic suffix of fewer than2m^2 steps
forces V/K to zero. This does not prove capture occurs for general models.

Next round: prioritize a static weak-background object that avoids late
two-point capture, or whose total core matrix differs from I, with all
true-distance and global-curvature costs explicit. The best observed
near-parallel model is a lead only. Do not silently spend reserves on a
new parameter scan, or call a lower witness on a bad word a minimax
disproof. Read TASK and latest master_guidance again before acting.

`research/weak_ridge_forward_raw/`.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000735 -->
# C95: weak-background static Huber ridge dynamics

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. Formal verifier allowance is zero.
This work belongs to completed round 26 (baseline 25, ceiling 27).
Accepted mathematical sources: b98bb807d514e80d (single cuts),
1fca3e5dd555cb4a (alternating rays), e9a55f57461f253c (finite original H6).
C93/C94 are motivation only. Every new assertion used here is derived below.

## Static model and exact legality

For positive radii r_- and r_+, let phi(0)=0 and
phi'(s)=max(-r_-,min(s,r_+)). Thus phi=s^2/2 on the core,
phi=r_+ s-r_+^2/2 to the right, and phi=-r_- s-r_-^2/2 to the left.
Let A be a symmetric PSD matrix, u_j unit vectors, and alpha_j>0.
Define one static function

    F(x)=x^T A x/2 + sum_j alpha_j phi_j(u_j^T x),
    C=A+sum_j alpha_j u_j u_j^T,   0<=C<=I.

Every scalar clip is monotone and 1-Lipschitz. For any x,y its difference
is theta_j u_j^T(x-y), with 0<=theta_j<=1. Therefore

    grad F(x)-grad F(y)=(A+sum_j alpha_j theta_j u_j u_j^T)(x-y).

The matrix in parentheses lies between zero and I, so its norm is at
most one. This proves global 1-smoothness; convexity also follows termwise
from monotonicity of each scalar derivative and A>=0. No local Hessian
rescaling of the physical steps is permitted or needed.

Every summand is nonnegative, and phi_j(s)=0 iff s=0. Consequently

    argmin F=ker A intersect intersection_j u_j^perp=ker C,
    min F=0.

The initial distance is the norm of the orthogonal projection onto
(ker C)^perp. When C is positive definite this is exactly ||x_0||;
the one-dimensional calibration also has its unique minimizer at zero.
All matrices below have explicit rational certificates, with no eigenvalue
clipping. A small eigenvalue is not silently removed from the distance.

Useful exact planar directions are

    u_+=(4/5,3/5), u_-=(4/5,-3/5), v=(0,1).

Weights alpha_+=alpha_-=25/32 give total matrix diag(1,9/16).
Adding weight 7/16 on v gives I. With A=mu I and multiplying every
ridge weight by 1-mu, 0<=mu<1, these become respectively
diag(1,mu+(1-mu)9/16) and I. The background floor can be zero or
arbitrarily weak. A fixed gate change changes one rank-one matrix and
can therefore change the principal axes; no independent switching matrix
is supplied to gradient descent.

## Original finite words and references

For m in {6,7,8}, set c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), beta=1-1/m,
a=beta/c, and H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.
All subwords retain the enclosing-m values c,beta,a. Let h=H_m,
n=2^m-1, S=sum h. Physical GD is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F(x_i).
The normalized actual objective is V=2F(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2.
These finite horizons do not test any m>=128 theorem.

Accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives

    K=max(1/(1+2S), max_j (h_j-1)^2/((1+P_j)^2*(1+2T_j))),

where P_j,T_j are the preceding and following step masses.
At m=6 the stronger accepted e9a55f57461f253c gives
W_63(H_6)>56/2711>1/50. It uses its own actual high-dimensional function;
its 4160 checks are not replayed, and this reference is not transplanted
to m=7 or m=8. Saved original benchmark CSV entries may be read but no
benchmark dynamic program is rerun.

Here is a direct sufficient test for the complete accepted ray family to
equal K. For i<j put P=sum_(t<i)h_t and D=sum_(i<t<j)h_t. If

    (h_i-1)(h_j-1)<(1+P)(1+D)                 (R)

for every i<j, no feasible accepted ray selection has two checkpoints.
Indeed its first two checkpoint gradients are
m_0=1/(1+P), m_1=(h_i-1)m_0/(1+D), with old offset P*m_0.
The second-return budget is (h_j-1)m_1-P*m_0<m_0 by (R).
With exactly two checkpoints the old-gradient requirement is at least
m_0, and with more checkpoints the third magnitude would be below m_0.
Both contradict the explicit feasibility conditions of 1fca3e5dd555cb4a.
Zero and one checkpoint produce exactly K. Any numerical check of (R)
will be labeled numerical until an exact symbolic bound is supplied.

## Calibration and accounting

The initial calibration is the accepted one-dimensional central single cut.
If j=2^(m-1)-1, P=T=sum H_(m-1), b=h_j, choose
r_+=1/(1+P), r_-=(b-1)/((1+P)(1+2T)), A=0, alpha=1, x_0=1.
Its exact payoff is (b-1)^2/((1+P)^2(1+2T)). Its gradient is r_+ until
the central cut and -r_- afterward, with a possible boundary label at
the cut itself. This is one charged calibration, not a free preliminary run.

All forward work is standard-library Python, through the main-owned
bounded_ridge_forward_meter.py and run_ridge_with_remaining_budget.py.
The model source is research/weak_background_forward.py. The allocation is
36 total cases, at most 32 discovery, four total calibration/reconstruction
reserves, 30 measured/accounted seconds across both rounds and all scripts.
No case is run while authoring this draft. Source and request snapshots,
hashes, raw iterates/gradients/values, true distance, static parameters,
gate labels at queried and terminal points, and failures will be shared.

No numerical observation is a verified fact, a minimax disproof, or an
asymptotic conclusion. The original project target remains open.

## Exact finite-word ray comparison, with no exponential enumeration

Write r_j=a*c^j and M_j=1+sum H_j. Then

    M_0=1, M_(j+1)=2*M_j+r_j,
    t_j=r_j/M_j, t_0=a, t_(j+1)=c*t_j/(2+t_j).

For m>=6, c>1+sqrt(2) and beta<1 imply a<c-2. Hence t_j increases
and stays below c-2. Also

    beta-(c-2) = 3-(1+sqrt(2))-(2+sqrt(2))/m
       >= (10-7*sqrt(2))/6 >0,

so t_j>=a>(c-2)/c.

For any two chronological indices i<j with levels u,v, define the ratio
R_ij in (R) with the right side as its denominator. If u>=v, the prefix
before i contains H_u and the gap before j contains H_v; thus
R_ij<=t_u*t_v. The largest level m-1 occurs only once, so at least one
of u,v is at most m-2. It follows that

    R_ij<=t_(m-1)*t_(m-2)<c*t_(m-2)^2.

If u<v, both the prefix before i and the gap from i to j contain H_u.
Their total mass is at least sum H_v-(1+r_u), since the prefix before
j contains H_v. Consequently, putting x=1+P and y=1+D,
x,y>=M_u and x+y>=M_v-r_u. From
(x-M_u)(y-M_u)>=0 we obtain

    x*y>=M_u*(M_v-M_u-r_u).

Let d=v-u>=1 and t=t_u. Directly iterating the recurrence for M gives

    (M_v-M_u-r_u)/M_u
      =2^d-1+t*((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1)
      >=c^(d-1)+(c-1)*(2^d-2)/c>=c^(d-1).

The first inequality uses t>=(c-2)/c and the nonnegative coefficient
((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1). Therefore R_ij<=c*t_u^2<=c*t_(m-2)^2.
Equality in the complete maximum occurs at the first level-(m-2) bridge
and the immediately following level-(m-1) bridge: both intervening
prefix/gap masses are sum H_(m-2). Thus

    max_(i<j) R_ij = c*t_(m-2)^2.                         (C95.1)

This proves the full pair test symbolically; it does not rely on sampled
pair scans. Here are short rational upper certificates for the three
authorized horizons. Monotonicity of c*t/(2+t) in both positive variables
allows use of the displayed upper parameters throughout.

* m=6: c<17/6 and a<3/10. Four recurrence steps give
  t_4<83521/144462<7/12. Thus c*t_4^2<833/864<1.
  The intermediate bounds are 17/46,289/654,4913/9582,83521/144462.
* m=7: c<83/30 and a<5/16. Bounds for t_1,...,t_5 are respectively
  3/8,7/16,1/2,83/150,3/5. For the last step,
  6889/11490<3/5; hence c*t_5^2<747/750<1.
* m=8: c<68/25 and a<13/40. The upper recurrence has reciprocal at
  its sixth step equal to
  25/18+(395/234)*(25/34)^6. This is greater than 33/20 because
  (25/34)^3>79/200 and (79/200)^2>611/3950. Therefore t_6<20/33,
  and c*t_6^2<27200/27225<1.

All parameter enclosures follow by squaring positive rational bounds for
sqrt(2). In particular sqrt(2)<10/7, sqrt(2)>29/21 prove the m=6
bounds; sqrt(2)<341/240 and sqrt(2)>7/5 prove m=7;
sqrt(2)<319/225 and sqrt(2)>163/117 prove m=8.
Combining (C95.1) with the accepted ray feasibility conditions proves

    F_ray_(2^m-1)(H_m)=K_(2^m-1)(H_m), m=6,7,8.           (C95.2)

No m>=128 result is used here. This is a new UNVERIFIED finite symbolic
comparison derived jointly, not an accepted graph fact.

For completeness the largest single-cut term is the central cut. For a
fixed level u put p=1+P, q=1+T; both are at least M_u and
p+q=M_m-r_u. The denominator p^2*(2q-1) has no interior minimum on
that interval: its derivative is 2p*(2*(M_m-r_u)-3p-1), with at most
one interior critical point, a maximum. The two endpoint denominators
are ordered by monotonicity of z^2/(2z-1) for z>=1, so the earliest
occurrence of level u is best. Its value is

    t_u^2/[2*M_m-2*M_u-2*r_u-1].

The numerator increases and the positive denominator decreases with u.
Thus the central level m-1 maximizes it. In particular K is exactly
the larger of the global Huber term and the central single-cut term;
no choice of untested checkpoints is hidden in the numerical reference.

## Exact capture criterion for a saturated core curvature budget

Assume C=A+sum_j alpha_j u_j*u_j^T=I, and let D be the closed convex
intersection of all core strips -r_(j,-)<=u_j^T x<=r_(j,+).
At every point in D, grad F(x)=x and 2F(x)=||x||^2.

Fix any k>=1 with k<=m, retaining the same enclosing-m parameters. A
run of H_k starting at z stays in D at every query AND at the terminal
point if and only if both z and -a*z lie in D. In that event its exact
iterate after t steps is

    x_t=(-1)^t*beta^t*c^(-s_2(t))*z, 0<=t<=2^k-1,         (C95.3)

where s_2(t) counts binary ones and s_2(0)=0. In particular

    x_end=-beta^(2^k-1)*c^(-k)*z,
    2F(x_end)=beta^(2*(2^k-1))*c^(-2k)*||z||^2.           (C95.4)

Proof: the level at step t-1 is v_2(t), the exponent of 2 dividing t.
The identity sum_(j=1)^t v_2(j)=t-s_2(t) follows either by summing
floor(t/2^q), or by writing t as a sum of distinct powers of 2. Multiplying
the actual scalar factors -a*c^(v_2(j)) yields (C95.3). For positive t,
its absolute multiplier is at most beta/c=a because s_2(t)>=1 and
beta^t<=beta. When t is even the multiplier is nonnegative and at most1;
when t is odd it lies between -a and0. Thus every candidate x_t lies
on one of the segments [0,z] and [0,-a*z], both contained in D. Induction
now proves the actual recursion never leaves D, including the terminal
point. Necessity follows from the first actual step, whose multiplier is
-a when z is in D. At t=2^k-1, s_2(t)=k, giving (C95.4).

The reflection condition is essential for asymmetric ridges. Take one
scalar ridge with r_-=1,r_+=4,A=0,alpha=1,z=4. On any m>=6,
a>=5/(7*(1+sqrt(2)))>1/4. The first actual point -4a lies below -1.
The objective is legal and z is in the core, but all-core invariance fails.
This is an analytical counterexample, not a numerical case or a new fact.

## A scalable, conditional weak-background obstruction

The preceding criterion has an explicit normalization cost. Let d0=||x0||,
which is the true initial distance because C=I, and put

    Gcap=sum_j alpha_j*max(r_(j,-),r_(j,+)),
    hmax=max h_i, Gcap*S_m<=Ccap*d0,
    0<=A<=2*I/hmax,

where Ccap>=0 is a constant independent of m. The radii may be asymmetric.
Since ||I-h_i*A||<=1 and the clipped-ridge gradient has norm at most Gcap,
the true recursion gives ||x_i||<=d0+Gcap*sum_(j<i)h_j<=(1+Ccap)*d0.

Suppose the actual state z at the start of the FINAL H_k suffix satisfies
z,-a*z in D. No condition is imposed on any earlier queried regions.
By (C95.4), the normalized payoff obeys

    V<= (1+Ccap)^2*beta^(2*(2^k-1))*c^(-2k),
    V/K <=2*(1+Ccap)^2*c^(m-2k)*beta^(2*(2^k-1)).         (C95.5)

Here K>=1/(2S_m+1), and M_m<=c^m: the latter follows by induction from
M_(j+1)=2M_j+a*c^j and 2+a<c, proved above.

For m>=16 take k=ceil(log_2(m^2)). Then k<=m, 2^k>=m^2, and the final
suffix has fewer than 2m^2 steps. The elementary inequality log(1-1/m)
<=-1/m and c<3 imply

    V/K <=2*(1+Ccap)^2*exp(2/m)*(3/exp(2))^m ->0.        (C95.6)

The ratio 3/exp(2) is below1, since exp(2)>1+2+2=5. Thus a family meeting
these hypotheses that remains scalar-competitive cannot pass this capture
test at the start of that final suffix for infinitely many unbounded horizons.
This prices weak (even zero) backgrounds and arbitrarily many preceding
gate changes. It does not prove that capture occurs. Cases that stay
outside D, fail the reflected-point condition, have C!=I, or violate the
displayed cap/background hypotheses remain outside the conclusion.

## Completed finite discovery and exact same-object reconstruction

The first allocation round used 16 charged cases: one calibration, fourteen
discovery trajectories, and one exact reconstruction of discovery case10.
All returned finite data, with no timeout, stale lock, failed prefilter,
installation, optimization solver, or formal verifier call. The public ledger
records 0.6265509749991907 measured/accounted seconds and no external timeout
debit. Twenty cases remain: at most eighteen discovery plus two reserves.
The ledger remains open across the next authorized round; no renewal occurs.

Calibration case1 matched accepted central-cut behavior to a relative
floating discrepancy below 7e-14. Its initial source looked for a stored
benchmark in c76_dp_run01.csv, whose saved table ends at31; the absent63
entry is honestly recorded as null. Subsequent source versions read the
existing c76_dp_run02.csv through4095. No dynamic program or calibration
trajectory was rerun to make this change.

No discovery case beats K. Cases2 and3 never change a queried gate. Twelve
of the fourteen discovery cases do change queried gates. Among these, the
largest floating ratio is case12, approximately0.8972369014*K, or
approximately0.9486665934 times the saved original U_63. It uses the rational
near-parallel directions (12/13,+/-5/13), weights169/288 on each, and an
additional vertical ridge of weight119/144, so C=I and A=0. This observed
payoff is far below the accepted H6 reference56/2711. Neither a global
impossibility claim nor an improving lower witness is inferred.

The central-split family in cases10,14,15 was run on the three separate
original words, each as its own charged case. Their floating V/K ratios
are respectively approximately4.6184e-6,1.5747e-7,2.9887e-9. The raw data
suggested late capture; these three observations do not prove an asymptotic
law or that all nearby models capture. Equations(C95.3)--(C95.6), with their
explicit hypotheses, are the separate symbolic relation extracted here.

The selected same-object reconstruction is case16, reproducing case10:

    m=6, A=0, x0=(1,0),
    u1=(4/5,3/5),u2=(4/5,-3/5),u3=(0,1),
    alpha1=alpha2=25/32, alpha3=7/16.

Put P=sum H5, b=the central bridge, p=1/(1+P),
q=(b-1)/((1+P)*(1+2P)), and d=1/(1+2S6). The two first ridges have
radii (r_minus,r_plus)=(4q/5,p) and(4q/5,3p/5), while the vertical
ridge has(d,d). These are exact elements of Q(sqrt(2)), not rounded inputs.
The two sources use identical defining matrices, directions, weights,
radii formulas, physical word and initial point. The exact run retained
every sampled value, gradient and point, including the terminal sample.

It reproduced the entire numerical gate itinerary exactly:

    ridge1 changes at28,31,32,33,34,35;
    ridge2 changes at32,60;
    ridge3 changes at1,32,60.

All terminal gates are core. Exact arithmetic checked that z=x60 and -a*z
both lie in every closed core strip. The remaining suffix is H2 with the
original m=6 parameters, so (C95.4) gives

    x63=(-125/98+(125/147)*sqrt(2))*x60,
    V=(-125/98+(125/147)*sqrt(2))^2*||x60||^2 < K/100000.

The finite exact check of that strict inequality passed; its approximate
V is1.0540484156979085e-8. This finite reconstruction is supporting evidence
for the joint draft, not a verifier verdict. The analytic legality proof
above and the exact core multiplier explain what was checked; correctness
has not been promoted to the fact graph.

The public raw directory is research/weak_ridge_forward_raw. It contains
the immutable source and request snapshots plus all sixteen raw cases.
The exact source SHA256 is
08af55d51da0a3ada29b2ba0f31e6101941562bcdbbb5b3eaa524dead8a3bee8;
the exact request SHA256 is
479203688567b53b55b3c938335d87ac6e186a781abb157da20600c7d557a87f;
case16 raw SHA256 is
2cc1090655cc4a20547328150b52dd797e026c8367a389f5509a9118485662b7.
The companion summary indexes every raw record and all earlier hashes.

The useful open continuation is precise: seek an actual weak-background
static object whose late state fails the two-point capture condition, or
whose total core matrix C differs from I, while improving on the now-priced
same-word ray reference. Multiple sign or gate changes alone did not supply
that gain in the fourteen completed cases. Eighteen authorized discovery
cases and two reserves remain, under the original cumulative time cap.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000736 -->
# C96: persistent outer gates with an exact transverse cost

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT, not a fact-graph result. Formal
verification is forbidden by the current allocation. The original target is
OPEN. This is the second and last allocated round (completed ceiling 27).

Accepted sources used: b98bb807d514e80d (single-cut and Huber bounds),
1fca3e5dd555cb4a (ray feasibility and objective), e9a55f57461f253c (the
stronger fixed H6 reference), and 04219554e41d9750 (the unconditional
original-denominator limit Z_1). No conditional seed from the last fact is
assumed. C93--C95 are motivation, never proof premises. All new assertions
used in this draft are proved together below.

## 1. Static model, physical word, and parameters

Fix an integer m>=6. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda*(1+1/m),
beta=1-1/m, a=beta/c, and ell=1+a. Define H_0=() and

    H_(k+1)=(H_k,1+a*c^k,H_k), 0<=k<m.

Every subword uses these SAME enclosing-m parameters. Let

    j=2^(m-1)-1, n=2^m-1, P=sum H_(m-1),
    r=a*c^(m-1), b=1+r, d=1/(P+1), sigma=r*d/(2P+1),
    Kcut=r^2*d^2/(2P+1),
    p=12/13, q=5/13, rho=q^2/p^2=25/144,
    kappa=1/ell, J=1+rho*ell, w=1/(2p^2)=169/288.

The symbol Kcut is the accepted central single-cut value on this exact word;
it is not the entire worst-case functional W_n and need not equal the Huber
value. Choose dimensionless constants

    0<epsilon<=1/4, |eta|<=1/4.

For positive Rminus,Rplus define phi(0)=0 and
phi'(s)=clip(s,[-Rminus,Rplus]). Explicitly phi(s)=s^2/2 in the core,
phi(s)=Rplus*s-Rplus^2/2 on the positive outer interval, and
phi(s)=-Rminus*s-Rminus^2/2 on the negative outer interval.

Use u_+=(p,q), u_-=(p,-q), positive radii

    Rplus_+=p*d*(1+epsilon), Rplus_-=p*d*(1-epsilon),
    Rminus_+=p*sigma*(1+eta), Rminus_-=p*sigma*(1-eta),

and the corresponding two functions phi_+,phi_-. Define ONE static function

    F(x,y)=kappa*y^2/2+w*phi_+(p*x+q*y)+w*phi_-(p*x-q*y).

Run actual GD from X_0=(1,0) using L=1 and exactly the word H_m. There is
no objective switch, stepsize adaptation, or local curvature rescaling.

## 2. Global legality and the actual minimizer

The useful uniform parameter bounds are

    2/7<a<sqrt(2)-1<1/2, 9/7<ell<3/2,
    0<kappa<7/9, 0<rho<1/5, 1<J<13/10,
    P>31, r>9, b>10, b/r<10/9.                     (C96.1)

Indeed a=(m-1)/((m+1)*lambda)>=5/(7lambda)>2/7 because
lambda<5/2; also a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1. There are at least 31 steps in
H_(m-1), all greater than one. Finally c>2 gives
r>(2/7)*2^5=64/7>9. The remaining inequalities follow by substitution.

For any two scalar arguments, the difference of a clip is theta times
the difference of those arguments, where 0<=theta<=1. Thus the difference
of gradients at X,Y equals D(X-Y), where

    D=diag(0,kappa)+w*theta_+*u_+*u_+^T+w*theta_-*u_-*u_-^T,
    0<=D<=C=diag(1,kappa+rho)<=diag(1,137/144)<=I.

The Euclidean norm of D is at most one, proving global 1-smoothness.
Each summand is convex, proving convexity of F. Every summand is
nonnegative. Since the two nonparallel ridge vectors span R^2 and each
phi vanishes only at zero, argmin F={0} and min F=0. Consequently the
true initial squared distance is exactly one and the payoff is V=2F(X_n).

The quadratic background is singular, so it has no positive curvature
floor on the full active plane. The total core matrix C is not I. These
properties follow from exact algebraic inequalities, not rounded clipping.

## 3. Exact prefix and its central queried gate

Write X_i=(x_i,y_i). Put

    delta=epsilon*p*d, zeta=eta*p*sigma,
    y_plus=-epsilon*(q/p)*d*ell,
    y_minus=eta*(q/p)*sigma*ell,
    Lcut=epsilon*d*J/2.

As long as both ridges are in the positive outer intervals, the gradient is

    grad F=(d,kappa*y+delta*q/p^2).

The FIRST physical step is ell=1/kappa. It sends y_0=0 exactly to y_plus,
the stationary value of this affine scalar recursion. Every subsequent
step preserves y_plus until a ridge changes gate, irrespective of its size.
The proposed prefix is therefore

    x_i=1-d*sum_(t<i) h_t,
    y_0=0, y_i=y_plus for 1<=i<=j.                 (C96.2)

Here is an induction validating every proposed gate, rather than assuming
the affine formula remains applicable. At i=0 both projections are p, while
Rplus_+<=5p/(4(P+1))<p. For 1<=i<j, the remaining prefix mass includes its
last step ell, so x_i-d>=ell*d. Hence

    p*x_i+q*y_plus-Rplus_+
       >=p*d*(ell-epsilon*J)>0.

The other projection is even larger than p*x_i, and Rplus_-<p*d. This
proves that both ridges remain strictly positive outer before index j.
Thus (C96.2) follows from the ACTUAL recursion, and x_j=d.

At index j the first ridge projection is

    p*d+q*y_plus=p*d*(1-epsilon*(J-1)).

It is positive because epsilon*(J-1)<3/40, and strictly less than
Rplus_+. It therefore lies strictly in that ridge's core. The second
projection is p*d*(1+epsilon*(J-1))>Rplus_-, so the second ridge is still
positive outer. Direct differentiation now gives

    grad F(X_j)=(d-Lcut,-(q/p)*Lcut),
    x_(j+1)=-r*d+b*Lcut,
    y_(j+1)=epsilon*(q/p)*d*(b*J/2-ell).          (C96.3)

The last y-coordinate is positive by b>10 and ell<3/2. In particular the
central gradient is noncollinear with the stationary precentral gradient
(d,0). The actual gate Hessians diag(0,kappa) and
diag(0,kappa)+w*u_+*u_+^T do not commute: their off-diagonal commutator
has nonzero magnitude kappa*w*p*q.

## 4. Both ridges stay negative outer throughout the full suffix

First verify the point immediately after the central step. By (C96.1),

    x_(j+1)+(q/p)*abs(y_(j+1))
      <=d*(-r+b*epsilon*J*(1+rho)/2)
      <d*(-r+39*b/200)
      <-47*r*d/60.

Since 2P+1>63 and |eta|<=1/4, this is strictly below

    -sigma*(1+|eta|) >= -5*r*d/(4*(2P+1)).

More explicitly, 47/60>5/(4*(2P+1)), so the last strict comparison has
the required orientation. Both ridge projections are therefore below
their own negative thresholds at X_(j+1).

On the negative outer intersection the full gradient is

    grad F=(-sigma,kappa*y-q*zeta/p^2).

The FIRST step of the right H_(m-1) block is again ell. It sets y exactly
to y_minus. Subsequent steps preserve that value as long as the gates
stay negative outer, while x increases monotonically. The proposed terminal
point is

    x_n=-sigma*(P+1)+b*Lcut, y_n=y_minus.         (C96.4)

To validate all the proposed post-first-step suffix gates it suffices to
check the largest proposed x, namely x_n. The inequality

    x_n+(q/p)*abs(y_minus)<-sigma*(1+|eta|)

is equivalent to

    P-|eta|*J > (b/r)*(2P+1)*epsilon*J/2.        (C96.5)

The left side exceeds P-13/40. The right side is less than
13*(2P+1)/72. Their difference exceeds
(23/36)*P-13/40-13/72>0, because P>31. This proves (C96.5).
Induction now verifies the complete actual suffix, including its unqueried
terminal point. It does not replace physical steps by a Hessian-dependent
schedule. Every one of the slanted ridges remains strictly negative outer
at every postcentral query and at the terminal point.

This trajectory NEVER visits the common core intersection. In particular,
it refutes an unconditional extension of eventual common-core capture to
all singular-background, nonsaturated-core models. Its poor objective
cannot be attributed to entering the common quadratic region.

## 5. Exact objective and a whole two-parameter family obstruction

When both ridges are negative outer, expanding the defining static function
gives

    F(x,y)=-sigma*x-(q*zeta/p^2)*y
           -(sigma^2+zeta^2/p^2)/2+kappa*y^2/2.

No function-value offsets are deleted. At y=y_minus=q*zeta/(p^2*kappa),
the transverse linear and quadratic terms combine to
-q^2*zeta^2/(2p^4*kappa). Substituting (C96.4) yields

    V=2F(X_n)
     =Kcut-2*sigma*b*Lcut-zeta^2*J/p^2
     =Kcut*(1-epsilon*b*J/r-eta^2*J/(2P+1)).    (C96.6)

This is the main joint statement: for EVERY m>=6 and EVERY allowed
epsilon,eta the single static two-ridge function above is globally legal,
has the stated actual minimizer and queried itinerary, and obeys (C96.6).
The proof is entirely symbolic and independent of finite trajectory runs.

The payoff is strictly smaller than Kcut because epsilon>0. V is positive,
since the terminal point is strictly in the negative outer region and is
not the unique minimizer. As an algebraic check, epsilon*b*J/r<13/36
and eta^2*J/(2P+1)<13/10080, so the displayed bracket is positive as well.

Accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives Kcut<=K_n(H_m), and accepted
1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives K_n(H_m)<=F_ray_n(H_m)<=W_n(H_m). Consequently
this entire explicit changing-region family fails to improve even the
accepted central single cut, for all horizons in its stated domain. A
negative tilt eta cannot repair the loss: it incurs the exact additional
quadratic price in (C96.6). This is not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.

The same conclusion prices a free initial transverse displacement as long
as the initial point (1,y0) lies in both positive outer regions. Its first
step sends y0 to the same y_plus, so all later states and terminal values
are unchanged. Its TRUE initial squared distance is 1+y0^2; its normalized
payoff is the expression in (C96.6) divided by 1+y0^2. A large transverse
initial coordinate cannot be silently removed from the denominator.

## 6. A scalable law on the ORIGINAL enclosing-m sequence

Fix epsilon,eta in the displayed ranges independently of m. Let
M_k=1+sum H_k and r_k=a*c^k. From the physical word,

    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+r_k,
    M_k=2^k+a*(c^k-2^k)/(c-2).                 (C96.7)

Here c,a still depend on m. As m tends to infinity, c tends to lambda,
a tends to 1/lambda=lambda-2, (2/c)^(m-1) tends to zero, and therefore
M_(m-1)/c^(m-1) tends to 1. Also (1+1/m)^(m-1) tends to exp(1). Thus

    r/(P+1) -> 1/lambda,
    lambda^m*Kcut -> 1/(2*exp(1)*lambda),
    J -> 1+rho*sqrt(2), b/r -> 1.

The second limit follows directly by writing
Kcut=[r/(P+1)]^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). In (C96.6), the eta term tends to zero.
We obtain the fully priced nonzero limit

    lambda^m*V ->
      [1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/[2*exp(1)*lambda].   (C96.8)

The unconditional portion of accepted fact 04219554e41d9750 states that
Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1) exists and is finite and positive, for the
ORIGINAL all-split benchmark denominator. Hence

    B_(2^m-1)*V ->
      Z_1*[1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/[2*exp(1)*lambda]. (C96.9)

Only that accepted unconditional limit is used; no seed assumption or
conditional separation from the same fact is imported. Formula (C96.9)
is the payoff of this exhibited family, not the true worst-case value.

For comparison with the same-word global Huber witness, S=sum H_m and
M_m=1+S. Equations (C96.7) give lambda^m/(1+2S)->1/(2*exp(1)), so

    V / [1/(1+2S)] ->
      [1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/lambda < 1.

No finite m=6,7,8 observation is used to prove these limits. Conversely
none of those finite cases is described as a test of an m>=128 theorem.

## 7. Bounded discovery, reconstruction, and failure accounting

The round started from the existing ledger at 16 charged cases, 14 discovery
cases, one calibration, one exact reconstruction, and 0.6265509749991907
measured/accounted seconds. Calibration against the accepted scalar central
cut had already passed in case1; it was not rerun or treated as free.

Cases17--22 weakened or removed the vertical ridge in the previous rational
paired-ridge models. All six returned finite trajectories and changed queried
gates; none exceeded K. Cases23--28 replaced that vertical ridge by a singular
quadratic background with rational coefficient. All six also returned finite
trajectories and no gain. The largest successful new discovery ratio was
case23 at approximately 0.8972369014212274*K. Its background has a zero
horizontal eigenvalue, so no uniform positive background floor is presumed.

Case29 was a CHARGED FAILED proposal. Its exact algebraic-curvature prefilter
raised TypeError: "'>=' not supported between instances of 'Q2' and 'int'".
The raw error, request/source hashes, and public launch event are retained.
The run stopped after that error. Defining the missing exact comparison
operators repaired the implementation; separately charged case30 retried
the same proposed static function. No accounting was reset or erased.

Case30 uses epsilon=1/12, eta=0 in the family above. Case31 uses
epsilon=eta=1/12. Case32 uses epsilon=1/4, eta=0. Their numerical V/K ratios
were respectively approximately 0.8959721668863, 0.8959216103034, and
0.6879165006589. Their actual two-ridge itineraries are exactly the proposed
central chronology, with changes at indices 31,32 for ridge1 and 32 for
ridge2. Both terminal ridges are negative outer. No finite gain is claimed.

Broad discovery stopped after these cases. The final two reserves were used
ONLY for exact reconstruction of the SAME static objects as cases30 and31:
cases33 and34 respectively. They preserve the original m=6 physical steps,
algebraic kappa, rational directions and weights, all radii definitions, and
initial point. Exact Q(sqrt(2)) arithmetic used rational-square sign tests;
decimal conversion was only for readable outputs. Both runs checked every
actual gate, every sampled value/gradient/point, the central and terminal
coordinate formulas, and the EXACT identity (C96.6), including its strict
comparison with the central cut. Neither is a verifier verdict.

The cumulative ledger before closure has 34 charged cases: 30 discovery
(including the one charged error), one calibration, and three exact
reconstructions. There are 33 finite raw statuses and one error. The two
remaining discovery slots are retired on allocation closure; reserves are
fully used. No solver, installation, formal verifier call, C59 replay, or
benchmark dynamic program was run. Final metered seconds and hashes are
recorded in the companion manifest after closure, not estimated here.

## 8. Same-word references and interpretation

All forward results retain the global Huber term, every single-cut value,
the best single-cut index, and the sufficient first-two-ray comparison on
the SAME physical word. The complete finite symbolic argument showing
F_ray=K for m=6,7,8 is reproduced as an integrated appendix below. It is
joint draft mathematics, not an invocation of unverified C95 as a fact.

For m=6 the stronger accepted reference is e9a55f57461f253c:
W_63(H6)>56/2711>1/50. The exhibited root family is far smaller even by
an elementary comparison. If t_k=r_k/M_k, then
t_(k+1)=c*t_k/(2+t_k). Since c>lambda, one has
c*(c-2)>lambda*(lambda-2)=1>beta and hence a<c-2. Also
c<=7*lambda/6<35/12<3. Thus a<c-2<1, and induction gives t_k<c-2<1,
so Kcut<1/(2P+1)<1/63<1/50. The C59 reference is not transplanted to
different enclosing-m words, and none of its 4160 checks was replayed.

This work supplies a concrete scoped obstruction and a useful exact relation.
Persistent outer gates, a singular background, a nonsaturated core matrix,
and a noncommuting central gate do not by themselves yield improved payoff.
The decisive restriction in the explicit solved family is the reciprocal
short-step coefficient, which kills the transverse transient at the first
step of each child. General coefficients retain a transverse polynomial
and are outside this exact family theorem. General multilevel gate changes,
unequal ridge directions/weights, and arbitrary static models remain open.

The general coefficient relation, useful for a future authorized round, is
as follows. Retain a fixed kappa>0 and the same symmetric directions/weights
and radii means. Suppose the ACTUAL chronology is both plus before the
central query, core/plus there, and both minus afterwards. Let
R=product_(h in H_(m-1))(1-kappa*h), delta=(Rplus_+-Rplus_-)/2,
zeta=(Rminus_+-Rminus_-)/2, and

    yj=-delta*q*(1-R)/(p^2*kappa),
    L=(delta-q*yj)/(2p),
    yc=[1-b*(kappa+rho/2)]*yj-b*delta*q/(2p^2),
    ye=q*zeta/(p^2*kappa),
    xn=-r*d+b*L+P*sigma, yn=ye+(yc-ye)*R.

Solving the two affine recursions and differentiating the central core gate
gives these formulas directly. Substitution into the exact static value gives

    V=-2*sigma*xn-2*q*zeta*yn/p^2-sigma^2-zeta^2/p^2+kappa*yn^2.

Unlike (C96.6), this last interface is CONDITIONAL on its stated complete
gate itinerary. It is not a construction certificate for arbitrary kappa.
The exact root family proves that itinerary separately in Sections 3--4.

The original arbitrary-schedule exact-profile problem remains unresolved.
No lower witness on H_m, even if stronger, would alone disprove minimax
optimality: that negative goal needs an improving schedule's full-class
upper certificate.

## Appendix: complete same-word finite scalar comparison

This appendix is included in the present joint derivation. It has no
unverified predecessor. Here K is the accepted functional K_n(H_m), and
F_ray is the complete accepted family from fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a.

Here is a direct sufficient test for the complete accepted ray family to
equal K. For i<j put P=sum_(t<i)h_t and D=sum_(i<t<j)h_t. If

    (h_i-1)(h_j-1)<(1+P)(1+D)                 (R)

for every i<j, no feasible accepted ray selection has two checkpoints.
Indeed its first two checkpoint gradients are
m_0=1/(1+P), m_1=(h_i-1)m_0/(1+D), with old offset P*m_0.
The second-return budget is (h_j-1)m_1-P*m_0<m_0 by (R).
With exactly two checkpoints the old-gradient requirement is at least
m_0, and with more checkpoints the third magnitude would be below m_0.
Both contradict the explicit feasibility conditions of 1fca3e5dd555cb4a.
Zero and one checkpoint produce exactly K. Any numerical check of (R)
will be labeled numerical until an exact symbolic bound is supplied.



Write r_j=a*c^j and M_j=1+sum H_j. Then

    M_0=1, M_(j+1)=2*M_j+r_j,
    t_j=r_j/M_j, t_0=a, t_(j+1)=c*t_j/(2+t_j).

For m>=6, c>1+sqrt(2) and beta<1 imply a<c-2. Hence t_j increases
and stays below c-2. Also

    beta-(c-2) = 3-(1+sqrt(2))-(2+sqrt(2))/m
       >= (10-7*sqrt(2))/6 >0,

so t_j>=a>(c-2)/c.

For any two chronological indices i<j with levels u,v, define the ratio
R_ij in (R) with the right side as its denominator. If u>=v, the prefix
before i contains H_u and the gap before j contains H_v; thus
R_ij<=t_u*t_v. The largest level m-1 occurs only once, so at least one
of u,v is at most m-2. It follows that

    R_ij<=t_(m-1)*t_(m-2)<c*t_(m-2)^2.

If u<v, both the prefix before i and the gap from i to j contain H_u.
Their total mass is at least sum H_v-(1+r_u), since the prefix before
j contains H_v. Consequently, putting x=1+P and y=1+D,
x,y>=M_u and x+y>=M_v-r_u. From
(x-M_u)(y-M_u)>=0 we obtain

    x*y>=M_u*(M_v-M_u-r_u).

Let d=v-u>=1 and t=t_u. Directly iterating the recurrence for M gives

    (M_v-M_u-r_u)/M_u
      =2^d-1+t*((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1)
      >=c^(d-1)+(c-1)*(2^d-2)/c>=c^(d-1).

The first inequality uses t>=(c-2)/c and the nonnegative coefficient
((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1). Therefore R_ij<=c*t_u^2<=c*t_(m-2)^2.
Equality in the complete maximum occurs at the first level-(m-2) bridge
and the immediately following level-(m-1) bridge: both intervening
prefix/gap masses are sum H_(m-2). Thus

    max_(i<j) R_ij = c*t_(m-2)^2.                         (A1)

This proves the full pair test symbolically; it does not rely on sampled
pair scans. Here are short rational upper certificates for the three
authorized horizons. Monotonicity of c*t/(2+t) in both positive variables
allows use of the displayed upper parameters throughout.

* m=6: c<17/6 and a<3/10. Four recurrence steps give
  t_4<83521/144462<7/12. Thus c*t_4^2<833/864<1.
  The intermediate bounds are 17/46,289/654,4913/9582,83521/144462.
* m=7: c<83/30 and a<5/16. Bounds for t_1,...,t_5 are respectively
  3/8,7/16,1/2,83/150,3/5. For the last step,
  6889/11490<3/5; hence c*t_5^2<747/750<1.
* m=8: c<68/25 and a<13/40. The upper recurrence has reciprocal at
  its sixth step equal to
  25/18+(395/234)*(25/34)^6. This is greater than 33/20 because
  (25/34)^3>79/200 and (79/200)^2>611/3950. Therefore t_6<20/33,
  and c*t_6^2<27200/27225<1.

All parameter enclosures follow by squaring positive rational bounds for
sqrt(2). In particular sqrt(2)<10/7, sqrt(2)>29/21 prove the m=6
bounds; sqrt(2)<341/240 and sqrt(2)>7/5 prove m=7;
sqrt(2)<319/225 and sqrt(2)>163/117 prove m=8.
Combining (A1) with the accepted ray feasibility conditions proves

    F_ray_(2^m-1)(H_m)=K_(2^m-1)(H_m), m=6,7,8.           (A2)

No m>=128 result is used here. This finite symbolic comparison is part of the present UNVERIFIED joint
draft; it is not an accepted graph fact.

For completeness the largest single-cut term is the central cut. For a
fixed level u put p=1+P, q=1+T; both are at least M_u and
p+q=M_m-r_u. The denominator p^2*(2q-1) has no interior minimum on
that interval: its derivative is 2p*(2*(M_m-r_u)-3p-1), with at most
one interior critical point, a maximum. The two endpoint denominators
are ordered by monotonicity of z^2/(2z-1) for z>=1, so the earliest
occurrence of level u is best. Its value is

    t_u^2/[2*M_m-2*M_u-2*r_u-1].

The numerator increases and the positive denominator decreases with u.
Thus the central level m-1 maximizes it. In particular K is exactly
the larger of the global Huber term and the central single-cut term;
no choice of untested checkpoints is hidden in the numerical reference.

The COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED C96 draft is finding2d8525da4bc3b684,
public file research/max3_c96_persistent_outer_gate_exact_loss.md.
Counterexample a18eedfbe5d827cf records the failure of unconditional
common-core capture. Scoped closure cc5db6e6597542fc prices only the
displayed family. The precursor plan is2032a0fd3a8973ab.

For every original H_m,m>=6, define ell=1+a, P=sum H_(m-1), central
step b, d=1/(P+1), sigma=(b-1)*d/(2P+1), p=12/13,q=5/13,
kappa=1/ell, J=1+(25/144)*ell. The STATIC function is

    kappa*y^2/2+(169/288)*[phi_+(p*x+q*y)+phi_-(p*x-q*y)].

Its positive radii are p*d*(1+/-epsilon), and its negative radii are
p*sigma*(1+/-eta), where0<epsilon<=1/4 and|eta|<=1/4. Its total core
matrix is diag(1,kappa+25/144)<=diag(1,137/144), its background is
singular, and its unique minimizer is0. Starting at(1,0), its true
initial squared distance is1.

The actual queried itinerary is both plus before the central query,
core/plus at that query, and both strictly minus at EVERY subsequent
query and at the terminal point. The visited Hessians do not commute.
The trajectory NEVER enters the common core. All gate inequalities are
proved uniformly in m and in the two asymmetry parameters.

Nevertheless, with Kcut the accepted central single-cut value,

    V=Kcut*[1-epsilon*b*J/(b-1)-eta^2*J/(2P+1)]<Kcut.

The first physical step in each child kills the transverse affine
transient exactly; the central positive asymmetry costs linearly, and
negative asymmetry costs quadratically. An initial transverse coordinate
y0 satisfying the initial plus gates leaves every later state unchanged
and divides the same objective numerator by the true distance1+y0^2.

For fixed epsilon,eta, the separate symbolic argument gives

    lim B_(2^m-1)*V
      =Z_1*[1-epsilon*(1+(25/144)*sqrt(2))]
           /[2*exp(1)*(1+sqrt(2))].

Only the unconditional accepted denominator limit in04219554e41d9750 is
used here. No conditional seed is presumed. This family is weaker than
the accepted single cut, and eventually weaker than the global Huber
witness. It is NOT asymptotic target-strength progress.

The joint draft retains a precise conditional interface away from the
reciprocal coefficient: R=product_(h in H_(m-1))(1-kappa*h) appears in
both affine child recursions. The terminal transverse energy can then
survive. The missing step is a legal useful chronology whose energy pays
for the central horizontal loss and true initial distance. No uniform
conclusion about all nonsaturated static ridge models is established.

The cumulative forward ledger is now closed_by_owner at34 charged cases,
30 discovery,1 calibration,3 exact reconstructions, and
1.3588828420033678 measured/accounted seconds. This round added18 cases.
All4 reserves have been used; the2 unused discovery slots are retired.
There were33 finite raw statuses and1 charged error. No timeout or stale
lock was encountered. No solver, installation, benchmark-DP run, C59
4160-check replay, verifier call, or subagent occurred.

Case29 failed during the algebraic PSD prefilter with
TypeError: "'>=' not supported between instances of 'Q2' and 'int'".
The source lacked the two greater-than comparison methods. The error
and its hashes remain public. The model stopped after the failure;
case30 was a separately charged retry after the exact-method repair.
No failure or debit was erased.

No gain over the same-word scalar reference appeared. Exact case33
reconstructs the SAME object as case30 (epsilon1/12,eta0); exact case34
reconstructs case31 (epsilon=eta=1/12). Their exact Q(sqrt(2)) runs

## DOC-000738 — archive_full_20261010/project/research/max3_chebyshev_cut_obstruction.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010-full-evidence.zip!/project/research/max3_chebyshev_cut_obstruction.md

原始字节数：7351；原始 SHA-256：ae2f1b356acdaa8a20291c05c883eef90dcb04bd7fd1a2da340660019ee50845

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000738 -->
# An explicit ordering defeating quadratics plus all single-cut Huber witnesses

Status: complete candidate derivation, unverified. The initial-wave verification
allowance is already used by fact b98bb807d514e80d. This strengthening is for
main-agent prioritization. Worker: max3.

## Statement and scope

Let K_n(h) be the verified single-cut functional in fact b98bb807d514e80d.
For a schedule h define its exact quadratic-class worst case

\[
Q_n(h)=\max_{0\leq\lambda\leq1}
\lambda\prod_{t=0}^{n-1}(1-h_t\lambda)^2,
\qquad J_n(h)=\max\{K_n(h),Q_n(h)\}.
\]

Here Q_n is exactly the normalized worst case over convex quadratics in any
finite dimension with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L, after scaling
L=1. Indeed diagonalizing their symmetric Hessian makes the normalized error
a weighted average of the displayed scalar expression, and each scalar value
is attained by a one-dimensional quadratic. Thus J_n(h)<=W_n(h).

For every integer n>=3 put theta=pi/(2n+1) and

\[
a_k=\frac1{\sin^2(k\theta)}\quad(1\leq k\leq n).
\]

Define h^(n) by listing a_k with even indices k in decreasing index order,
followed by a_k with odd indices k in increasing index order. For example the
index order at n=7 is (6,4,2,1,3,5,7). This is a fully explicit positive,
finite, predetermined schedule. Then

\[
Q_n(h^{(n)})=\frac1{(2n+1)^2},\qquad
K_n(h^{(n)})\leq72\sin^2\theta
\leq\frac{72\pi^2}{(2n+1)^2}.
\]

Using B_n=1/U_n^ZJ<=1+4n^p with p=log_2 3<2 from verified fact
b98bb807d514e80d, it follows that

\[
\frac{J_n(h^{(n)})}{U_n^{ZJ}}
\leq\frac{72\pi^2(1+4n^p)}{(2n+1)^2}\longrightarrow0.
\]

This rules out proving the target by taking only the maximum of all quadratic
witnesses, the global Huber witness, and the single-cut witnesses in K_n.
It does not give any upper bound on W_n(h^(n)); a nonlinear history-dependent
witness could yield a much larger error.

## Exact quadratic calculation

Define polynomials T_0(x)=1, T_1(x)=x and
T_{r+1}(x)=2xT_r(x)-T_{r-1}(x) for integers r>=1. The cosine addition formula
and induction give T_r(cos u)=cos(ru). The recurrence also shows that T_r
has degree r and parity r. Write M=2n+1. Then T_M is odd and
T_M'(0)=M sin(M pi/2)=(-1)^n M, by differentiating the cosine identity
on -1<x<1. Therefore

\[
R_n(\lambda)=\frac{(-1)^nT_M(\sqrt\lambda)}{M\sqrt\lambda}
\]

extends to a polynomial of degree n with R_n(0)=1. For each k=1,...,n,
T_M(sin(k theta))=cos(M pi/2-k pi)=0. These give n distinct positive
roots of R_n, hence

\[
R_n(\lambda)=\prod_{k=1}^n\left(1-\frac\lambda{\sin^2(k\theta)}\right).
\]

For lambda in [0,1], |T_M(sqrt(lambda))|<=1. It follows that
lambda R_n(lambda)^2<=1/M^2. At lambda=1, T_M(1)=1 and equality holds.
Permutation of the step factors leaves the product unchanged, proving Q_n.

## Bounds on neighboring step sizes in the stated ordering

All a_k are at least1 and decrease with k. The function sin u/u decreases
on 0<u<=pi/2, since its derivative has numerator u cos u-sin u and the
derivative of that numerator is -u sin u<=0. Hence whenever k+2<=n,

\[
\frac{a_k}{a_{k+2}}
=\frac{\sin^2((k+2)\theta)}{\sin^2(k\theta)}
\leq\left(\frac{k+2}{k}\right)^2.
\tag{1}
\]

The first even-indexed step and the last odd-indexed step have index at
least n-1. Since n>=3 implies (n-1)/(2n+1)>=1/4, both are at most2.
Also a_2>=a_1/4 and a_3>=a_1/9, by the same decreasing-sinc argument
(or the double-angle identity for a_2).

## Every single-cut term remains small

For a selected position j, write b=h_j, P=P_j, T=T_j as in the verified
definition of K_n. Replace (b-1)_+ by b when an upper estimate is needed.

1. A step before the central largest step a_1 has even index. If it is the
first step, b/(P+1)<=2. Otherwise its preceding step is a_{k+2}; by (1),
b<=4a_{k+2}<=4P. Thus b/(P+1)<=4 in either case. Its suffix contains a_1,
so its cut term is at most16/(1+2a_1)<=8/a_1.

2. At a_1 the prefix contains a_2>=a_1/4 and the suffix contains
a_3>=a_1/9. Therefore its cut term is at most

\[
\frac{a_1^2}{(a_1/4)^2(2a_1/9)}=\frac{72}{a_1}.
\]

3. After a_1, the prefix always has P>=a_1. For a step a_k that is not
the last step, k is odd and k>=3, and its suffix contains a_{k+2}.
By (1), a_{k+2}>=a_k/4. Its cut term is at most

\[
\frac{a_k^2}{a_1^2(2a_k/4)}
=\frac{2a_k}{a_1^2}\leq\frac2{a_1}.
\]

At the last step b<=2, hence (b-1)^2<=1 and the cut term is at most
1/a_1^2<=1/a_1.

Finally the global Huber term is at most1/(1+2a_1)<=1/(2a_1).
The maximum of these bounds is72/a_1=72 sin^2 theta. Since sin(theta)<=theta,
this proves the claimed estimate, entirely analytically for every n>=3.

## Source grounding

The standard trigonometric definition of T_r is documented at NIST DLMF
18.5.1, https://dlmf.nist.gov/18.5. The polynomial identities used here are
derived above from their recurrence, so no external theorem is used as a
black-box proof step. Matlas was queried for the weighted Chebyshev product
identity and did not return a useful exact hit.

## A legal two-curvature witness exposes the missing interaction

The schedules above are severely unstable on an explicit globally legal
one-dimensional function. This calculation is part of the same unverified
candidate package and does not claim that every schedule is unstable.

For n>=3 keep M=2n+1 and theta=pi/M and put
mu=sin^2(5 theta/2). Define

\[
f_n(x)=\begin{cases}
\mu x^2/2,&x\geq0,\\
x^2/2,&x\leq0.
\end{cases}
\]

The derivative equals mu x on the nonnegative side and x on the nonpositive
side. It is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz: within each half-line
this holds by its slope, while for x<=0<=y its increment mu y-x is between0
and y-x. Hence f_n is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth, and minimized at0.
Start x_0=1.

The chosen angle gives sin^2(2theta)<mu<sin^2(3theta). Along the even-index
prefix, every index k>=4 has 1-a_k mu>0, so the iterates stay positive.
At the last even step a_2, the factor is negative, so the next point is
negative. The following step a_1 therefore uses curvature1; because a_1>1,
it changes the sign back to positive. Every remaining odd-index step has
k>=3 and 1-a_k mu>0, so all later iterates stay positive. No factor is zero.

Relative to the ordinary quadratic product R_n(mu), exactly one factor,
at a_1, is replaced. Consequently

\[
x_n=\frac{1-a_1}{1-a_1\mu}R_n(\mu).
\]

Since arcsin(sqrt(mu))=5theta/2, the trigonometric identity proved above gives
R_n(mu)=sin(5pi/2)/(M sqrt(mu))=1/(M sqrt(mu)). Thus the exact normalized
error on this legal instance is

\[
2 f_n(x_n)=\mu x_n^2
=\frac{\cos^4\theta}
{M^2[\sin^2(5\theta/2)-\sin^2\theta]^2}.
\tag{3}
\]

For every n>=3, theta<=pi/7<pi/4, so cos^4(theta)>=1/4, and the square
bracket in (3) is positive and at most(25/4)theta^2. Therefore

\[
W_n(h^{(n)})\geq\frac{4(2n+1)^2}{625\pi^4}.
\]

More precisely the displayed instance's error divided by M^2 tends to
16/(441 pi^4), since sin u/u tends to1 and cos u tends to1. This is a direct
analytic GD trajectory, not an interpolation relaxation or numerical test.

The mechanism is the ordered pair a_2,a_1: the first crosses into the unit
curvature side, the second amplifies there and returns to the low-curvature
side. A next all-schedule candidate can therefore retain a fixed pair of
one-sided curvatures and its chronological sign transition rule, rather than
only the terminal quadratic polynomial or one selected crossing.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000739 -->
# Exact two-envelope interpolation state across a GD cut

Status: support derivation, unverified; not a target proof or a proposed scalar
comparison with the Zhang--Jiang benchmark. Worker: max3.

Normalize L=1 and the minimizer datum to (x_star,g_star,f_star)=(0,0,0).
For every sampled jet (x_i,g_i,f_i) in a finite history I define

\[
y_i=x_i-g_i,\qquad z_i=f_i-\tfrac12\|g_i\|^2.
\]

The smooth-convex interpolation inequality is exactly

\[
z_i\geq z_j+\langle g_j,y_i-y_j\rangle\quad(i,j\in I).
\]

Define two finite polyhedral envelopes, retaining their full functions:

\[
A_I(u)=\max_{i\in I}\{z_i+\langle g_i,u-y_i\rangle\},
\qquad
B_I(v)=\max_{i\in I}\{\langle v,y_i\rangle-z_i\}.
\]

Both are nonnegative because the minimizer datum is in I. A proposed new jet
at x with gradient g and transformed value z=f-||g||^2/2 is compatible with
every old jet if and only if, writing y=x-g,

\[
A_I(y)\leq z\leq\langle g,y\rangle-B_I(g).
\tag{1}
\]

The left inequality lists all old-to-new constraints. The right inequality
lists all new-to-old constraints. In particular the exact gradient admissibility
test is A_I(x-g)+B_I(g)<=<g,x-g>. There is no maximization or deletion of
cross-correlations before this test.

After accepting (y,g,z), the updates are exactly

\[
A_{I+}(u)=\max\{A_I(u),z+\langle g,u-y\rangle\},\quad
B_{I+}(v)=\max\{B_I(v),\langle v,y\rangle-z\}.
\tag{2}
\]

Thus a full-history state can be represented by two polyhedral functions and
the current point/gradient. A redundant affine piece may be removed only when
it is pointwise dominated in the corresponding entire envelope. The number of
pieces is not bounded independently of the horizon by this derivation.

## Explicit legal witness for any admitted finite transcript

Let phi=A_I for the complete admitted transcript. Its defining inequalities
give phi(y_i)=z_i and
phi(u)>=phi(y_i)+<g_i,u-y_i>. Moreover phi>=0 and phi(0)=0.
Define the globally finite function

\[
f(x)=\min_u\{\phi(u)+\tfrac12\|x-u\|^2\}.
\tag{3}
\]

The minimizer p(x) exists because phi>=0 makes the objective coercive, and is
unique because the quadratic is strictly convex. At x_i, the displayed support
inequality and x_i=y_i+g_i show that the difference between the objective at u
and at y_i is at least ||u-y_i||^2/2. Therefore p(x_i)=y_i,
f(x_i)=z_i+||g_i||^2/2=f_i.

For completeness the smoothness argument is elementary. If p=p(x), minimizing
along the segment from p to an arbitrary u and using convexity of phi gives
phi(u)>=phi(p)+<x-p,u-p>. Thus x-p is a subgradient of phi at p. Applying
this support inequality in both directions at x and x' gives

\[
\langle (x-p(x))-(x'-p(x')),p(x)-p(x')\rangle\geq0.
\]

For r(x)=x-p(x), substitute p(x)-p(x')=x-x'-(r(x)-r(x')) to obtain
||r(x)-r(x')||^2<=<r(x)-r(x'),x-x'>. Cauchy--Schwarz gives that r is
1-Lipschitz. Evaluating (3) at p(x) proves
f(x+d)<=f(x)+<r(x),d>+||d||^2/2. Reversing the points and using the
Lipschitz bound on r gives a matching first-order lower estimate with error
at most 3||d||^2/2. Hence f is differentiable and grad f=r. Convexity follows
by taking convex combinations of candidate minimizing points in the jointly
convex objective of (3). Since phi>=0 and phi(0)=0, f>=0 and f(0)=0.
This proves finite-dimensional realizability, convexity, smoothness, and a valid
minimizer, with no appeal to a merely PSD matrix.

## Relation to the true objective, and its limitation

At step t, query at the point x_t already fixed by the prior GD update; choose
(g_t,z_t) satisfying (1), update the envelopes by (2), and set
x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t g_t. Starting from A=B=0 (the minimizer datum), every complete
admitted transcript is realized by (3), and every legal trajectory satisfies
(1). The final normalized objective is
2 z_n+||g_n||^2 when ||x_0||=1. Consequently taking the supremum of this
quantity over the exact envelope recursion and all finite dimensions is the
actual W_n(h). Zero steps and arbitrary finite positive steps need no exception.

Necessity of the interpolation inequality can also be derived directly: for
fixed x_j put q(x)=f(x)-<g_j,x>. This convex 1-smooth function is minimized
at x_j. The descent estimate, obtained by integrating its Lipschitz gradient,
gives q(x_i-grad q(x_i))<=q(x_i)-||grad q(x_i)||^2/2. Comparing with q(x_j)
is exactly the required inequality.

This is an exact growing-state reformulation, not a reduction in minimax
strength. No invariant here compares its value to U_n^ZJ. A claim that the
envelope game uniformly dominates that benchmark, without a new mechanism,
would merely restate the target lower bound and must be marked BLOCKED.

Source context: Taylor, Hendrickx, Glineur, arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem 1,
Theorem 4, Corollary 1 and their proofs were downloaded and read. The algebraic
state and witness proof above are written out to avoid using them as unproved
black boxes. No external theorem is invoked in this support derivation.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000740 -->
# C80: an exact two-dimensional four-step primitive obstruction

Status: UNVERIFIED joint candidate, no verifier allocation. This tests a
specific full primitive interface, not the original asymptotic theorem.
The defining construction and every required normal are given below.

Let h=(a,b,c,d), with a,d>=0, b>1, c>1. Define

 P=a+1, B=b-1, C=c-1, D=d+1, U=b+c+d, A=1+a+b+c+d.

Assume D>BC and put

 Kappa=B^2 C+U(D-BC), t=B^2 C/Kappa.

Then Kappa>0 and0<t<1. In the Euclidean plane define

 u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(B u+D w)/c,
 K=conv(u,v,w), F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).

Here K is a nondegenerate triangle. Since B u+c v+D w=0 and B,c,D>0,
zero is in its interior. Completing the square shows grad F=P_K,
the Euclidean projection onto K. For completeness, the projection normal
inequalities at two points imply
||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>, so the projection is1-Lipschitz.
The unique-maximizer formula gives
0<=F(x+z)-F(x)-<P_K(x),z><=<P_K(x+z)-P_K(x),z><=||z||^2,
proving differentiability. F is convex as a supremum of affine functions.
Since0 is interior to K, for every x!=0 sufficiently small epsilon>0
has epsilon x in K, whence F(x)>=epsilon(1-epsilon/2)||x||^2>0.
Thus0 is its unique minimizer and F(0)=0.

The actual iterates and gradients are

 x=(P u,u,-B u,D w,w), g=(u,u,v,w,w).                    (1)

The four GD updates follow from c v=-B u-D w and D=d+1.
It remains to check that each listed gradient is the actual projection.
The inner products are

 ||u||^2=1, <u,w>=||w||^2=t,
 <u,v>=-(B+D t)/c, <v,w>=-(B+D)t/c.

At x0 the residual x0-u=a u has nonpositive pairing with v-u and w-u;
at x1 the residual is zero. At x2 the residual y2=-B u-v satisfies

 <w-v,y2>=[-B^2(c-1)+Kappa t]/c^2=0,
 <u-v,y2>=-B(1-t)(c-1)/c<0.

The first equality uses the expansion
Kappa=D(2B+D)+c(D-BD+B)-B c^2
       =B^2(c-1)+(b+c+d)(D-B(c-1)).
At x3 the residual d w pairs to zero with u-w and to
-d t[1+(B+D)/c]<=0 with v-w. At x4 the residual is zero.
These are all vertex comparisons and establish every projection normal.

Put f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Then for every original ordered pair

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
      =<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0.

The optimum comparisons hold because0 belongs to K. In fact Q_(i,star)=0
for all i. There is one explicit globally legal function, with true initial
distance P to its unique minimizer, and no missing terminal comparison.

For the intrinsic energy

 V_h=2sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(f_i-f_4)+sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                            -s(s+1)||g_4||^2,

where s=A-1, summing the GD norm identities and using
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 gives

 V_h=||x0||^2-||x4||^2-2s f4-s(s+1)||g4||^2
     =P^2-A^2 t.                                       (2)

Any original full nonnegative interpolation/PSD certificate must therefore
obey the following additional necessary inequality in this regime:

 P^2 D >= BC[P^2+2BP+BU].                               (3)

Indeed A=P+U and
A^2 B^2C-P^2 Kappa=U{BC[P^2+2BP+BU]-P^2D}, so (2)>=0
is equivalent to (3). This is only a necessary condition for b,c>1 and
D>BC; it is not a full characterization or a global capacity obstruction.

## Fixed rational specialization

One proposed exact word, selected by rounding the geometry of saved
discovery record71, is

 h=(1450,2269,1656,1345)/1000, sum h=168/25.              (4)

The rational evaluator is a single charged fixed-candidate operation.
It will preserve every original rational Gram entry, function value and
ordered Q, all four cut tests, the full scalar-curvature polynomial,
and the exact energy. No numerical PSD repair, clipping, solver rank
assumption or scan is used to establish the triangle construction.
