# C79: four-step full-cone discovery and exact interfaces

Status: UNVERIFIED joint research draft. Wave8, cumulative round18.
No fact_submit is authorized. All new algebra below is derived jointly;
older shared drafts are orientation, never correctness premises.
The accepted interpolation fact1252b8823bb5a290 is the only fact used.

## Primitive energy and the full cone

Normalize smoothness to1. For a nonnegative word h of length n, put
s=sum h_i, A=s+1, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, T_i=sum_(t>i)h_t and
x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. Define

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                      -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.

Let Lambda be the full nonnegative array on distinct finite indices0..n,
with zero diagonal. Set e=(1,...,1)^T, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i, and

 omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
 D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)).

Expansion of the value coefficients gives L^T e=omega, and expansion of
the gradient coefficients gives

 V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>,
 M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.                                      (1)

For example the position part of sum Lambda Q has matrix
sym(L(T-I)); the squared-gradient differences contribute
sym(L)-diag(omega)/2. Adding the displayed diagonal of V gives (1).
Consequently the original full interface is exactly flow, Lambda>=0 and
M>=0. It is sufficient for V>=0 on every actual run, by Q_ij<=0 from
accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Every original finite gradient is retained.

Flow alone forces e^T M e=0: Te has entries1+P_i (P_n=s), and
e^T LTe=omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2, whereas
e^T De=-s^2-sum h_i^2. PSD therefore forces Me=0. Indeed for any v,
positivity of (e+t v)^T M(e+t v) for both signs of small t forces
v^T Me=0. Thus the exact dual formulation may impose Me=0 and maximize
t subject to M-t(I-ee^T/(n+1))>=0. Its t>=0 feasibility is precisely the
original cone. This removes only the proved constant-gradient kernel;
no quadratic equality, rank, ordering, symmetry or sparsity is assumed.

The companion full primal minimizes V subject to Gram(g)>=0,
trace(Gram(g))=1, f_n=0 and every original ordered Q<=0. Its value is
always <=0: a constant-gradient Huber run gives V=0. Every negative
feasible transcript can be lifted to a legal function with a minimizer.
To see this, append the coordinate
g'_i=(g_i,epsilon), x'_i=(x_i,R-epsilon P_i),
f'_i=f_i+c-epsilon^2 P_i, with epsilon>0. Then Q'_ij=Q_ij.
Choose finite c and R so

 c>=max_i(-f_i+epsilon^2 P_i+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2),
 epsilon R>=max_i(f_i+c-<g_i,x_i>+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2).

These inequalities add the zero optimum jet in both orientations. The
conjugate construction of accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the actual
convex1-smooth function. The additional V is epsilon^2 times
2sum h_i(s-P_i)+sum h_i(1-h_i)-s(s+1)=0, since
2sum h_i P_i=s^2-sum h_i^2. No distance-preservation claim is made.

## Zero steps: exact two-way cone correspondence

Partition the query indices into maximal consecutive classes separated
by positive steps. In each class except the last, choose the index of
the positive outgoing step as representative; choose n in the last.
Let hhat be the ordered list of positive steps. Embedding any compressed
certificate on these representatives gives the original V and Q on
their independent formal gradients. All omitted coefficients are zero,
so its full matrix is the coordinate-selection congruence of the
compressed PSD matrix. This proves the forward embedding.

Conversely, restrict an original certificate to formal gradients and
values that are equal within each class. Its V becomes V_hhat; each
cross-class Q becomes the corresponding compressed Q; each within-class
Q is zero. Sum all original nonnegative multipliers on each ordered
pair of distinct classes. This yields compressed nonnegative multipliers,
the compressed flow, and a PSD matrix by congruence. Thus certificate
existence is equivalent in both directions, including all-zero schedules
(V=0, Lambda=0). No numerical model below uses this compression: all
five original gradients are still saved and optimized.

## Necessary filters proved from legal functions

For j with b=h_j>1, write P=P_j, R=T_j and a=(b-1)/(R+1)>0.
Use the one-dimensional convex1-smooth function whose derivative is
clip(x,-a,1), with minimum0, and start at x_0=P+1. Every query through
j is on the upper plateau, the bridge lands at -(R+1)a, and all later
queries remain on the lower plateau until x_n=-a. At every query
f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, equivalently Q_(i,star)=0. Summing the GD norm
identity and substituting this equality into V yields

 V_h=(P+1)^2-A^2 a^2.

Thus a necessary condition for the cone is

 A(h_j-1)_+ <= (P_j+1)(T_j+1), for every j.                (2)

For h_j<=1 this is automatic. At j=0 or n-1, (2) gives
h_j<=2(s+1)/(s+2)<2 when h_j>1. Also, for h_j>1,
h_j-1 <=(P_j+1)(T_j+1)/A<=min(P_j+1,T_j+1), because
A=P_j+T_j+h_j+1 >=max(P_j+1,T_j+1).
At n4 this bounds h_1,h_2<4 using the adjacent endpoint bound.
So [0,2]x[0,4]x[0,4]x[0,2] is a lossless closed outer box.
It does not imply all points of the box, or all points satisfying (2),
are primitive. In particular no upper sum below12 is claimed.

For curvature z in(0,1], use f(x)=z x^2/2, x_0=1, and
p_0(z)=1, p_(i+1)(z)=(1-z h_i)p_i(z). Direct substitution gives

 V_h(z)=sum_i z h_i(1+z-z h_i)p_i(z)^2
                      -s z[1+(s+1)z]p_n(z)^2 >=0.        (3)

At z=1 this becomes 1-A^2 product_i(1-h_i)^2>=0 by telescoping.
Each fixed z gives a necessary test only. The implementation records
z=1,1/2,1/4; it performs no hidden curvature or word scan.

## Exact calibrations

The word (1,2,1,1) is the ConPP join of (1) and (1,1): their masses are
2 and3, the bridge increment d=1 solves d(2+3+d)=2*3, and the physical
bridge is1+d=2. Independently of any external composition theorem, its
complete nonzero certificate entries are

 01:2, 10:4, 21:3, 31:3, 41:3, 14:3,
 23:8, 32:16, 34:35, 43:48, 24:3.

Substitution in (1) gives exactly
M=(e_0-e_1)(e_0-e_1)^T+4(e_2-e_3)(e_2-e_3)^T
                               +16(e_3-e_4)(e_3-e_4)^T.
The child certificates are lambda01=2,lambda10=4,M=(e0-e1)^2
for (1), and lambda01=2,lambda10=4,lambda12=8,lambda21=12,
M=(e0-e1)^2+4(e1-e2)^2 for (1,1).
Thus the exact primitive primal minimum is0, attained by constant jets.

For (3,1,1,1), use x=(1,-2,-3/2,-1,-1/2),
g=(1,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2), f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.
These are samples of the same clipped-gradient function with slopes
[-1/2,1]. They give V=-45/4 and sum g_i^2=2, hence the full primal
minimum is at most -45/8. All20 original interpolation inequalities
and both optimum orientations are obeyed. This fixes the expected sign.

An additional exact length4 zero-remainder ConPP calibration is built
recursively from the empty word: join children a,b of masses A,C using
d>0 satisfying d(A+C+d)=AC, k=((A+C+d)/C)^2, and h=(a,1+d,b).
For bridge query p and terminal query z, the exact identity is

 V_h=V_a+k V_b-(k-1)[sum_(j in b nonterminal)b_j Q_(j,p)
                 +Q_(z,p)+d Q_(p,z)+sum_j b_j Q_(j,z)].  (4)

It follows by expanding Q and using (k-1)(C-d)=2(A+d),
k d^2=A^2 and k C^2=(A+C+d)^2; C>d>0 gives every actual sign.
Each recursive child retains all its original indices. The baseline
uses a=join(empty,empty) and b=join(empty,a), then join(a,b).
The primitive calibration is (1), (4) and its explicit multiplier array,
not an ordinary objective-gap claim. Source terminology was checked in
Zhang--Jiang, arXiv2410.12395v2, Definition2.7 and Theorem3.1 proof;
the displayed identities are independently rederived here.

## Numerical scope and accounting

The authorized new ledger is max3_four_step_seed_discovery_budget.json:
128 charged model/optimization evaluations,120 measured build-plus-solve
seconds,96 distinct nonbaseline proposals,n4 only. Every proposal is
registered before its evaluation, including filtered or failed proposals;
all original data and source hashes are saved. Old closed ledgers are
neither imported as executable runners nor modified.

The search uses all four physical step coordinates with s>=168/25,
the lossless box, necessary (2)-(3), and full primal/dual sensitivities.
Several distant initializations are explored adaptively; neither the sum
nor the rank nor the order is fixed as a global reduction. A local
non-discovery is not global nonexistence. Numerical near-zero, success
status, a clipped eigenvalue or PSD repair is never an exact seed.
Any actual result must satisfy the original flow, all20 signs, full PSD
and sum threshold exactly; C78's separate audit must also be checked.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000742 -->
# Arbitrary even profiles with discrete radial homogeneity are too weak on H_m

Status: complete scoped candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No verifier
call. This extends the fixed-staircase analysis to every shape within a radial
period. Evenness and the stated exact discrete homogeneity are essential
hypotheses; the full scalar and multidimensional classes remain unresolved.

## 1. Statement

Let c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_u=a c^u, and

    H_0=(),  H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),
    N_m=2^m-1,
    s_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).

Consider ANY nondecreasing 1-Lipschitz function g:[0,infinity)->[0,infinity)
with g(0)=0 and

    g(cR)=c g(R),                         R>=0.              (1)

Extend it oddly to the real line and set
f(x)=integral_0^|x| g(R)dR. This gives an even convex differentiable
1-smooth function with fixed minimum f(0)=0. No prescribed plateau, ramp,
curvature pattern, knot count, or sign-crossing history is assumed.

For every m>=17, every x_0!=0, and the complete GD schedule H_m,

    2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2 <= [1/(a c)]c^(-m).                (2)

The function g itself may depend on the entire horizon m. Consequently the
supremum of these objective ratios over the full class (1), divided by the
exact U_(N_m)^ZJ, tends to zero. This is a class obstruction, not a bound
on the unrestricted W_(N_m)(H_m).

## 2. A scalar clock and the quantitative closure inequality

The identically zero g has zero objective. Otherwise g(R)>0 for every R>0:
if it vanished at one positive radius, (1) and monotonicity would make it
vanish on arbitrarily large intervals. The continuous ratio g(R)/R is
periodic in log R. Thus its maximum M>0 is attained on any multiplicative
period, and M<=1 by smoothness. Put ell=1/M>=1.

Define a flow clock

    F(R)=integral_1^R dt/g(t),        R>0.

The arbitrary reference 1 affects only an additive constant. Equation (1)
implies that

    F(cR)-F(R)=tau>0                                        (3)

is independent of R: differentiate in R, or substitute t=cu. Choose r>=17
minimal with b=b_r>=ell. Then b/c<ell<=b. Define

    D=max_(R>0) [F(b g(R))-F(R)].                           (4)

The expression in brackets is periodic under R->cR, so the maximum exists
on a compact radial period. At a maximizer of g(R)/R=1/ell, one has
b g(R)>=R. Therefore D>=0.

The key proved inequality is

    tau+D <=(c-1)b.                                        (5)

Here is a direct proof for every possible input radius; no functional
inequality is left as an assumption. Fix R>0, put u=g(R)>0 and q=R/u>=1.

If q<=b, monotonicity gives

    F(bu)-F(R)<=b-q.                                       (6)

On the period [R,cR], the endpoint gradients are u and cu. Monotonicity and
the 1-Lipschitz condition give the pointwise lower bound

    g(t)>=max(u, cu-(cR-t)).

The switch occurs at t=cR-(c-1)u>=R. Integrating the reciprocal of this
lower bound yields

    tau<= (c-1)(q-1)+log c.                                (7)

Adding (6)-(7) gives

    tau+F(bu)-F(R)
       <=b+(c-2)q-(c-1)+log c
       <=(c-1)b-(c-1)+log c <(c-1)b,

because q<=b, c>2 and log c<c-1.

If q>=b, use (3) to write the same quantity as

    tau+F(bu)-F(R)=F(cbu)-F(R).

When cbu<R this quantity is negative. Otherwise every point of integration
has gradient at least u, so it is at most cb-q<=(c-1)b. This proves (5)
uniformly in R, and hence for the actual maximum D in (4).

## 3. Actual block and checkpoint updates of the same clock

Every step of H_r is at most b/c<ell. Since g(R)<=R/ell, these steps cannot
cross zero from a nonzero point. For a positive-radius step
R'=R-hg(R)>0, monotonicity gives

    F(R)-F(R')=integral_(R')^R dt/g(t)
        >=(R-R')/g(R)=h.                                  (8)

The same calculation applies after reflecting a negative point, because f
is even. Therefore each actual H_r block decreases the clock of the absolute
radius by at least S=s_r.

For a checkpoint of level k, h=b c^k, consider the actual next radius.
If it does not cross zero, its clock decreases. If it crosses, then

    R'=h g(R)-R <= c^k b g(R).

By (3)-(4), this gives

    F(R')-F(R)<=k tau+D.                                   (9)

Since D>=0, (9) also bounds a same-sign checkpoint. A zero landing has zero
terminal objective and can be excluded from the clock calculation.

For r>=17, one has S>=eta b with eta=13/8. To check this directly, note
(10/13)^3<1/2 and (10/13)^2<4/5, hence (10/13)^17<1/40; substitute in
s_r/b_r=[1-(2/c)^r]/(c-2). Since eta>c-1=8/5, (5) gives

    S-tau-D>0.                                            (10)

Thus the growing-state flow description has a genuine, uniformly profitable
update. It is not just a restatement of the unknown scalar optimization.

## 4. Sum the exact recursive word and retain the original normalization

First suppose m>=r and put J=m-r. The word H_m contains 2^J copies of H_r.
Its checkpoint levels k=0,...,J-1 occur 2^(J-k-1) times. Their total level
sum is 2^J-J-1, and their number is 2^J-1. Adding (8)-(9) along the full
actual trajectory gives

    F(|x_(N_m)|)-F(|x_0|)
       <=-2^J S+(2^J-J-1)tau+(2^J-1)D
       =-2^J(S-tau-D)-(J+1)tau-D
       <=-(J+1)tau.                                      (11)

Equation (3) and monotonicity of F imply

    |x_(N_m)|<=c^(-J-1)|x_0|.                             (12)

The global secant bound g(R)<=R/ell supplies the complete area bound
f(x)<=x^2/(2ell). Since ell>b/c,

    2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2
       <=ell^(-1)c^(-2J-2)
       <=b^(-1)c^(-2J-1)
       =a^(-1)c^(-m-J-1)
       <=[1/(a c)]c^(-m).                                (13)

If m<r, every step in H_m is smaller than ell and the entire trajectory
stays on its initial ray. Let w=g(|x_(N_m)|) and R=|x_(N_m)|. Monotonicity
and smoothness give R+s_m w<=|x_0| and f(x_(N_m))<=Rw-w^2/2. Thus

    2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2<=1/(1+2s_m)
       <=[1/(2eta a)]c^(-m)
       <=[1/(a c)]c^(-m),

using m>=17 and 2eta>c. This proves (2) in every case. All distances refer
to the same fixed minimizer zero. Scaling f by L gives exactly the project's
normalization and GD steps h_t/L.

## 5. Explicit exact-profile comparison and finite-knot realizations

For completeness set C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and D_n=2S_bullet(n)+1. Their shifted
joins are Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2 and
G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. The bound
Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) reduces to
t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1. With x=t^(1/3),y=(1-t)^(1/3),
the difference from 1 is x^3 y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0:
both reciprocal terms are at least 1/3 and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction gives
C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Since G(A,D)<=D+4A and the power 4/3 is superadditive on
nonnegative inputs, the dominant DP gives D_n<=1+4n^(4/3). Multiplying (2)
by D_(N_m) proves the claimed vanishing ratio, because c^3>16.

If the profile has finitely many affine pieces within one radial period,
any one finite nonzero trajectory has a finite-knot realization preserving
all its values and gradients. Choose a radius R below every nonzero query
and terminal radius, and put u=g(R)>0 and A=f(R). Smoothness and monotonicity
give

    u^2/2<=A<=Ru-u^2/2.

Replace the inner profile by zero up to
delta=R-u/2-A/u, then a unit-slope ramp of length u, then a plateau at u up
to R. The displayed bounds imply 0<=delta<=R-u. Its terminal gradient is u
and its area is Ru-delta u-u^2/2=A. All larger-radius gradients and values
are therefore unchanged. Apply the same replacement on both rays and cap
outside the largest sampled radius. The original finite GD trajectory and
its exact objective normalization are preserved. This does not claim that
an arbitrary nonhomogeneous core replacement obeys the same global theorem.

The new obstruction includes arbitrary legal within-period geometry,
horizon-dependent shape and scale, dense gradient values, arbitrarily many
knots, and every crossing pattern. It is still restricted to even functions
with the exact radial period c=13/5. The independently phased staircase
result in research/max3_multiscale_flow_obstruction.md remains a separate
asymmetric extension. Neither result decides the full scalar class or the
project's unrestricted all-schedule minimax problem.

## DOC-000743 — archive_full_20261010/project/research/max3_geometric_relay_obstruction.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010-full-evidence.zip!/project/research/max3_geometric_relay_obstruction.md

原始字节数：8591；原始 SHA-256：03c67fa359a55c1b9fcd8180dd43304d1df8cae3fe96b6a4232e2a40fd6597e6

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000743 -->
# Geometric gradient ladders cannot sustain the full recursive crossing pattern

Status: complete scoped candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No new verifier
call. This is a growing-complexity scalar obstruction with an explicit gradient
recurrence. It does not assume nested inward returns. Its hypotheses are the
specified crossing pattern and a geometric grid for gradients on one ray.

## 1. The schedule and the class being tested

Put c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_u=a c^u, H_0=(), and

    H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),
    s_u=sum H_u=a(c^u-2^u)/(c-2).

Fix m>r>=18, set J=m-r, b=b_r, and S=s_r. The exact word H_m consists
of 2^J copies of H_r separated by 2^J-1 checkpoint steps. Their levels form

    T_0=(),       T_(j+1)=(T_j,j,T_j),

where a level ell stands for the actual step b c^ell. In zero-based order,
every even-indexed checkpoint has level zero. The odd-indexed checkpoints
have levels ell>=1; level ell occurs 2^(J-ell-1) times for 1<=ell<=J-1.
The last checkpoint has level zero.

Let f be ANY fixed differentiable convex scalar function with 1-Lipschitz
derivative, f(0)=0=min f. Assume its GD trajectory starts at x_0>0 and has
positive terminal objective. Impose the following two trajectory hypotheses:

1. Every displayed checkpoint crosses zero strictly; no step within a copy
   of H_r crosses zero. Gradients inside those copies may vary arbitrarily.
2. The positive gradient magnitudes just before the level-zero checkpoints
   all belong to one grid {theta gamma^z : z is an integer}, with theta>0
   and gamma>=c. Both parameters may depend on the complete horizon.

There is no grid assumption on negative gradients, no symmetry assumption on
f, no fixed core curvature, and no restriction on whether a return is inward,
overlapping, or outward. The number of crossings is 2^J-1 and can grow with m.

Then the normalized terminal objective satisfies

    2f(x_(2^m-1))/x_0^2
      <= [(b-1)^2/((S+1)^2(2S+1))] gamma^(2-2J)
      <= [1/(2 eta^3 b)] gamma^(2-2J),   eta=13/8.            (1)

In particular, uniformly over r, all gradient-grid parameters, every legal
choice of knots and all such trajectories,

    2f(x_(2^m-1))/x_0^2 <= [c/(2 eta^3 a)] c^(-m).           (2)

The ratio of the supremum in (2) to the exact U_(2^m-1)^ZJ tends to zero.

## 2. Proof of the exact gradient recurrence

For a same-sign block of total step sum S ending at gradient magnitude v,
all earlier magnitudes in that block are at least v and its final radius is
at least v. Consequently its starting radius A satisfies

    A >= (S+1)v.                                           (3)

A crossing step beta whose input gradient magnitude is u has landing radius
at most (beta-1)u, since the input radius is at least u. Thus two consecutive
checkpoints of levels (0,ell), and the two intervening same-sign H_r blocks,
give the following bound between their successive positive checkpoint
gradient magnitudes u and u_next:

    u_next/u <= [(b-1)(b c^ell-1)]/(S+1)^2.                 (4)

This follows by applying (3) first on the negative block and then on the
positive block. It does not make any assumption about their derivative
values between endpoints beyond monotonicity on each ray.

Since (2/c)^17<1/40, we have S>=eta b for r>=18. Also

    eta^2=169/64 > 13/5=c.

Therefore (4) is strictly smaller than

    c^ell/eta^2 < c^(ell-1) <= gamma^(ell-1).                (5)

Hypothesis 2 makes u_next/u an integer power of gamma. It follows that

    u_next/u <= gamma^(ell-2).                             (6)

This strict-integer step is the essential use of the geometric grid. Without
the grid, (5) alone does not imply (6).

Pair the checkpoints as (0,ell), leaving the final level-zero checkpoint
unpaired. The total exponent in (6) is

    sum_(ell=1)^(J-1) (ell-2)2^(J-ell-1)=1-J.               (7)

For example this sum is empty and zero for J=1. In general it follows by
subtracting twice sum 2^(J-ell-1)=2^(J-1)-1 from
sum ell 2^(J-ell-1)=2^J-J-1. Thus if u_first and u_last denote the positive
gradient magnitudes at the first and last checkpoints,

    u_last <= gamma^(1-J) u_first.                         (8)

The first H_r gives x_0>=(S+1)u_first. After the last checkpoint the negative
landing radius A is at most (b-1)u_last. The remaining H_r stays on that ray.
Let w be its terminal gradient magnitude and R its terminal radius. Then
R+S w<=A. Integrating the globally fixed derivative on this ray gives

    f(x_n)<=Rw-w^2/2,
    2f(x_n)<=2A w-(2S+1)w^2<=A^2/(2S+1).                  (9)

Indeed the derivative magnitude at radius t<=R is at most min(t,w). This
includes all terminal area and keeps the minimizer fixed at zero. Combining
(8)-(9) proves the first bound in (1). The second follows from S>=eta b.

Since J>=1 and gamma>=c, gamma^(2-2J)<=c^(2-2J). Substituting b=a c^r
and m=r+J bounds (1) by

    [1/(2 eta^3 a)] c^(-m) c^(2-J)
       <=[c/(2 eta^3 a)]c^(-m),

which proves (2). The proof applies even when r and all profile parameters
change with m. No finite-parameter compactness claim is being substituted.

## 3. Why a homothetic staircase relay necessarily enters this obstruction

The proposed self-similar positive-ray profile uses plateau heights

    M_z=theta gamma^z

at radial intervals [ell_0 M_z,u_0 M_z], where 1<=ell_0<=u_0. Adjacent
plateaus can be connected by a nondecreasing 1-Lipschitz derivative only if

    gamma ell_0-u_0 >= gamma-1.                            (10)

Equality corresponds to a slope-one connecting ramp; strict inequality
allows gentler or nonconstant connecting slopes. Together with an arbitrary
legal negative-ray derivative this defines a globally fixed convex smooth
function. The infinite staircase extends continuously to zero; alternatively
one can retain the finitely many relevant levels and attach a legal inner
core and outer cap before the run. All endpoints and slopes must be fixed.

Consider the proposed relay in which each positive-ray H_r block remains on
one of these plateaus, and every displayed checkpoint crosses. Its motion
during such a block has radial length S M_z. Therefore the plateau width
must satisfy

    u_0-ell_0 >= S.

Together with (10), this forces

    (gamma-1)(ell_0-1)>=S.                                 (11)

At the first, level-zero checkpoint, the departure radius is at least
ell_0 M_z. A strict crossing with step b requires ell_0<b. Hence

    gamma > 1+S/(b-1) > 1+eta =21/8 > c.                   (12)

The same positive profile therefore satisfies the grid hypothesis of
Section 1 automatically. Its negative ray and every negative H_r block may
be arbitrary subject to the stated sign pattern. The construction may use
an increasing number of plateaus, may repeatedly revisit them, may have
different curvatures between them, and may change all its parameters with
the horizon. It is nevertheless bounded by (1)-(2).

This is a concrete failure of the intended multiscale plateau relay: the
geometric spacing needed to accommodate an entire ordinary block forces a
full gradient-level loss at the frequent low checkpoints. The rarer high
checkpoints cannot compensate; their exact total exponent is 1-J.

## 4. Exact-profile comparison and boundary of the result

Here is a self-contained sufficient benchmark estimate. For the exact DP set
C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and D_n=2S_bullet(n)+1. Their joins are

    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,
    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

The inequality Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) reduces to
t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1. With x=t^(1/3) and
y=(1-t)^(1/3), its difference from 1 is
x^3 y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0: each reciprocal is at least 1/3
and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Since
G(A,D)<=D+4A and u^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3), induction in the dominant
DP gives D_n<=1+4n^(4/3). Now c^3>16, so multiplying (2) by D_(2^m-1)
proves that the normalized exact-profile ratio tends to zero.

The theorem does NOT cover arbitrary geometric-gradient profiles when their
trajectory skips one of the prescribed crossings, crosses inside H_r, or
has positive checkpoint gradients outside the stated grid. It does NOT
exclude general scalar functions, and it does NOT bound multidimensional W.
In particular the already shared coupled-coordinate separation is compatible
with it.

A next scalar construction must evade the explicit lost-grid-level step:
for example by traversing several knots inside the positive H_r blocks with
dense gradient levels, or by altering the coarse crossing pattern. Merely
adding more homothetic plateau levels does not evade the proof.

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000744 -->
# A quantitative obstruction to chronological-window gluing

Status: complete explicit candidate proof; not yet verifier-accepted.
Project: gd-exact-profile-20260926. Worker: max3.

## Exact scope

An exact conditional completion state for smooth-convex GD histories cannot
consist only of the initial jet, minimizer, prescribed schedule, every past point
and gradient, and any fixed-length chronological window of function values.
There are two legal histories with identical such retained data but conditional
terminal objective maxima differing by a factor of 16. The missing information
in this example is exactly one old function value.

This does not exclude other finite-dimensional state representations, including
states that encode the missing interpolation constraint. It does not determine
the unrestricted worst case of the displayed schedule, nor the minimax target.

## Explicit functions and legal histories

Normalize L=1, x_star=0, and f_star=0. Define

\[
F(x)=\begin{cases}
x^2/2,&x\leq0,\\
0,&0\leq x\leq1,\\
(x-1)^2/2,&1\leq x\leq3,\\
2x-4,&x\geq3.
\end{cases}
\]

Let G(x)=integral from 0 to x of q(t) dt, where

\[
q(t)=\begin{cases}
t,&t\leq1/2,\\
1/2,&1/2\leq t\leq11/6,\\
t-4/3,&11/6\leq t\leq10/3,\\
2,&t\geq10/3.
\end{cases}
\]

The derivatives of F and G are continuous and nondecreasing and have slopes
only 0 or 1. They are globally 1-Lipschitz. Both functions are differentiable,
convex, and have a minimizer at 0 with value 0. The functions coincide on the
negative half-line.

At 4, both functions have value 4 and derivative 2. For G, direct integration
gives G(11/6)=19/24, G(10/3)=19/24+15/8=8/3, and G(4)=4.
At p=3/2 their common derivative is 1/2, but

\[
F(p)=1/8,\qquad G(p)=5/8.
\]

Fix an integer m>=1. With x_0=4 use the predetermined schedule

\[
h_0=5/4,\quad h_1=5,\quad
h_{2+j}=1/2\quad(0\leq j<m),\quad
h_{m+2}=1+(5/4)2^m.
\]

All m+3 steps are strictly positive and finite. Under either function the
prefix iterates and derivatives coincide:

\[
x_1=4-(5/4)2=3/2,
\quad x_2=3/2-5(1/2)=-1,
\quad x_{2+j}=-2^{-j}\quad(0\leq j\leq m).
\]

For the negative tail, the derivative equals the coordinate, so each step
1/2 halves the coordinate. At the cut k=m+2 the common point and derivative
are -2^{-m}. The last step takes both trajectories to

\[
y=-2^{-m}+[1+(5/4)2^m]2^{-m}=5/4.
\]

All sampled prefix function values coincide except the value at x_1=p.
For any prescribed finite chronological window choose m long enough that p
lies outside it. This gives identical retained data even if every sampled
point and gradient, the initial jet, minimizer, radius, and full schedule are
also retained. No repeated points or zero steps are needed.

## Exact conditional terminal maxima

A completion of a prefix means any differentiable convex function on R with
1-Lipschitz derivative that interpolates every point, value and derivative in
that prefix and has f(0)=0 and f'(0)=0. Its final query point is y=5/4 because
the cut data and last step are fixed.

For an F-prefix completion f, smoothness gives

\[
f'(t)\geq f'(p)-(p-t)=t-1\qquad(y\leq t\leq p).
\]

Integrating gives

\[
f(y)=f(p)-\int_y^p f'(t)\,dt
\leq\frac18-\int_{5/4}^{3/2}(t-1)\,dt
=\frac18-\frac3{32}=\frac1{32}.
\]

F attains this bound, hence the conditional maximum is 1/32.

For a G-prefix completion f, smoothness from 0 and monotonicity up to p give

\[
f'(t)\leq\min\{t,1/2\}\qquad(0\leq t\leq p).
\]

The integral of the right side equals 1/8+1/2=5/8, exactly f(p)-f(0).
The continuous nonnegative difference between this upper envelope and f'
therefore has integral zero and is identically zero. Consequently

\[
f(y)=\int_0^{5/4}\min\{t,1/2\}\,dt
=\frac18+\frac12\left(\frac54-\frac12\right)=\frac12.
\]

G is a completion, so the second conditional maximum is exactly 1/2.
Their ratio is 16. Since |x_0-x_star|^2=16, the normalized conditional
objective values 2(f(y)-f_star)/|x_0-x_star|^2 are 1/256 and 1/16.

## Consequence for a split rule

Take the full F prefix and the G continuation. The continuation is legal and
agrees with every retained datum listed above, but no single legal function can
realize their union: the first conditional bound forbids the continuation's
value 1/2 at y. Thus matching those retained data is not a lossless gluing
criterion. Any proposed exact state compression must retain the old value's
constraint, or prove an equivalent replacement. This construction does not
preclude a different compressed state or an argument at the unconditional
maximized value level.

<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000744 -->

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000745 -->
# Two-cut inner returns and an exact rejection of the paired four-copy seed

Status: complete explicit candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No additional
verification allocation is assumed. This refutes a proposed primitive schedule;
it is not a negative or positive resolution of the unrestricted minimax target.

## 1. Exact legal inner-return construction

Partition any nonnegative schedule chronologically into

    (block_0, b_0, block_1, b_1, block_2),

with ordinary block sums s_0,s_1,s_2. Choose numbers 0<w<=1 and R>=w.
Put

    r_outer=R+1-w,
    A=b_0-r_outer=b_0-1-R+w,
    v=A/(1+s_1),
    z=(b_1-1)v.

Require A>0 and

    (1+s_2)w <= z <= R.                                    (1)

The following SINGLE derivative is fixed before running the schedule:

    g(x)=-v                     for x<=-v,
    g(x)=x                      for -v<=x<=w,
    g(x)=w                      for w<=x<=R,
    g(x)=x-R+w                  for R<=x<=r_outer,
    g(x)=1                      for x>=r_outer.             (2)

It is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz: the successive slopes are
0,1,0,1,0 and endpoint values agree. Define f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt.
Then f is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth, and minimized at zero with f(0)=0.
Explicitly,

    f(x)=-v x-v^2/2                            for x<=-v,
    f(x)=x^2/2                                 for -v<=x<=w,
    f(x)=w x-w^2/2                             for w<=x<=R,
    f(x)=(x-R+w)^2/2+wR-w^2                    for R<=x<=r_outer,
    f(x)=x-r_outer+1/2+wR-w^2                   for x>=r_outer.

Start at x_0=s_0+r_outer. The entire first block has gradient1 and reaches
r_outer. The first checkpoint reaches -A. The middle block has gradient -v:
every partial sum is at most s_1, so it stays in [-A,-v] and ends at -v.
The second checkpoint reaches z. By (1), the final block stays on the inner
positive plateau [w,R], with gradient w, and ends at

    x_n=z-s_2 w >= w.

All individual steps, including zero steps, obey the supplied GD recursion.
The exact normalized terminal objective is

    2 f(x_n)/x_0^2
       = [2wz-(2s_2+1)w^2]/(s_0+R+1-w)^2.                 (3)

This differs from the verified outward-ray C6 construction: the second return
can have gradient w strictly smaller than the old positive gradient1. The old
positive plateau lies farther out. The connecting ramp and its area cannot be
discarded in a primitive-energy calculation.

## 2. Exact primitive energy, retaining the remote area

Let s=s_0+b_0+s_1+b_1+s_2. The saturated intrinsic primitive energy is

    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2-s(s+1)g_n^2.          (4)

A primitive schedule must make (4) nonnegative for every legal smooth convex
trajectory. Set Q_i=f_i-x_i g_i+g_i^2/2. Expanding
x_(i+1)^2=x_i^2-2h_i x_i g_i+h_i^2g_i^2 and summing gives the exact identity

    V_h=x_0^2-x_n^2-2s f_n-s(s+1)g_n^2+2sum_(i<n)h_i Q_i.

For all first-block samples and the first checkpoint query, (2) gives

    Q_i=-(1-w)(R-w).

For every remaining sample, including the terminal sample, Q_i=0, since its
ray is a constant extension of the unit quadratic core. Also g_n=w and
f_n=w x_n-w^2/2. Therefore

    V_h=(s_0+R+1-w)^2
          -[z+(s_0+b_0+s_1+b_1)w]^2
          -2(s_0+b_0)(1-w)(R-w).                          (5)

The last term is the contribution of the old positive area. It is negative
and is required by the exact function values. Formula (5), with constraints
(1), is a new necessary scalar test for any claimed primitive schedule.
It is not a sufficient certificate.

## 3. The actual eleven-step candidate is not primitive

The candidate in finding0bdb4dfb180b15a0 is

    h=(3/2,3/2,19/5,3/2,3/2,5,3/2,3/2,9/5,3/2,3/2).

Its sum is s=113/5 and shifted sum is118/5. Choose the two checkpoints19/5
and5, so s_0=s_1=3 and s_2=39/5. Set

    w=1/6,       R=89/60,       r_outer=139/60,
    v=89/240,    z=R,          x_0=319/60.

Here A=89/60=4v and (1+s_2)w=22/15=88/60<R, so all feasibility conditions
hold. The complete exact GD data are:

| i | x_i | g_i | f_i |
|---|---|---|---|
|0|319/60|1|1339/360|
|1|229/60|1|799/360|
|2|139/60|1|259/360|
|3|-89/60|-89/240|55447/115200|
|4|-89/96|-89/240|7921/28800|
|5|-89/240|-89/240|7921/115200|
|6|89/60|1/6|7/30|
|7|37/30|1/6|23/120|
|8|59/60|1/6|3/20|
|9|41/60|1/6|1/10|
|10|13/30|1/6|7/120|
|11|11/60|1/6|1/60|

Every row follows from (2) and x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i. In particular the first
two ordinary pairs use the outer positive and negative plateaus, while the
last five steps use the lower positive plateau. Direct substitution in (4),
or the shorter identity (5), gives

    V_h=(319/60)^2-(79/20)^2
          -2(34/5)(5/6)(79/60)
        = -508/225 < 0.                                    (6)

The literal primitive property of this eleven-step schedule is false. The
counterexample is to the actual paired seed, not merely to the generic join
rule with an empty seed. The shifted-sum advantage therefore cannot be used
in the conditional primitive-seed amplification argument.

The objective error of this particular instance is only
2f_11/x_0^2=120/319^2. Thus (6) must not be presented as a strong lower bound on
its worst-case objective or as a disproof of some different direct objective
certificate. It specifically blocks using this schedule as a primitive seed.

## 4. A compact symbolic slice explaining the failure

Keep s_0=s_1=3, b_0=19/5, b_1=5, s_2=39/5 and impose z=R. Then

    R=7/5+w/2,       0<w<=14/83.

Formula (5) becomes

    V_h=204/25-(112/5)w-(6016/25)w^2.

The rational choice w=1/6 lies in this interval and gives (6). This is an
analytic family, not a numerical sign inferred near a floating-point zero.
The finite rational calculation of all twelve rows was independently run as
an arithmetic sanity check; no SDP, sweep, or verifier call was used.

## 5. The same four-copy rule fails for every seed, not only the rational pair

Let a_seed be ANY finite nonnegative schedule with sum A-1, where A>=1.
Form

    J(a_seed)=[a_seed,1+(7/10)A,a_seed,1+A,
               a_seed,1+(1/5)A,a_seed].

Use the first two displayed bridges as the checkpoints in Section1; the last
bridge is part of the final constant-gradient block. Thus

    s_0=s_1=A-1,  s_2=(11/5)A-1,
    b_0=1+(7/10)A,  b_1=1+A.

Choose

    w=7/44,       R=7A/20+7/88,       v=R/A,       z=R.

We have R>=w, A_landing=b_0-1-R+w=R, and
(1+s_2)w=7A/20<R. These are exactly the hypotheses of Section1, regardless
of the internal steps of a_seed. Each copy used before or between checkpoints
stays on its assigned plateau because its partial sums are nonnegative and
at most its total. The last two copies and intervening bridge remain on the
lower positive plateau by the same argument.

For general bridge factors c and e with middle bridge1+A, this z=R slice has

    R=(cA+w)/2,
    V_J/A^2=1-c^2-2cw-[(3+c)^2+2(2+c)/A]w^2.              (7)

Substituting c=7/10 and the displayed w into (7) gives the exact expression

    V_J=-(2293/38720)A^2-(1323/9680)A < 0.                 (8)

Hence this particular four-copy rule cannot be repaired by choosing a
different primitive seed, a larger seed, or a seed with a different quadratic
product. No primitive property of the input seed was used in the refutation.
The obstruction concerns the exact rule with bridge factors7/10,1,1/5; it
does not cover all possible four-copy rules.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000746 -->
# Quantitative limit of the exact two-cut inner-return objective witness

Status: complete scoped candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No new verifier
call. This bounds the explicit two-cut construction, not the entire scalar
class and not the unrestricted smooth-convex worst case.

## 1. The construction being bounded

Take a schedule split as (prefix,b,middle,d,tail), with nonnegative ordinary
block sums P,M,T and b,d>1. Choose 0<w<=1 and R>=w. The fixed derivative is

    g(x)=-v                 when x<=-v,
    g(x)=x                  when -v<=x<=w,
    g(x)=w                  when w<=x<=R,
    g(x)=x-R+w              when R<=x<=R+1-w,
    g(x)=1                  when x>=R+1-w,

where v=(b-1-R+w)/(1+M)>0. Start at X=P+R+1-w, and put z=(d-1)v.
Require (1+T)w<=z<=R. As in the complete construction proof, every ordinary
block stays on its indicated plateau, the checkpoints cross the minimum,
and the normalized terminal objective is

    J=[2wz-(2T+1)w^2]/X^2.                                  (1)

All inequalities below use just these displayed definitions. In particular
no unverified result is imported as a proof premise. An overall scaling of
the gradient/radii is immaterial to (1).

Since X>=P+R, z<=R, and completing the square gives
2wz-(2T+1)w^2<=z^2/(2T+1), we have

    J <= [z/(P+R)]^2/(2T+1) <= 1/(2T+1).                    (2)

Also w<=1 and R>=w imply b-1-R+w<=b, so z<=(d-1)b/(1+M).
The strict crossing and z<=R moreover give R<b and z<b: the latter follows
from z<=min(R,(d-1)(b-R)/(1+M)), where w<=1 was used. Consequently

    J <=2z/X^2 <=2b/P^2                                    (3)

when P>0. Combining the first inequality in (2) with the preceding bound on z
gives

    J <= [b(d-1)/(P(1+M))]^2/(2T+1)                        (4)

when P>0. These are distinct valid relaxations; the w<=1 restriction in (3)
is essential near the end of the schedule.

## 2. Uniform bound on the recursive hostile family

Put c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, and

    H_0=(),  H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),
    s_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).

For m>=19 let B=b_(m-1) be the central, largest step. Write eta=13/8.
Since (2/c)^17<1/40, direct substitution gives s_u>=eta b_u for all u>=17.
Any eligible selected step greater than1 has level u>=18. An occurrence of
b_u has a complete H_u immediately before and immediately after it, so its
entire prefix and tail sums are at least eta b_u.

Consider ANY feasible pair of selected checkpoints in H_m and ANY feasible
w,R in Section1. There are three exhaustive cases.

1. The first selected checkpoint is at or after the central B-step. Then
P>=s_(m-1)>=eta B and b<=B. Equation (3) gives

    J <=2/(eta^2 B).

2. The second selected checkpoint is at or before the central B-step. Then
T>=s_(m-1)>=eta B. Equation (2) gives

    J <=1/(1+2eta B) <=1/(2eta B).

3. The first selected checkpoint is strictly before the central step and the
second is strictly after it. The middle sum contains the central step, hence
M>=B. Also P>=eta b, T>=eta d, and d<=B/c. Equation (4) yields

    J <=d^2/[eta^2 B^2(1+2eta d)]
       <=d/(2eta^3 B^2)
       <=1/(2eta^3 c B).

Each bound is at most2/(eta^2 B). Thus, uniformly over every feasible pair,
every threshold, and every choice of ordinary blocks within H_m,

    sup J <=2/(eta^2 a c^(m-1))
           =[2c/(eta^2 a)] c^(-m).                        (5)

This covers the arbitrary lower-positive-plateau height and the retained old
positive ramp/area in the exact C10 object. It does not cover more than two
checkpoints, visits inside nonconstant ordinary blocks, or arbitrary core
curvatures; no such generalization is asserted.

## 3. The exact benchmark comparison, with an elementary bound included

Let C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 for the exact project recursions.
Their shifted joins are

    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,
    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

We prove the sufficient rough bounds C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3) and
B_n<=1+4n^(4/3) directly here. For positive A,D put
Z=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3). The inequality Z>=Omega(A,D) is equivalent,
after normalizing A^(3/4)+D^(3/4), to

    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1,  0<=t<=1.

To prove it set x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3), so x^3+y^3=1. The difference
between the right and left sides equals

    x^3 y^3 [1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy].

Each reciprocal is at least1/3, and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3, so the bracket is
nonnegative. This proves the join inequality and, by induction in the exact
primitive recursion, C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3).

Next G(A,D)<=D+4A follows from sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A. In the dominant recursion
each left subtree has k+1 leaves for k steps and the right subtree has n-k-1
steps. Induction, together with u^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3), yields
B_n<=1+4n^(4/3). The empty-horizon initial values are C_0=B_0=1.

At n=2^m-1, (5) and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n imply

    (sup J)/U_(2^m-1)^ZJ
       <=[2c/(eta^2 a)] c^(-m) [1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0,

because c^3=2197/125>16. Therefore the exact two-cut inner-return objective
channel also cannot repair the recursive-family profile gap.

This does not undo its primitive-energy counterexample: the latter includes
weighted old-area residuals absent from the last-iterate objective. Nor does
it imply that H_m has a small unrestricted worst case; the coupled-coordinate
construction already shared provides a genuine divergent lower bound there.

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010-full-evidence.zip!/project/research/max3_multiscale_flow_obstruction.md

原始字节数：14895；原始 SHA-256：677a80ea03495e97af1caf8c66e5d9dfd88ac197aa1392e492abbef8e8c4ad45

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000747 -->
# A full log-periodic staircase class is controlled by scalar flow time

Status: complete scoped candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No new verifier
call. Unlike the earlier geometric-grid relay bound, this result puts no
restriction on the crossing pattern, knot visits, or gradients within ordinary
blocks. It covers the entire explicitly specified function class below, with
parameters and finite realizations allowed to depend on the horizon. It does
not cover every scalar convex smooth function or the unrestricted W_n.

## 1. The recursive schedules and a legal multiscale derivative

Set c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_u=a c^u, H_0=(), and

    H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),
    s_u=sum H_u=a(c^u-2^u)/(c-2).

Fix integers m>=r>=17, and write b=b_r, S=s_r, and J=m-r. Put eta=13/8.
Since (10/13)^3<1/2 and (10/13)^2<4/5,
(10/13)^17<1/40. Therefore S>=eta b.

Choose, independently for each ray sigma in {+,-}, a positive phase theta_sigma
and a parameter ell_sigma satisfying

    b <= ell_sigma <= 1+(S-log c)/(c-1).                     (1)

This interval is nonempty: at ell=b its associated expression
(c-1)(b-1)+log c is smaller than S, since
eta-(c-1)=1/40 and log c<1<c-1. Define

    u_sigma=c ell_sigma-(c-1),
    M_(sigma,z)=theta_sigma c^z,          z any integer.

On ray sigma, prescribe gradient magnitude G_sigma as follows. For each z,
let M=M_(sigma,z) and put

    G_sigma(R)=M,
       ell_sigma M <= R <= u_sigma M;

    G_sigma(R)=R-(u_sigma-1)M,
       u_sigma M <= R <= c ell_sigma M.                     (2)

The second interval is the ramp from gradient M to cM. Its outer endpoint is
the next plateau's inner endpoint. These intervals cover every positive R;
their endpoints agree. Set G_sigma(0)=0.

The profile is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz, with slopes zero
or one. It satisfies

    G_sigma(cR)=c G_sigma(R),
    0<=G_sigma(R)<=R/ell_sigma<=R/b.                        (3)

For the secant inequality on a ramp, write G=R-(u_sigma-1)M. The ratio G/R
increases to 1/ell_sigma at the ramp's outer endpoint; on a plateau the ratio
is at most its inner-endpoint value 1/ell_sigma. Thus (3) holds everywhere.
Continuity at zero follows from (3).

Define g(x)=G_+(x) for x>=0 and g(x)=-G_-(-x) for x<=0, and define

    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t) dt.

This is one globally fixed convex differentiable 1-smooth function, with
f(0)=0=min f. Across opposite rays, the Lipschitz inequality follows from
G_+(R)+G_-(T)<=R+T. The phases and parameters of the two rays need not agree.
There is no fixed quadratic neighborhood of zero; the knots accumulate there.

## 2. Exact areas and finite versions of the same trajectory

Write ell=ell_sigma and

    K_sigma=1/2+(ell-1)/(c+1).

At the inner endpoint ell M of any plateau,

    f(sigma ell M)=K_sigma M^2.                            (4)

All ramps below that endpoint together traverse gradient magnitudes from
zero to M and contribute M^2/2. The lower plateaus contribute

    (c-1)(ell-1)M^2 sum_(j>=1)c^(-2j)
       =[(ell-1)/(c+1)]M^2,

which proves (4). On the plateau itself,

    f(sigma R)=K_sigma M^2+M(R-ell M).                       (5)

On the following ramp, start with (5) at R=u_sigma M and add
[G_sigma(R)^2-M^2]/2. These are complete areas, including every inner level.
In particular f(sigma cR)=c^2 f(sigma R).

For any one finite nonzero trajectory, a finite-knot function can realize
exactly the same gradients and values. On each ray choose an inner plateau
endpoint ell M below every nonzero sampled radius. Replace the profile below
it by a zero segment up to

    delta=[c/(c+1)](ell-1)M,

then a slope-one ramp of length M, then a plateau at M up to ell M. The
endpoint gradient remains M, and the enclosed area is

    ell M^2-delta M-M^2/2=K_sigma M^2.

Thus all larger-radius gradients and values remain unchanged. Extend the
outermost needed plateau to a cap beyond all sampled radii. This produces
a globally legal finite-knot function and the identical full GD trajectory,
with the same fixed minimizer zero and the same initial-distance normalization.
The number of retained levels may grow with the horizon. This is a trajectory
preserving truncation; arbitrary unrelated core replacements are not claimed
to preserve the bound proved below.

## 3. Every ordinary block contracts by one complete radial period

For either ray, the exact flow time across one multiplicative period is

    integral_(R/c)^R dt/G_sigma(t)
        = tau_sigma :=(c-1)(ell_sigma-1)+log c.              (6)

It is independent of R. Indeed G_sigma(ct)=cG_sigma(t) makes this integral
periodic in log R with a full-period integration window. Evaluating from
ell_sigma M to c ell_sigma M gives plateau time u_sigma-ell_sigma and ramp
time log c, which proves (6). Condition (1) says tau_sigma<=S.

Every step of H_r is at most b/c. By (3), such a step h takes a nonzero
radius R to a strictly positive smaller radius R'=R-hG_sigma(R), with
R'>=(1-1/c)R. Therefore there are no sign crossings within any H_r block,
regardless of its starting radius or how many knots it traverses.

For each of its steps, monotonicity gives

    integral_(R')^R dt/G_sigma(t)
        >= (R-R')/G_sigma(R)=h.                             (7)

Add (7) across the full block. If its input and output radii are A and B,
the flow time integral from B to A is at least S>=tau_sigma. By (6) and
positivity of the integrand, B<=A/c. Hence every H_r, from every point on
either ray, satisfies

    |x_after H_r| <= |x_before H_r|/c.                      (8)

No endpoint is selected separately at successive steps. This is a bound
on every actual trajectory of the fixed function (2), and consequently on
its complete interval image as well.

## 4. Exact coarse-word bookkeeping and the objective bound

The exact decomposition of H_m has 2^J copies of H_r and 2^J-1 checkpoint
steps. A level k checkpoint has h=b c^k and occurs 2^(J-k-1) times,
for 0<=k<=J-1. At such a step, (3) gives

    |x-hg(x)| <= max(1,h/b-1)|x| <= c^k |x|.                (9)

This allows a crossing, a same-sign step, or a zero landing. No prescribed
crossing pattern is used. The total checkpoint exponent is

    sum_(k=0)^(J-1) k 2^(J-k-1)=2^J-J-1.

Combining every copy of (8) with every copy of (9) yields

    |x_(2^m-1)| <= c^(-J-1)|x_0|.                         (10)

When J=0 the formula is just (8); the empty sum convention is consistent.
Finally (3) implies f(x)<=x^2/(2b) by integration from the fixed minimizer.
Therefore every initial point and every function specified by (1)-(2) obey

    2f(x_(2^m-1))/|x_0|^2
        <= b^(-1)c^(-2J-2)
        = a^(-1)c^(-m-J-2)
        <= [1/(a c^2)]c^(-m).                             (11)

The bound is uniform in r, both phases, both admissible ell parameters and
the initial point. These choices can all depend on m. The finite trajectory
preserving versions of Section 2 have the identical objective ratio.

## 5. Comparison with the exact profile and the remaining gap

A self-contained rough exact-DP estimate suffices. For C_n=S_circle(n)+1
and D_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, the shifted joins are

    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,
    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

The inequality Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) is equivalent to
t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1. To prove it put
x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3). The difference from 1 is
x^3y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0, since each reciprocal is at least
1/3 and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). The estimate
G(A,D)<=D+4A and u^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3) give D_n<=1+4n^(4/3).
Since c^3>16, multiplying (11) by D_(2^m-1) proves that the supremum
objective ratio in this class, divided by U_(2^m-1)^ZJ, tends to zero.

The profile class has arbitrary numbers of knots, asymmetric ray phases,
multiple visits, and ordinary blocks that can traverse many knots. Its
limitation is instead its precise discrete homogeneity and the calibrated
flow-time/secant range (1). Functions outside that range, nonperiodic radial
geometry, and the complete scalar class remain open. A scalar class bound
does not upper-bound the unrestricted multidimensional W; the already shared
coupled-coordinate divergent example remains compatible with this result.

## 6. The calibration restriction can be removed for a common staircase parameter

The following extension covers EVERY ell>=1 in the profile (2), with
ell_+=ell_-=ell, while keeping theta_+ and theta_- completely independent.
There is no condition (1), no prescribed crossing pattern, and no restriction
on the initial point. The number of knots, phases, ell, and the finite
trajectory-preserving truncations can all depend on m.

For every m>=17 this larger class satisfies the uniform bound

    2f(x_(2^m-1))/|x_0|^2 <= (c^3/a)c^(-m).                (12)

It consequently also has vanishing ratio to the exact U_(2^m-1)^ZJ, by
the elementary benchmark estimate already proved in Section 5.

### 6.1 Choose the coarse scale and define the two flow clocks

Choose the smallest r>=17 for which b=b_r>=ell. Then b/c<ell<=b; the strict
left inequality also holds when ell=1 and r=17. Set S=s_r. Every step in H_r
is at most b/c, and hence preserves sign by the bound |g(x)|<=|x|/ell.

If m<r, the entire H_m preserves sign. Put w=|g(x_n)| and R=|x_n|. Then
R+s_m w<=|x_0|, while f(x_n)<=Rw-w^2/2. Completing the square gives

    2f(x_n)/|x_0|^2 <=1/(1+2s_m)
       <=[1/(2eta a)]c^(-m),

which is smaller than (12). Hence assume m>=r and put J=m-r.

Reflect the function if needed so that x_0>0. Multiplying a phase theta_sigma
by c does not change its derivative profile. We can therefore choose phase
representatives theta_+=theta and theta_-=q theta with 1<=q<c.

Let G be the template (2) with plateau heights c^z and common parameter ell,
and define its signed flow coordinate on R>0 by

    F(R)=integral_ell^R dt/G(t).

The integral is negative when R<ell. Put

    tau=(c-1)(ell-1)+log c,
    E=(c-1)-log c>0,
    F_+(R)=F(R/theta),       F_-(R)=F(R/(q theta)).

Then F_sigma(cR)=F_sigma(R)+tau. Each positive or negative H_r block reduces
its own flow clock by at least S, by the stepwise integral inequality (7).

### 6.2 Exact worst crossing-clock costs

Define

    D_+=F(b/q),       D_-=F(bq),
    delta=(D_++D_-)/2,
    v=(D_--D_+)/2,
    P_+(R)=F_+(R),    P_-(R)=F_-(R)+v.                    (13)

For a step h=b c^k that crosses from ray sigma to the other ray, its landing
radius is h G_sigma(R)-R<=h G_sigma(R). Its change in the unadjusted clock
is therefore at most

    k tau + F_other(b G_sigma(R))-F_sigma(R).              (14)

The final difference in (14) is maximized at an inner plateau endpoint.
On a plateau its first term is constant and its second increases with R,
so the difference decreases. On a unit-slope ramp, use the current gradient
u as coordinate; then dR/du=1 and its derivative is

    b/G_other(bu)-1/u >=(ell-1)/u>=0,

by the secant bound G_other(y)<=y/ell. Thus it increases on a ramp. At all
inner plateau endpoints on one ray the difference has the same value, by
the common radial period and common tau. That value is exactly D_+ or D_-
from (13). The additive constant v therefore makes BOTH crossing directions
have adjusted-clock change at most k tau+delta.

This same bound also holds for a checkpoint that does not cross. Its clock
decreases by at least h>=b, by (7). Since b/q>=ell/c and bq>=ell,

    delta>=-tau/2>-b,

where tau<(c-1)ell<2b. Consequently -b<=k tau+delta for every k>=0.
A zero landing has zero terminal objective and needs no logarithmic clock.
We have proved, for every nonzero trajectory and every checkpoint,

    change of P <= k tau+delta.                            (15)

### 6.3 Uniform bound on the phase-dependent cost

We now prove

    tau+delta <=(c-1)b<S.                                 (16)

This quantitative inequality is essential; it is not an assumed profile
closure. Put p=b/ell, so 1<=p<c. For 1<=z<=c, the template gradient is at
least 1 on [ell,ell z], whence

    F(ell z)<=ell(z-1),       tau=(c-1)ell-E.               (17)

Reduce p q and p/q by powers of c into z_1,z_2 in [1,c]. Let K be the sum
of those two powers. Then

    z_1 z_2=p^2/c^K,
    2delta<=K tau+ell(z_1+z_2-2).                          (18)

For z_1,z_2 in [1,c], their sum is at most 1+z_1z_2 when the product is
at most c, and at most c+(z_1z_2)/c when the product is at least c.
These follow respectively from (z_1-1)(z_2-1)>=0 and
(c-z_1)(c-z_2)>=0.

If 1<=p<=sqrt(c), the possible K are -1 and 0. Applying these two sum bounds
in (18) gives

    delta <=[ell(p^2-1)+E]/2.

Therefore

    tau+delta <=ell[p^2/2+c-3/2]-E/2 <=(c-1)b.

The last inequality is equivalent, after dropping the favorable E/2, to
(p-1)(p-(2c-3))<=0. It holds because sqrt(c)<2c-3 for c=13/5.

If sqrt(c)<=p<c, the possible K are 0 and 1. The same calculation gives

    delta <=ell[c-2+p^2/c]/2,
    tau+delta <=ell[3c/2-2+p^2/(2c)]-E <=(c-1)b.

For the last inequality, the difference divided by ell, before the favorable
-E term, is the convex quadratic
p^2/(2c)-(c-1)p+3c/2-2. At p=sqrt(c) it equals
(c-1)(3/2-sqrt(c))<0; at p=c it equals -(c-1)(c-2)<0.
Convexity therefore makes it nonpositive on the full interval. This proves
the first inequality in (16). The second follows from S>=eta b and
eta=13/8>c-1=8/5.

### 6.4 Complete clock recurrence and normalization

There are 2^J ordinary H_r blocks, the checkpoint level sum is
2^J-J-1, and the number of checkpoints is 2^J-1. Adding all actual clock
changes using (15) gives

    P_final-P_initial
       <=-2^J[S-tau-delta]-(J+1)tau-delta
       <=-(J+1)tau-delta.                                 (19)

All crossing histories and all ray changes are included. The two clocks
differ from a common reference by a controlled phase amount: since
q in [1,c), one has F_+(R)-tau<=F_-(R)<=F_+(R), and 0<=v<=tau.
The latter follows because D_--D_+=F(bq)-F(b/q)<=2tau.
Thus, for either final ray, P_final>=F_+(|x_n|)-tau, whereas
P_initial=F_+(|x_0|). Together with delta>=-tau/2, (19) yields

    F_+(|x_n|)-F_+(|x_0|) <=-(J-1/2)tau
                                  <=-(J-1)tau.

The clock is increasing and has period increment tau, so

    |x_n|<=c^(1-J)|x_0|.

This bound is deliberately loose when J=0, but remains valid. The global
secant bound gives f(x)<=x^2/(2ell), and b/c<ell implies

    2f(x_n)/|x_0|^2
        <=ell^(-1)c^(2-2J)
        <=b^(-1)c^(3-2J)
        =a^(-1)c^(-m)c^(3-J)
        <=(c^3/a)c^(-m),

which proves (12).

The remaining restriction is genuine discrete radial homogeneity with factor
c and a COMMON ell on both rays. Their phases are arbitrary. Section 1 also
allows different ell values in its stated calibrated range. These results
do not cover arbitrary independently scaled rays outside that range,
different radial periods, nonperiodic knot geometry, or the entire scalar
class. The full multidimensional exact-profile target remains unresolved.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000748 -->
# Arbitrarily many nested inward scalar returns remain too weak on H_m

Status: complete scoped candidate proofs, not verifier-accepted. No additional
verification call is allocated. The main result allows asymmetric functions,
arbitrary core curvature, nonconstant gradients between crossings, and any
number of crossings. Its essential restriction is the nested-return condition
defined below. It is not an upper bound on the full scalar or multidimensional
worst case.

## 1. A schedule-level obstruction for nested returns

Let f:R->R be differentiable and convex, with 1-Lipschitz derivative g,
f(0)=0=min f, and let x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t g(x_t) for a finite nonnegative schedule.
Write H=max_t h_t and S=sum_t h_t. Assume that some eta>=1 has the property

    sum_(t<j) h_t >= eta h_j and sum_(t>j) h_t >= eta h_j
    whenever h_j>1.                                             (1)

A nonzero terminal objective implies that the trajectory never hits a
minimizer. Reflect the line if needed so that x_0>0. List every strict crossing
of zero as checkpoint steps b_0,...,b_(k-1), and let block_i be the intervening
same-sign steps, with sum s_i. Let a_i be the absolute position at the start of
block_i, and r_i its absolute position at its end, before the next checkpoint;
set r_k=|x_n|. Thus a_0=x_0. Each block has nonincreasing absolute positions.

The nested-return hypothesis is exactly

    a_(i+2) <= r_i,                  0<=i<=k-2.                  (2)

Each re-entry to a ray therefore lands inside the interval traversed on its
previous visit. There is no assumption that the derivative is constant on a
block, that the two rays agree, or that their curvature is one.

For k>=1, conditions (1)-(2) imply

    2 f(x_n)/x_0^2 <= 2(1+1/eta)/H.                            (3)

For k=0, the sharper bound

    2 f(x_n)/x_0^2 <= 1/(1+2S)                                (4)

holds without (1). If the terminal objective is zero all these bounds hold
directly. Scaling f by L gives the same normalized statements for arbitrary
L>0 and steps h_t/L.

### Proof

On either ray, put G(r)=|g(plus-or-minus r)|. Convexity and smoothness give
0<=G(r)<=r and make G nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. A strict sign crossing
requires its step b>1. Let u be the positive gradient magnitude immediately
before the first crossing. The preceding block gives

    a_0 >= (1+s_0)u,
    a_1 = b_0 u-r_0 <= (b_0-1)u.

The first inequality follows since every earlier gradient magnitude is at
least u and r_0>=u. Since s_0 is the entire prefix before b_0, (1) gives

    a_1 <= [(b_0-1)/(1+s_0)]a_0 <= a_0/eta.                    (5)

By (2), every later point on the initial ray has radius <=a_0, and every
point on the opposite ray has radius <=a_1. This controls all iterates in the
fixed interval [-a_1,a_0], hence every single-step displacement by a_0+a_1.

Let w=|g(x_n)|>0 and let v be the gradient magnitude immediately before the
last crossing b=b_(k-1). All tail gradients are at least w, so the landing
radius on the final ray satisfies

    a_k >= (1+s_k)w.

Here r_k>=w accounts for the extra 1. The crossing itself gives

    a_k=bv-r_(k-1)<=(b-1)v.

The entire tail after this last crossing has sum s_k, and (1) therefore yields

    v >= [(1+s_k)/(b-1)]w >= eta w >= w.                       (6)

Nested returns and raywise monotonicity imply that every earlier gradient on
the final ray is at least w, while every earlier gradient on the other ray is
at least v. Thus every sampled gradient magnitude, including at the largest
step H, is at least w. At that step, (5) gives

    H w <= H |g(x_t)|=|x_(t+1)-x_t|
        <= a_0+a_1 <= (1+1/eta)a_0.                           (7)

Finally convexity gives f(x_n)<=|x_n|w<=a_0 w. Combining this with (7) proves
(3). Every value and gradient belongs to the same fixed function; no oracle
is chosen independently at different times.

For k=0, put r=|x_n| and w=|g(x_n)|. Then r+S w<=a_0. Since
G(u)<=min(u,w) for 0<=u<=r, integration gives f(x_n)<=rw-w^2/2. Consequently

    2f(x_n)<=2a_0 w-(2S+1)w^2<=a_0^2/(2S+1),

which proves (4). This also supplies a complete area calculation for that case.

## 2. Application to the recursive hostile schedules

Define c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_u=a c^u, H_0=(), and

    H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),
    N_m=2^m-1,
    s_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).

Put eta=13/8. Since (2/c)^17<1/40, we have

    s_u/b_u=[1-(2/c)^u]/(c-2)>=eta,       u>=17.               (8)

Every step greater than 1 in H_m is b_u for u>=18. Its occurrence has a
complete H_u immediately before it and a complete H_u immediately after it,
by the defining recursion. Thus its entire prefix and tail satisfy (1).
The largest step is B=b_(m-1). For m>=19, (3)-(4) give the uniform bound

    sup_(nested scalar trajectories) 2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2
       <= (42/13)/B = [42/(5a)]c^(-m).                       (9)

In the zero-crossing case use S>=B in (4), which is smaller than (9).
The supremum permits any asymmetric convex 1-smooth function and any initial
point whose full trajectory satisfies (2). The function and all its knots may
depend on the full schedule and on m; there is no fixed-function-across-horizons
restriction.

For completeness, the elementary exact-DP bound needed to compare (9) with
the target profile is proved here. With C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and
D_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, the shifted joins are

    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,
    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

One has Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3). After normalization and squaring
at the positive root, this is equivalent to

    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1,  0<=t<=1.

Set x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3). The difference from 1 is

    x^3 y^3 [1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy],

which is nonnegative because the reciprocal terms are each at least 1/3
and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction in the exact primitive DP gives
C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Also G(A,D)<=D+4A. The dominant recursion then gives
D_n<=1+4n^(4/3), using u^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3).

Since c^3>16 and U_n^ZJ=1/D_n, (9) implies

    sup_(nested scalar trajectories) [2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2]
             /U_(N_m)^ZJ ->0.                               (10)

This excludes arbitrarily many nested inward returns, not mixed returns
that revisit or extend a previously traversed radial interval. The shared
coupled-coordinate candidate d1d7cfb25e10bfc5 supplies a divergent lower
bound on the actual multidimensional W_(N_m)(H_m); there is no contradiction.
That separate candidate is not needed in the proof of (9)-(10).

## 3. An explicit fixed multi-knot family inside this obstruction

This construction records the proposed new inward mechanism and its complete
old-area terms. Split any nonnegative schedule into k checkpoints b_i>1 and
k+1 ordinary blocks of sums s_i. Choose e>=0, put d_i=s_i for i<k and
d_k=s_k+e, choose m_k>0, and define backwards

    m_i=[m_(i+1)+sum_(j=i+1)^k d_j m_j]/(b_i-1),
                                            i=k-1,...,0.       (11)

Require m_i>=m_(i+2) for 0<=i<=k-2. Put

    O_i=sum_(j>i, j congruent i modulo 2) d_j m_j,
    ell_i=m_i+O_i,     a_i=ell_i+d_i m_i.                     (12)

On the ray of parity i, prescribe a plateau of gradient magnitude m_i on
[ell_i,a_i]. Build each ray from its innermost plateau outward. Start with a
unit-curvature core up to its innermost m_i. Consecutive plateaus on this ray
are connected by a slope-one ramp, since

    ell_i-a_(i+2)=m_i-m_(i+2)>=0.                            (13)

Equal heights simply merge. Extend the outermost plateau to infinity; if
one ray has no prescribed plateau, a unit quadratic on that ray suffices.
These two profiles, with the negative ray assigned a negative derivative,
define one continuous, nondecreasing, globally 1-Lipschitz derivative.
Define f(x) by integrating it from zero. Then f is convex and 1-smooth,
with its fixed minimum f(0)=0.

Start at x_0=a_0. For i<k, the ordinary block moves from radius a_i to ell_i
with gradient magnitude m_i, regardless of its individual nonnegative steps.
The next checkpoint lands at radius a_(i+1), because (11)-(12) give

    b_i m_i-ell_i=a_(i+1).

The last ordinary block moves from (1+s_k+e)m_k to (1+e)m_k. Thus the exact
normalized terminal objective is

    J=(1+2e)m_k^2/a_0^2.                                    (14)

Equation (13) makes every return nested in the sense (2). Therefore all
choices of k, checkpoints, m_k, and feasible e in this construction on H_m
are already covered by the uniform bound (9). Increasing the knot count in
this particular construction cannot repair the missing profile comparison.

The retained old-area defect on plateau i is

    Q_i=f(x)-xg(x)+g(x)^2/2
       =-sum_(j>i, j congruent i modulo 2)
                      d_j m_j(m_i-m_j) =:-D_i.              (15)

To check (15), at the inner end ell_i=m_i+sum_j d_j m_j integrate the ray's
gradient magnitude over [0,ell_i]. All unit ramps together span gradient
magnitudes from 0 to m_i and contribute m_i^2/2 in gradient coordinates.
Every inner plateau j contributes its additional area d_j m_j^2. Thus
f(plus-or-minus ell_i)=m_i^2/2+sum_j d_j m_j^2. Subtract ell_i m_i and add
m_i^2/2 to obtain (15). Moving along plateau i leaves f-xg unchanged, so (15)
holds throughout.

Let S be the total sum of the actual schedule, including checkpoints but
excluding e. For the primitive energy used in the earlier two-cut test,

    V=2 sum_t h_t[f(x_t)-f(x_n)]
       +sum_t h_t(1-h_t)g(x_t)^2-S(S+1)g(x_n)^2,

the exact formula is

    V=a_0^2-(S+1+e)^2 m_k^2
          -2 sum_(i=0)^(k-1) (s_i+b_i)D_i.                 (16)

Indeed, substituting Q=f-xg+g^2/2 and telescoping
2h xg-h^2g^2=x^2-(x-hg)^2 gives
V=x_0^2-x_n^2-2S f(x_n)-S(S+1)m_k^2+2sum_t h_t Q_t.
The final plateau has Q_k=0, while |x_n|=(1+e)m_k and
f(x_n)=(1/2+e)m_k^2. These yield (16).

No old area is discarded. Formula (16) can still be useful for rejecting
primitive certificates, despite the objective limitation (9).

## 4. Next mechanism

The remaining scalar search must permit at least one genuine revisit or
outward re-entry, a_(i+2)>r_i. Merely adding nested inner plateaus, changing
their curvature, or allowing more ordinary gradient variation cannot avoid
(9). A mixed-return construction must preserve the derivative values and
integrals on the overlap with the already traversed interval; this is exactly
the compatibility condition absent from a timewise choice of gradients.

## DOC-000749 — archive_full_20261010/project/research/max3_one_cut_limit.md

# Exact single-cut Huber bound and its asymptotic insufficiency

Status: verifier-accepted as fact b98bb807d514e80d. Worker: max3.

## Statement

Let W_n(h) be the project's normalized worst-case objective error for GD with
a predetermined nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}). For 0<=j<n set
P_j=sum_{t<j}h_t and T_j=sum_{t>j}h_t. Define

\[
K_n(h)=\max\left\{\frac1{1+2\sum_t h_t},\quad
\max_{0\leq j<n}
\frac{(h_j-1)_+^2}{(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)}\right\},
\]

where a_+=max(a,0). For n=0 the inner maximum is omitted. Then
W_n(h)>=K_n(h) for every schedule, and each nonzero cut term is the exact
normalized error of an explicit one-dimensional asymmetric Huber function.

Let S_circle,S_bullet and U_n^ZJ be exactly the recursions and profile in the
problem statement. For the schedules ell^(n)_t=t+1 (0<=t<n), n>=2,

\[
K_n(\ell^{(n)})\leq\frac{16}{n^2},\qquad
\frac{K_n(\ell^{(n)})}{U_n^{ZJ}}
\leq\frac{16(1+4n^p)}{n^2}\longrightarrow0,
\quad p=\log_2 3<2.
\]

Thus the maximum of the global Huber bound and all these exact single-cut
witnesses cannot by itself prove a uniform lower bound asymptotic to the exact
benchmark. This excludes only the displayed functional, not richer witnesses,
several-checkpoint constructions, or the target lower bound itself.

## Legal single-cut witness

Scaling permits L=1. Fix j with b=h_j>1; put P=P_j,T=T_j,
c=1/(P+1), a=c(b-1)/(2T+1). Define f(0)=0 and
f'(x)=max(-a,min(c,x)). This derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and
1-Lipschitz. Hence f is differentiable, convex, 1-smooth, and minimized at0.
Start at x_0=1. Up to the checkpoint all gradients equal c: nonnegative steps
give x_i=1-c sum_{t<i}h_t>=1-cP=c for i<=j. Thus x_j=c, and the checkpoint
sends x_{j+1}=c(1-b)=-a(2T+1).

On the suffix all gradients equal -a. Every suffix partial sum is at most T,
so each iterate is at most -a(2T+1)+aT=-a(T+1)<=-a. Therefore
x_n=-a(T+1), f(x_n)=a^2(T+1/2), and

\[
2f(x_n)=a^2(2T+1)=\frac{(b-1)^2}{(P+1)^2(2T+1)}.
\]

The initial squared distance is1. Multiplying f by L proves the same normalized
statement for every L>0. Terms with h_j<=1 are zero and require no construction.
The global Huber term is proved in predecessor fact 0be8b54e0a33381c, proof
part 1, including zero and empty schedules. Taking a finite maximum preserves
the lower bound, because the worst-case function may depend on h and j.

## A crude exact-recursion bound sufficient for the separation

Set p=log_2 3, C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1.
The primitive recurrence becomes

\[
C_n=\max_{0\leq k<n}\Omega(C_k,C_{n-k-1}),\qquad
\Omega(a,b)=\frac{a+b+\sqrt{(a+b)^2+4ab}}2.
\]

The function Omega is increasing in both nonnegative arguments. When a>=b>=0,
the inequality sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab)<=a+3b follows by squaring, with the right
square exceeding the left by8b^2. Thus Omega(a,b)<=a+2b.
For real u>=v>0, put r=u/v>=1. The derivative of
(r+1)^p-r^p is p[(r+1)^(p-1)-r^(p-1)]>0, so
(r+1)^p-r^p>=2^p-1=2. Consequently
Omega(u^p,v^p)<=u^p+2v^p<=(u+v)^p.
Symmetry handles v>=u. Since C_0=1, induction on n using
u=k+1, v=n-k proves C_n<=(n+1)^p.

The dominant mass recurrence, obtained directly from psi and also displayed
in predecessor 0be8b54e0a33381c, is

\[
B_n=\max_{0\leq k<n}G(C_k,B_{n-k-1}),\qquad
G(a,d)=2a+\frac d2+\frac12\sqrt{d^2+8ad}.
\]

Since sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=d+4a for nonnegative a,d, G(a,d)<=d+4a.
Starting with B_0=1, induction and the primitive bound give, for m=n-k-1,

\[
G(C_k,B_m)\leq1+4m^p+4(k+1)^p\leq1+4n^p.
\]

The last inequality uses u^p+v^p<=(u+v)^p for p>=1 and nonnegative u,v;
this follows, when v>0, by the increasing function (r+1)^p-r^p being at
least its value1 at r=0, and is equality when v=0. Hence B_n<=1+4n^p.

## Linear schedule defeats the single-cut functional

For h_t=t+1, the total sum is n(n+1)/2, so the global Huber term is at most
1/n^2. At checkpoint j, P_j=j(j+1)/2 and (h_j-1)_+=j.
When j>=n/2,

\[
\frac{j^2}{(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)}
\leq\frac{4}{j^2}\leq\frac{16}{n^2}.
\]

When j<n/2, the integers satisfy j+1<=(n+1)/2 and j+2<=(n+3)/2. Thus

\[
1+2T_j=1+n(n+1)-(j+1)(j+2)\geq(3n^2+1)/4\geq n^2/2.
\]

Also 1+P_j>=j, so this cut term is at most2/n^2. This includes j=0,
where the numerator is zero. Combining the cases gives K_n<=16/n^2.
Because U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, the ratio is at most16(1+4n^p)/n^2 and tends to0.
All estimates are analytic and valid for every integer n>=2.

The schedules ell^(n) are only counterexamples to the strength of K_n. No
small upper bound on their actual W_n is asserted; they may be very unstable.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000750 -->
# A fixed quadratic core with an exact terminal affine cap

Status: complete new candidate derivation, unverified. Worker: max3.
This repairs one specific deficiency of the verified outward-ray construction:
the prefix may return inside a previously visited region arbitrarily many
times, because one fixed quadratic governs all those returns.

## Legal function and exact trajectory

Fix any n>=1 and nonnegative finite schedule h, any lambda in (0,1], a sign
sigma in {-1,+1}, and an index j in {0,...,n-1}. Define prefix polynomials

\[
p_0(\lambda)=1,\qquad p_t(\lambda)=\prod_{r<t}(1-h_r\lambda).
\]

Define

\[
R=\max\{0,\sigma p_t(\lambda):0\leq t\leq j\},\qquad
A=\sigma p_{j+1}(\lambda),\qquad T=\sum_{r>j}h_r.
\]

Assume A>0 and A>=(1+lambda T)R. Choose a cap radius

\[
r\in[R,A/(1+\lambda T)].
\]

The explicit function is

\[
f(x)=\begin{cases}
\lambda x^2/2,&\sigma x\leq r,\\
\lambda r\sigma x-\lambda r^2/2,&\sigma x\geq r.
\end{cases}
\tag{1}
\]

The two branches and their derivatives agree at sigma x=r. Its derivative
is continuous, nondecreasing, and lambda-Lipschitz, hence at most1-Lipschitz;
it is convex and differentiable and has minimum0 at0. These properties also
hold when r=0, in which case one side is flat. The entire function is fixed
before the GD run, after observing the predetermined schedule.

Start x_0=1. For every query through j, sigma p_t(lambda)<=r. An induction
therefore gives x_t=p_t(lambda) and f'(x_t)=lambda p_t(lambda) through that
prefix. All sign changes and inner returns in the prefix are legal without
resetting the function or dropping older values. The update at j gives
sigma x_{j+1}=A. On the suffix the derivative is the fixed value
sigma lambda r. Nonnegative steps and A>=(1+lambda T)r imply that every
suffix iterate stays in the capped region, since its signed coordinate is at
least A-lambda rT>=r. Thus

\[
\sigma x_n=A-\lambda rT,\qquad
2f(x_n)=2\lambda rA-\lambda(1+2\lambda T)r^2.
\tag{2}
\]

The initial squared distance to the minimizer is1. Multiplying (1) by L
gives the same normalized error for the original h_t/L steps at arbitrary
L>0. The best radius in the displayed interval is

\[
r_{\rm opt}=\max\{R,A/(1+2\lambda T)\}.
\tag{3}
\]

The right endpoint is never exceeded by (3), because T>=0. This gives a
fully explicit lower-bound functional after maximizing over feasible
(lambda,sigma,j). The record R is essential: choosing a smaller radius would
change the already used prefix function and invalidate the polynomial formula.

## A strict improvement beyond outward rays plus quadratics

Take the exact four-step schedule h=(8/5,8/5,13,1), choose lambda=1,
sigma=-1 and j=2. The core prefix is

\[
x_0=1,\quad x_1=-3/5,\quad x_2=9/25,\quad x_3=-108/25.
\]

Thus R=3/5, A=108/25, T=1 and r_opt=36/25. Formula (1) is simply the
unit-curvature quadratic above -36/25, continued affinely below that point.
The final step gives x_4=-72/25, and the normalized error is exactly

\[
2f(x_4)=\frac{A^2}{3}=\frac{3888}{625}.
\tag{4}
\]

This strictly exceeds the combination of the previously considered two
channels on this schedule, as the following exact comparisons establish.

### Exact outward-ray value

Use the functional F_ray_4 from verified fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a. Any feasible
chain with two or more checkpoints uses only the first three steps: a final
checkpoint equal to1 gives nonpositive distance budget and cannot satisfy the
positive old-slope requirement. Chains with zero or one checkpoint give

\[
K_4(h)=\max\{5/177,9/805,9/4901,400/147,0\}=400/147.
\]

For two checkpoints at indices(0,1), the terminal budget is9/25 whereas its
required lower bound is15, so it is infeasible. For indices(0,2), the verified
recurrence gives m_0=1,m_1=3/13,O_1=0,z_term=36/13,s_2=1,m_old=1.
Its optimizing q is1 and the payoff is33/13. For indices(1,2), it gives
m_0=5/13,m_1=3/13,O_1=8/13,z_term=28/13,s_2=1,m_old=5/13,
e_term=40/169; its optimum payoff is1024/507. The three-checkpoint choice
(0,1,2) has m_2=9/25<m_0=1 and is infeasible. This exhausts all eligible
sets. Both33/13 and1024/507 are less than400/147. Therefore

\[
F_{\rm ray,4}(h)=400/147.
\]

### A sufficient exact quadratic upper bound

For this h the full quadratic-class error is

\[
Q_4(h)=\max_{0\leq t\leq1}
t(1-8t/5)^4(1-13t)^2(1-t)^2.
\]

For 0<=t<=1, |(1-13t)(1-t)|<=36/13. On [0,1/13] the product is at
most1 in absolute value; on [1/13,1] its magnitude is the concave quadratic
(13t-1)(1-t), whose maximum36/13 is attained at t=7/13.
If t<=5/8 then |1-8t/5|<=1 and the displayed objective is at most
(5/8)(36/13)^2=810/169. If t>=5/8 then |1-8t/5|<=3/5 and the objective
is at most(3/5)^4(36/13)^2=104976/105625<1. Thus

\[
\max\{F_{\rm ray,4}(h),Q_4(h)\}\leq810/169
<3888/625.
\]

The last inequality is exact: their ratio is4056/3125>1. The new witness
therefore adds a real legal interaction beyond a maximum of the old channels.
This fixed-horizon improvement in a lower-bound family is not a resolution
of the asymptotic minimax problem.

## Remaining target obligation

No claim is made that the supremum of (2), even together with F_ray_n and Q_n,
matches the exact ZJ profile for every schedule. The specific new mechanism
is a legal quadratic prefix, including inner returns, followed by a nonlinear
continuation constrained by an exact remote-excursion record. A next extension
could allow a fixed two-curvature core or repeated cap exits and returns, but
must preserve the old record and values instead of independently resampling
curvature. Any uniform profile comparison still needs an actual proof.
