<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000751 -->
# Quantitative tests of the verified alternating-ray functional

Status: new analytic candidate findings based on verified fact
1fca3e5dd555cb4a; not independently verifier-accepted. Worker: max3.

## A precise obstruction to the ray functional alone

For every n>=1 define h_t=(t+1)/2 for 0<=t<n. Then the alternating-ray
functional F_ray_n of fact 1fca3e5dd555cb4a equals the single-cut functional
K_n: no selected chain with two or more checkpoints is feasible.
For n>=2,

\[
F_{\rm ray,n}(h)=K_n(h)\leq16/n^2,
\qquad
\frac{F_{\rm ray,n}(h)}{U_n^{ZJ}}
\leq16(1+4n^{\log_2 3})/n^2\longrightarrow0.
\]

This refutes the exact uniform-profile comparison for F_ray_n alone. It does
not refute the same comparison after adding quadratic witnesses, and it is
not a negative solution of the minimax problem.

### General first-return obstruction

For any selected first two checkpoints at original indices i<j, let
P=sum_{t<i}h_t and M=sum_{i<t<j}h_t. The first two magnitudes in the verified
recurrence, if the second is positive, are

\[
m_0=1/(1+P),\quad
m_1=(h_i-1)/[(1+P)(1+M)],\quad O_1=P/(1+P).
\]

The available distance after the second checkpoint, before dividing by the
next ordinary-block mass, is therefore

\[
(h_j-1)m_1-O_1
=\frac{(h_i-1)(h_j-1)/(1+M)-P}{1+P}.
\tag{1}
\]

Every feasible chain with at least two checkpoints must have both h_i>1
and h_j>1. If the second checkpoint is nonterminal, otherwise m_1 or m_2
would be nonpositive. If it is terminal, its feasible final magnitude must
be at least m_0>0, forcing the same strict positivity.

If

\[
(h_i-1)(h_j-1)\leq P(1+M),
\tag{2}
\]

then (1) is nonpositive. This contradicts either the required positive m_2
when more checkpoints follow, or the terminal lower bound
z_term>=(1+s_2)m_0>0 when these are the only two checkpoints.
Consequently, if (2) holds for every eligible pair i<j, all feasible chains
have at most one checkpoint.

### The half-linear schedule satisfies every pair inequality

For h_t=(t+1)/2, an eligible i has i>=2. Write j=i+1+d with integer d>=0.
Then P=i(i+1)/4, M>=d(i+2)/2, and

\[
4(h_i-1)(h_j-1)=(i-1)(i+d).
\]

On the other hand

\[
4P(1+M)\geq i(i+1)[1+d(i+2)/2].
\]

At d=0, the right side minus the left is2i>0. Its coefficient of d is
i(i+1)(i+2)/2-(i-1)>0 for i>=2. Thus (2) holds strictly for all eligible
pairs, proving F_ray_n=K_n.

### Uniform bound on K_n for this family

The total step sum is n(n+1)/4, so the global Huber term is at most2/n^2.
For a cut j write P_j=j(j+1)/4 and
T_j=[n(n+1)-(j+1)(j+2)]/4. If j>=n/2, then
(h_j-1)_+<=j/2 and P_j>=j^2/4, so the cut term is at most4/j^2<=16/n^2.
If j<n/2, the inequalities
j+1<=(n+1)/2 and j+2<=(n+3)/2 give

\[
1+2T_j\geq(3n^2+5)/8\geq n^2/4.
\]

Also (h_j-1)_+<=1+P_j. Thus this cut term is at most4/n^2. Combining
the cases proves the stated16/n^2 estimate. Verified fact b98bb807d514e80d
supplies the exact-recursion estimate 1/U_n^ZJ<=1+4n^(log_2 3), which gives
the vanishing comparison without importing an asymptotic numerical constant.

## What the obstruction says about the mechanism

The alternating-ray construction demands that the second crossing can return
beyond the already visited positive-ray record. Inequality (2) prevents even
that first return, regardless of any later selected checkpoints. A genuine
GD trajectory can first return inside its old range and later expand. The
verified construction does not describe that behavior. Quadratic witnesses
are one way to capture it; arbitrary inner returns would require a new legal
extension of the state, rather than deleting the ray-feasibility conditions.

No bound on the full W_n of the half-linear schedules is claimed. Their
quadratic behavior may be large, so this construction is not evidence against
the combined functional max(F_ray_n,Q_n), where Q_n is the exact quadratic
worst-case polynomial functional.

## A second exact necessary condition for any adjacent checkpoint pair

For consecutive selected checkpoints b_i,b_{i+1}, the verified recurrence and
O_{i+1}=O_i+s_i m_i imply that a feasible next same-ray magnitude at least
m_i requires

\[
(b_i-1)(b_{i+1}-1)\geq
(1+s_{i+1})(1+s_i+s_{i+2})
 +\frac{b_{i+1}+s_{i+1}}{m_i}O_i.
\tag{3}
\]

For a nonterminal next visit this follows by multiplying its recurrence by
1+s_{i+1}, substituting m_{i+1}, and using m_{i+2}>=m_i. For a terminal
next visit, replace m_{i+2} by its allowed upper endpoint z_term/(1+s_{i+2});
the same feasibility inequality follows. Every quantity retained on the right
is nonnegative. Equation (3) is a useful local obstruction, not a sufficient
condition for a complete legal chain and not a profile theorem.

<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000751 -->

## DOC-000752 — archive_full_20261010/project/research/max3_recursive_channel_obstruction.md

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000752 -->
# A recursive family defeats the combined ray, quadratic, and single-cap channels

Status: complete symbolic candidate, not verifier-accepted. Worker: max3.
No extra verifier allocation is assumed. The result bounds specified lower-bound
functionals from above. It does not bound the true smooth-convex worst case from
above and does not decide the project's minimax target.

## Statement and definitions

Put c=13/5 and a=c^(-17). Define the ordered lists H_0=() and

    H_m=(H_(m-1), a c^(m-1), H_(m-1)),   m>=1.

Their lengths are N_m=2^m-1. Write b_u=a c^u for the step at level u and
s_m=sum H_m. Directly from the recursion,

    s_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2),    s_(m+1)=2s_m+b_m.

F_ray is precisely the verified functional of fact 1fca3e5dd555cb4a, including
all its sign, remote-offset, old-slope, and retained-area constraints. K is the
functional of fact b98bb807d514e80d. Define the full quadratic functional by

    Q_n(h)=max_{0<=lambda<=1} lambda product_(t<n)(1-lambda h_t)^2.

For clarity, define also the algebraic single-cap functional F_cap_n. For each
lambda in (0,1], sigma in {-1,1}, and checkpoint j<n, put

    p_t=product_(v<t)(1-lambda h_v),
    R=max(0,sigma p_t:0<=t<=j), A=sigma p_(j+1), T=sum_(v>j)h_v.

Take the supremum of

    2 lambda r A - lambda(1+2 lambda T)r^2

over A>0 and radii R<=r<=A/(1+lambda T), and include zero. This is exactly
the candidate quadratic-core/terminal-cap family, but its upper bounds below
use only this displayed definition, not any unverified prior lemma. It is a
legal lower-bound family directly: integrate the derivative of lambda x^2/2
until sigma x=r and continue with constant derivative sigma lambda r beyond
that point. The record constraint preserves the quadratic prefix, while
A-lambda rT>=r preserves the affine suffix. The derivative is continuous,
nondecreasing, and lambda-Lipschitz; the minimum is zero at zero and x_0=1.

For every m>=18 the candidate conclusions are

    F_ray_(N_m)(H_m)=K_(N_m)(H_m)<=1/(1+s_(m-1))
                                      <=8/(5a c^m),
    Q_(N_m)(H_m)<=1/(a c^m),
    F_cap_(N_m)(H_m)<=729 c^13/(a c^m).

In addition the exact benchmark recursions satisfy

    S_circle(n)+1<=(n+1)^(4/3),
    B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1<=1+4n^(4/3).

Consequently, since c^3=2197/125>16,

    max(F_ray_(N_m), Q_(N_m), F_cap_(N_m))(H_m) / U_(N_m)^ZJ -> 0.

Thus even adding the one-terminal-cap extension to the verified ray functional
and all convex quadratic witnesses cannot give the desired uniform comparison.
The comparison fails along an explicit subsequence of horizons, which suffices
to refute a proposed comparison uniform over all horizons and all schedules.

## 1. Elementary scale estimates

Let d=2/c=10/13. Since d^3<1/2 and d^2<4/5,
d^17=d^15 d^2<1/40. Therefore for u>=17,

    s_u/b_u=(1-d^u)/(c-2)>=(39/40)/(3/5)=13/8.

Write eta=13/8. Then eta>1, eta^2=169/64>13/5=c.
Every step b_u>1 has u>=18, so this estimate applies to every eligible
checkpoint. At ineligible levels b_u<=1, the inequality s_u>=b_u-1 also
holds. Thus s_u>=b_u-1 for every level u, and s_u>=eta b_u whenever u>=17.

## 2. Every feasible ray chain has at most one checkpoint

The j-th step of H_m, indexed here by 1<=j<2^m, is b_u where u is the
largest integer for which 2^u divides j. Each such occurrence has an exact
copy of H_u immediately before it and an exact copy immediately after it.

Consider proposed first two checkpoints at indices i<j, with step levels
u and v. Both steps must exceed one for a feasible chain: otherwise the
verified first crossing or second return has nonpositive distance budget.
Let P be the sum before i, and M the sum strictly between i and j.
The verified recurrence gives the distance budget after the second crossing as

    [(b_u-1)(b_v-1)/(1+M)-P]/(1+P).

Any chain of at least two checkpoints needs this budget positive. If more
checkpoints follow, the next magnitude must be positive; if these are the
only two, the terminal old-slope constraint is at least m_0>0. It therefore
suffices to show P M >= b_u b_v, which is a stronger obstruction.

If u>=v, the copy of H_u before i yields P>=s_u. The copy of H_v before
j lies strictly after i, since j-i is a positive multiple of 2^v; hence
M>=s_v. Thus P M>=s_u s_v>=b_u b_v.

Suppose u<v. If i lies before the copy of H_v immediately preceding j,
then M>=s_v and P>=s_u, with the same conclusion. Otherwise i lies inside
that copy. Let P_loc be the sum in that copy strictly before i. The local
copies of H_u on each side of i give

    P_loc>=s_u, M>=s_u, P_loc+M=s_v-b_u, P>=P_loc.

For two nonnegative numbers with this fixed sum and each at least s_u,
their product is at least s_u(s_v-b_u-s_u). This follows by factoring
(P_loc-s_u)(M-s_u)>=0.

For fixed u, put D_v=s_u(s_v-b_u-s_u)-b_u b_v. At v=u+1,

    D_(u+1)=s_u^2-c b_u^2>=0.

For v>=u+1, using s_(v+1)-s_v=s_v+b_v and b_(v+1)=c b_v,

    D_(v+1)-D_v=s_u(s_v+b_v)-(c-1)b_u b_v
                  >=(3-c)b_u b_v>0,

because s_u>=b_u and s_v>=b_v. Thus D_v>=0 for all v>u. This proves the
pair obstruction and rules out every chain with two or more checkpoints.
No offset or retained-area term has been dropped from a feasible chain;
the exact first-return condition prevents such a chain from existing.

## 3. Bound on all remaining single cuts

At an occurrence of b_u, prefix and tail sums P,T satisfy

    P,T>=s_u, P+T=s_m-b_u.

Put V=s_m-b_u-s_u. Since b_u+s_u increases with u,

    V>=s_m-b_(m-1)-s_(m-1)=s_(m-1).

On P in [s_u,V], the logarithm of
(1+P)^2[1+2(s_m-b_u-P)] is concave. Its minimum is therefore at an
endpoint. If b_u>1, the two endpoint values for the cut ratio are bounded by

    (b_u-1)^2/[(1+s_u)^2(1+2V)] <=1/(1+2V),
    (b_u-1)^2/[(1+V)^2(1+2s_u)]
                               <=b_u/(1+V)^2.

Here s_u>=b_u-1. Also b_u<=b_(m-1)<=1+s_(m-1) and V>=s_(m-1),
so both ratios are at most 1/(1+s_(m-1)). Ineligible cuts give zero. The
global Huber term 1/(1+2s_m) obeys the same bound. Fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a
therefore gives F_ray=K<=1/(1+s_(m-1)). For m>=18 the scale estimate yields
1/(1+s_(m-1))<=c/(eta a c^m)=8/(5a c^m).

## 4. Exact quadratic-product damping

For lambda in (0,1], put z=a lambda. The whole-block polynomial is

    P_m(lambda)=product_(u=0)^(m-1)(1-b_u lambda)^(2^(m-u-1)).

Choose the integer k>=17 such that c^k z<=1<c^(k+1)z, and set
y=c^k z in (1/c,1]. If a factor is zero, the following whole-block bounds
are immediate. Otherwise, when m>k,

    log|P_m| / 2^(m-k-1)
       =sum_(j=-k)^(m-k-1) 2^(-j) log|1-c^j y|.

For j<0 use log(1-c^j y)<=-c^j y. For j>=1 use
log|1-c^j y|<=log(c^j y)=j log c+log y; these latter upper bounds are
nonnegative because c^j y>1. Extending only these nonnegative bounds to an
infinite series gives

    log|P_m| / 2^(m-k-1)
       <=-2y(1-d^k)/(c-2)+log[y(1-y)]+2log c
       <=-(13/4)y+log(1/4)+2log c
       <=-5/4+log(169/100)
       <=-5/4+69/100=-14/25<-1/2.

The endpoint y=1 gives a zero factor when m>k and was already handled.
We have proved

    |P_m(lambda)|<=exp(-2^(m-k-2))  if m>k.

If m<=k, every factor has b_u lambda<=1, so |P_m|<=1. In particular
|P_m(lambda)|<=1 for every m and lambda in [0,1].

If lambda<=1/(a c^m), the desired weighted bound follows immediately.
Otherwise m>k. Put r=m-k>=1. Then

    lambda |P_m|^2
       <=(1/(a c^m)) c^r exp(-2^(r-1))
       <=1/(a c^m).

The last inequality uses c<e, hence r log c<r<=2^(r-1). For example,
e>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3>13/5, so no decimal estimate is required. Taking the
maximum over lambda proves the Q bound. This is also the full quadratic
class: diagonalizing a positive semidefinite Hessian with spectrum in [0,1]
makes normalized terminal error a weighted average of these scalar errors.

## 5. Uniform prefix bound and the entire one-cap extension

Define R_u(lambda)=P_u(lambda)(1-b_u lambda), a prefix block of length2^u.
Every prefix of H_m of length ell, 0<=ell<=2^m-1, has polynomial equal to
the product of R_u over the nonzero binary digits of ell. This follows by
successively consuming its largest complete block (H_u,b_u), of length2^u;
the remaining list begins another copy of the same lower-level pattern.

For u<=k, all factors of R_u have b_v lambda<=1, so |R_u|<=1. For
u=k+r with integer r>=1, the damping estimate gives

    |R_u|<=c^r exp(-2^(r-2)).

For r>=4, 2^(r-2)>=r and c<e, so this bound is at most1. For r=1,2,3
it is less than3: for r=1 use c<3; for r=2,3 use e>8/3 and respectively
c^2/(8/3)<3 and c^3/(8/3)^2<3. There are therefore at most three factors
exceeding one in any prefix, each less than3. Every prefix satisfies

    |p_t(lambda)|<=27.

We also need the weighted bound

    lambda |R_u(lambda)|^2 <= c^12/(a c^u).

If lambda<=1/(a c^u), it follows from |R_u|<=1. Otherwise u=k+r with
r>=1, and the left side is at most

    (1/(a c^u)) c^(3r) exp(-2^(r-1)).

For r<=4 this is at most c^12/(a c^u). For r>=5, 2^(r-1)>=3r and
c<e make its extra factor at most1. This proves the bound for every u.

Now consider any feasible single-cap checkpoint j in H_m. Maximizing over
all real r, rather than only its legal interval, gives the rigorous upper bound

    2 lambda r A-lambda(1+2lambda T)r^2
                           <=lambda A^2/(1+2lambda T).

Let u=m-1, and consider the central step b_u. If j precedes it, the tail
contains b_u, so T>=b_u, while |A|<=27. The cap payoff is at most
729/(2b_u). If j is central or follows it, the prefix contains the complete
block (H_u,b_u), followed by a prefix of H_u. Hence |A|<=27|R_u| and
the cap payoff is at most lambda A^2<=729 c^12/(a c^u).
Both cases are bounded by 729 c^13/(a c^m). All signed-record and radius
feasibility constraints were retained in defining the family; enlarging the
radius domain here only makes this upper bound more permissive.

## 6. A sufficient bound on the exact benchmark recurrence

This part uses the exact phi/psi recursions in fact b98bb807d514e80d, not an
asymptotic decimal constant. Put C_n=S_circle(n)+1. Their shifted joins are

    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,
    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

The first joins C_k,C_(n-k-1), and the second joins C_k,B_(n-k-1).
Both are increasing in their nonnegative arguments.

For 0<=t<=1,

    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.

To prove this, put x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3). Then x^3+y^3=1 and

    1-x^4-y^4-x^4y^4
      =x^3y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0.

Each reciprocal is at least1/3, and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3; the latter strict
inequality follows by cubing, since1/4<8/27. Endpoints cause no division
problem. Since Omega is the positive root of X^2-(A+D)X-AD=0, this proves

    Omega(A,D)<=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3).

Induction in the exact primitive recursion now gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3).
Also sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A, hence G(A,D)<=D+4A. Induction in the exact
dominant recursion gives

    B_n<=1+4[(n-k-1)^(4/3)+(k+1)^(4/3)]<=1+4n^(4/3)

for every split, so it holds for the maximum. The base values are C_0=B_0=1.

Combining Sections2--5 with this recurrence bound gives

    max(F_ray,Q,F_cap)(H_m)/U_(N_m)^ZJ
      <=[729 c^13/a] c^(-m)[1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0,

because 2^(4/3)<c. This completes the candidate restricted-channel result.

## Structural consequence and next mechanism

This family has recursive large steps separated by substantial symmetric
blocks. Those blocks prevent the first outward return in the ray construction
and damp every quadratic scale; the prefix damping also defeats a lone terminal
cap. A next witness must permit nonlinear changes followed by additional
returns and further changes, or use a genuinely different multidimensional
interaction. This result does not establish that these schedules perform well
on that richer class. No conclusion about W_(N_m)(H_m) from above is made.

Literature retrieval: a Matlas search for the displayed recursive-product
claim returned unrelated results, none used. Every new estimate above is
proved in the text. Correctness status remains unverified.

## 7. The same family controls every two-curvature homogeneous objective

This extension is part of the same unverified candidate proof. It does not
assume correctness of a separate unverified finding. For u,v in (0,1], define

    f_(u,v)(x)=u x^2/2 for x>=0, and v x^2/2 for x<=0.

Its derivative is continuous, monotone, and 1-Lipschitz, including across zero;
zero is a minimizer. Write F_two_n(h) for the supremum of normalized terminal
error on this family with either initial point +1 or -1. The claim is

    F_two_(N_m)(H_m)<=c/(a c^m),   m>=18.

Scaling or reflecting the initial point adds no larger case, by positive
homogeneity and by allowing both initial signs. Random mixtures and separable
direct sums of such objectives cannot exceed their supremum, since their
normalized errors are convex combinations of component errors.

It suffices first to consider u<=v, with neither reciprocal equal to a step
level. The reverse order follows by reflecting the objective. The exceptional
parameter pairs follow by continuity: each finite GD update and final objective
is continuous in (u,v), even at a sign change or zero iterate. All bounds are
uniform in the parameters. The cases with a zero curvature follow by the same
limit if they are included in the definition.

Let I be the first integer level with b_I v>1, and J the first with b_J u>1.
Then 18<=I<=J. A whole block H_l maps each starting sign to a deterministic
ending sign and nonnegative magnitude. Represent these two magnitudes by the
columns of a two-state map. Before level I, both signs are preserved, and the
whole-block magnitudes are P_l(u),P_l(v), both positive. If m<=I, the two
trajectories are ordinary quadratics, already bounded by Section4.

### 7a. Different first-crossing levels: I<J

The step b_I crosses from the negative side to the positive side but leaves
the positive side positive. Thus H_(I+1) sends both initial signs to the
positive side. Its amplitudes, from positive and negative starts respectively,
are

    A_(I+1)=(1-b_I u)P_I(u)^2,
    D_(I+1)=(b_I v-1)P_I(u)P_I(v).

Every larger whole block also ends positive from both starting signs, because
the last copy of the preceding block has this property. Put

    r_0=D_(I+1)/A_(I+1)>0.

The ratio D_l/A_l stays equal to r_0. Indeed composition with the central
step yields

    (A_(l+1),D_(l+1))=(1-b_l u)(A_l^2,A_l D_l),  l<J,
    (A_(l+1),D_(l+1))=(b_l u-1)(A_l D_l,D_l^2),  l>=J.

In particular, for I+1<=l<=J,

    A_l=P_l(u),
    D_l=(b_I v-1)P_I(v) P_l(u)/[(1-b_I u)P_I(u)].

All factors of P_l(u) in this range are positive. Dividing by
(1-b_I u)P_I(u) removes a submultiset of its factors with total step mass
b_I+s_I. Since 1-t<=exp(-t), it follows that

    A_l<=exp(-u s_l),
    D_l<=exp(-u s_l) exp(u(s_I+b_I)-v s_I+log(b_I v-1)).

Set S=s_I/b_I>=eta>1, t_u=b_I u<1 and t_v=b_I v>1. The exponent in
the latter extra factor is at most

    (S+1)-S t_v+log(t_v-1)
       =1-S(t_v-1)+log(t_v-1)<=-log S<=0.

Here the maximum of log w-Sw over w>0 is -1-log S, found by differentiating.
Consequently both whole-block amplitudes obey exp(-u s_l) up to l=J.
Their common terminal side has curvature u, so for I< m<=J the error is

    <=u exp(-2u s_m)<=1/(2e s_m)<=1/(a c^m),

using s_m>=eta a c^m.

For l>=J, put E_l=max(A_l,D_l). The exact recurrence gives

    E_(l+1)<=E_l^2(b_l u-1),    E_J<=exp(-u s_J).

Let t=b_J u in (1,c) and m=J+r with r>=1. Expanding this scalar recursion,

    log E_m / 2^(r-1)
      <=-2u s_J+sum_(j=0)^(r-1)2^(-j)log(c^j t-1)
      <=-2eta t+2log c+2log t
      <=-2eta+2<-1.

The series bound uses log(c^j t-1)<=j log c+log t and sums the nonnegative
upper bounds to infinity. The last inequality uses log c<1 and the fact that
-2eta t+2log t decreases on t>=1 because eta>1. Thus

    u E_m^2<=u exp(-2^r)
       <=[c/(a c^m)] c^r exp(-2^r)<=c/(a c^m),

since u<c/(a c^J), log c<1, and r<=2^r. This covers I<J completely.

### 7b. Equal first-crossing levels: I=J

At level I both signs cross, so H_(I+1) interchanges the two starting signs.
Every larger block also interchanges them: an interchange, the central
interchange, and another interchange compose to an interchange. Let A_l be
the amplitude from a positive start and D_l the amplitude from a negative
start. Then

    A_(I+1)=(b_I u-1)P_I(u)P_I(v),
    D_(I+1)=(b_I v-1)P_I(u)P_I(v),
    A_(l+1)=A_l^2(b_l v-1),
    D_(l+1)=D_l^2(b_l u-1),     l>=I+1.

Put S=s_I/b_I>=eta, t_u=b_I u>1, t_v=b_I v>1. Both t_u,t_v are less
than c. All earlier factors are positive, so P_I(w)<=exp(-w s_I).
For m=I+r, r>=1, the exact recursion therefore implies

    log A_m / 2^(r-1)
      <=log(t_u-1)-S(t_u+t_v)
                   +sum_(j=1)^(r-1)2^(-j)log(c^j t_v-1)
      <=[-S t_u+log(t_u-1)]+[-S t_v+log t_v]+2log c.

For any t>1, -St+log(t-1)<=-S-1-log S. Also -St+log t<=-S, because
S>1. The displayed bound is consequently

    <=-2S-1-log S+2log c<=-2eta+1=-9/4<-2.

The identical estimate for D_m follows after interchanging u,v. Thus both
amplitudes are at most exp(-2^r). Their terminal curvatures are at most
v<c/(a c^I), giving normalized error at most

    [c/(a c^m)] c^r exp(-2^(r+1))<=c/(a c^m).

This proves the asserted two-curvature bound. In conjunction with Sections1--6,
the maximum of F_ray, Q, F_cap, and F_two still has ratio tending to zero
relative to the exact ZJ benchmark along N_m=2^m-1.

### Scope of the stronger exclusion

The proof controls a continuous two-parameter nonlinear family, with repeated
sign changes and inner returns. It does not control a derivative whose slope
changes more than once along either ray, nor a genuinely coupled multidimensional
function. Such changes remain concrete mechanisms to investigate. Six fixed
curvature pairs were checked using only forty compositions of a two-state map
(about0.1 seconds, no long trajectory stored); those checks suggested the proof
but are not used in it. No numerical sweep or asymptotic computation is evidence
for the claimed bound.

## 8. Arbitrary monotone continuations after a quadratic-gradient prefix

One can enlarge the single-cap class substantially without changing this
obstruction. Consider any legal scalar convex1-smooth function with minimizer0,
subtract its minimum so that f(0)=0, and take initial point1. Choose a checkpoint
j such that its sampled gradients through j
satisfy g_t=lambda p_t, where p_t=product_(v<t)(1-lambda h_v) and
lambda in(0,1]. Function values on the prefix need not equal quadratic values.
After step j, allow an arbitrary nonlinear continuation, with the sole
restriction that all subsequent iterates stay on the same side of zero. A
zero iterate makes every later iterate zero and can be discarded as trivial.
The supremum of terminal error over this enlarged class, for H_m, is at most

    1458 c^13/(a c^m),   m>=18.

Here is a direct proof, not an appeal to a prior unverified claim about the
continuation. Let A=|p_(j+1)| and T=sum_(t>j)h_t. Reflect the current side so
that its coordinates are positive. Convexity implies every later gradient is
nonnegative; the no-crossing assumption and nonnegative steps make the
coordinates and gradients nonincreasing. Let x_f and g_f be the final radius
and gradient magnitude. Then

    x_f+T g_f<=A,    0<=g_f<=x_f.

For a monotone1-Lipschitz derivative with endpoint value g_f and value zero
at the origin, its integral from0 to x_f is at most
x_f g_f-g_f^2/2. Indeed its pointwise upper bound there is min(r,g_f), whose
integral has that value. Consequently

    2f(x_f)<=2A g_f-(2T+1)g_f^2<=A^2/(2T+1).       (8.1)

Set u=m-1, so the central step is b_u. If j is at or before the central
step, then T>=s_u, while Section5 gives A<=27. Thus (8.1) is at most
729/(1+2s_u), below the asserted bound.

If j follows the central step, both p_j and p_(j+1) contain the complete
factor R_u and a prefix factor of H_u. Hence each has magnitude at most
27|R_u|. There are two cases.

If lambda h_j<=1, this checkpoint does not cross zero. Since every later
point is closer to zero on the same side as p_j, monotonicity of the global
derivative gives g_f<=lambda |p_j|. The integral bound, or just convexity,
then gives

    2f(x_f)<=2x_f g_f<=1458 lambda |R_u|^2
                               <=1458 c^13/(a c^m).

If lambda h_j>1, the checkpoint is a crossing. Its level v obeys b_v>1,
and its following exact copy of H_v is included in the suffix. Therefore
T>=s_v>=eta b_v>eta/lambda. Combining this with (8.1) yields

    2f(x_f)<=729|R_u|^2/(1+2T)
             <=[729/(2eta)]lambda |R_u|^2
             <=[729/(2eta)]c^13/(a c^m).

This proves the stated bound. It applies to every monotone continuation
consistent with all old gradient data, including many changes of curvature and
interior interpolation gaps; neither a constant tail nor a preserved quadratic
prefix value was assumed. It makes no assertion about a continuation with
further sign crossings. Thus adding arbitrary curvature changes on one final
ray does not repair the earlier restricted constructions on this family. The
new scalar mechanism must permit nonlinear changes followed by additional
crossings of the minimizer.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000753 -->
# Coupled-coordinate instability on the recursive screening family

Status: unverified source-based derivation, not a new accepted fact.
Worker: max3. This is a companion to max3_recursive_channel_obstruction.md;
it is not a negative answer to the minimax target.

## Statement

Define c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_r=a c^r and
H_0=(), H_m=(H_(m-1),b_(m-1),H_(m-1)). Its length is N_m=2^m-1 and
its sum is s_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).

There is a fixed integer r, independent of m, for which the explicit
Ye--Liu coupled-coordinate construction yields

    W_(N_m)(H_m) >= (98/81)^(2^(m-r-3)) / [2(1+s_r)]

for all m>=r+4. In particular this genuine worst-case error tends to infinity,
even though the restricted ray, quadratic, one-cap, and two-curvature functionals
in the companion candidate tend to zero. Both claims remain unverified here.

## External input, definitions, and normalization

The source is Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for
Gradient Descent, arXiv:2609.09152v2, Section3.1, Lemmas3.1--3.2 and the
pre-weakening construction for Lemma2.2; its proof is AppendixA.1--A.3.
The PDF and extracted text are in this worker's downloads directory. The
definitions, the two-coordinate proof, the global assembly, and the terminal
objective calculation have been read. A Matlas search for the local transfer
statement returned unrelated results, none used. The direct source is
https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09152 .

The exact input used is as follows. For epsilon,gamma>0 put
chi=sqrt(1+2gamma+epsilon^2). There exist finite constants A_YL and B_YL,
depending only on those two parameters, such that for every finite nonnegative
schedule and every chosen chronological checkpoint set with all checkpoint
sizes z_i>=B_YL, a finite-dimensional convex 1-smooth function with a minimizer
at zero and initial point e_1 has normalized objective at least

    [1/(2(1+S_tail))] product_i
           [z_i/(chi(A_YL+S_i)+epsilon z_i)]^2.

Here S_i is the sum of ordinary steps after the previous checkpoint and before
z_i, and S_tail is the sum after the last checkpoint. The normalization is
W=2(f(x_N)-min f)/||x_0||^2. Section3.1 computes unnormalized final gap
D^2/[4(1+S_tail)] at initial squared distance1; multiplying by2 gives the
displayed coefficient. The paper then deliberately weakens its statement by
dropping that factor2. No normalization inference from the label R_n is used.

Applicability details: each local function is a translated/scaled unit Moreau
envelope of the support function of a finite convex hull of gradient vectors
and zero. Its gradient is Euclidean projection onto that convex hull, hence
it is convex and 1-smooth. The prescribed two-coordinate trajectories have
nonnegative first-coordinate descent and second-coordinate ascent. Two exact
zero regions ensure that old components and future components are inactive
throughout the full GD trajectory. The global objective is one half the sum of
these adjacent-coordinate functions and a final one-sided Huber term. Each
coordinate appears in at most two terms, giving global smoothness constant1;
the local steps are therefore h_t/2. All component offsets are nonnegative,
so the origin is a common minimizer. These checks preserve arbitrary finite
nonnegative steps and the entire checkpoint sequence. Dimension is the number
of checkpoints plus one, finite at every fixed horizon.

The paper permits B_YL=2(1+epsilon^(-2)) and sufficiently large finite A_YL.
Only finiteness and these hypotheses are used below; neither parameter is sent
to zero with m, and no constant is hidden in an asymptotic notation.

## Exact checkpoint calculation

Choose epsilon=1/100 and gamma=2099/20000, so chi=11/10. Fix any permitted
finite A_YL. Choose r large enough that

    b_r>=B_YL=20002,   chi A_YL/b_r<=1/100.

Such a fixed r exists since b_r=a c^r tends to infinity. In H_m choose every
step whose level is at least r. All ordinary blocks between consecutive selected
steps, as well as the first and last ordinary blocks, are exact copies of H_r.
Thus every S_i and S_tail equals s_r. Set M=m-r.

The selected step sizes are b_r c^ell, with level ell occurring
2^(M-ell-1) times for ell=0,...,M-1. Since

    s_r/b_r=(1-(2/c)^r)/(c-2)<=5/3,

the fixed normalized denominator satisfies

    d=chi(A_YL+s_r)/b_r<=1/100+11/6=553/300<37/20.

Every transfer is at least

    G_ell=c^ell/[37/20+c^ell/100].

This expression increases with ell. Direct rational comparisons give

    G_0>=1/2, G_1>=4/3, G_2>=7/2, G_3>=8,

and G_ell>=1 for every ell>=4. For M>=4, the total transfer product is
therefore at least

    [(1/2)^8 (4/3)^4 (7/2)^2 8]^(2^(M-4))
       =(98/81)^(2^(M-4)).

Squaring and multiplying the exact terminal coefficient from the source gives

    W_(N_m)(H_m)>=(98/81)^(2^(M-3))/[2(1+s_r)],

which is the claimed bound. All constants r,A_YL,s_r are fixed independently
of m. The exponent equals (N_m+1)/2^(r+3), so the lower bound grows
exponentially in the horizon, despite its very small fixed growth constant.

## What this identifies and what it does not

This is a strict separation of mechanisms on a concrete schedule family. The
coupled-coordinate witness can undergo many shrinking transfers and then grow
through larger checkpoints, without having to revisit the previously defined
one-dimensional ray. In the binary level counts the first four transfer types
already have product greater than one. The verified ray construction cannot
execute even its first outward return on the same family. A two-curvature
homogeneous core becomes a scalar contraction after its two signs collapse or
interchange; that is also insufficient on these schedules.

This calculation is not a constant/profile lower bound for arbitrary schedules
and is not intended to reproduce the known silver-exponent theorem. Its use is
to locate a real geometric difference that a stronger exact construction must
retain. The local additive price A_YL and checkpoint threshold B_YL remain
fixed costs of the cited mechanism; nothing here removes them or shows that
they are negligible at the desired leading-constant scale.

## DOC-000754 — archive_full_20261010/project/research/max3_scalar_interval_reachability.md

# Exact interval reachability for odd scalar gradients

Status: unverified construction and supporting calculations. No further verifier
submission is allocated. This is not the arbitrary-schedule profile theorem.

## 1. A legal scalar construction with an exact one-state recursion

Let g:[0,infinity)->[0,infinity) be nondecreasing, 1-Lipschitz, and g(0)=0.
Extend it oddly to the real line and define f(x)=integral_0^|x| g(r) dr.
The odd extension is nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz: for opposite signs use
g(r)+g(s)<=r+s. Thus f is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth, even, and minimized
at zero. It is fixed before the run.

For each nonnegative step h define

    M_h(R)=max_{0<=r<=R} |r-h g(r)|,       R>=0.

For a prescribed schedule h_0,...,h_(n-1), set

    R_(t+1)=M_(h_t)(R_t),                R_0>0.                 (1)

The set of all points reachable after t steps from the entire initial interval
[-R_0,R_0] is exactly [-R_t,R_t]. Indeed x->x-h g(x) is continuous and odd.
The image of a compact interval under a continuous real function is an interval;
oddness makes this interval symmetric, and its radius is the displayed maximum.
Apply this observation inductively. In particular some single initial point
x_0 in [-R_0,R_0] produces terminal point R_n. The point can be chosen after
the complete prescribed schedule is known, by successively choosing preimages.
It is not changed during the trajectory. If R_n>0, that initial point is nonzero.

Consequently

    W_n(h) >= 2 integral_0^R_n g(r) dr / R_0^2.                (2)

The possibly smaller actual initial distance only strengthens (2). No change of
normalization and no random/adaptive step choice is involved.

Taking the supremum in (2) over R_0>0 gives exactly the worst-case normalized
gap for this fixed f over all nonzero scalar initial points. One direction was
just proved. Conversely take R_0=|x_0| for any prescribed initial point; its
terminal absolute value is <=R_n and f is nondecreasing on the positive ray.
Taking also the supremum over g therefore gives exactly the full even-function
scalar adversary class, rather than merely a subfamily of its trajectories.
This equality does not identify its value or compare it to the project target.

The same idea is exact for every scalar convex 1-smooth function, without
symmetry, using two endpoints. Choose two nondecreasing 1-Lipschitz profiles
g_+,g_- on [0,infinity), both zero at zero; define f(x)=integral_0^x g_+(r)dr
for x>=0 and f(x)=integral_0^(-x)g_-(r)dr for x<=0. Its derivative on the
negative ray is -g_-(-x), and the cross-ray Lipschitz check is unchanged.
For each sign define

    D_h^sign(R)=max_(0<=r<=R)(r-h g_sign(r)),
    O_h^sign(R)=max_(0<=r<=R)(h g_sign(r)-r).

Starting from A_0=B_0=R_0, the exact reachable interval [-A_t,B_t] obeys

    B_(t+1)=max(D_h^+(B_t),O_h^-(A_t)),
    A_(t+1)=max(D_h^-(A_t),O_h^+(B_t)).                     (1a)

Both maxima are nonnegative because r=0 is allowed. Connectedness gives the
same exact reachability proof, and the terminal normalized witness is

    2 max(integral_0^B_n g_+, integral_0^A_n g_-)/R_0^2.     (2a)

Taking the supremum over R_0 and over the two fixed profiles gives exactly the
entire one-dimensional smooth-convex adversary class. This is still not an
optimization over the higher-dimensional class in the project target. The
state is Markov only after the whole function has been fixed: choosing new
profiles inconsistently after each radius update would invalidate the proof.

For a finite piecewise-linear g, every maximum in M_h is attained at R, zero,
or one of the gradient knots in [0,R], because r-hg(r) is affine between knots.
The retained terminal area is a sum of trapezoids plus the final partial piece.
This keeps a globally fixed legal derivative and all its areas. It permits
arbitrary repeated inner returns. The scalar state is exact here because it
represents a whole reachable interval, not a single point with forgotten data.

## 2. Symmetric Huber interval maps

For g(r)=min(r,1), direct maximization gives

    M_h(R)=|1-h|R                         if 0<=R<=1,
    M_h(R)=R-h                            if R>=1 and 0<=h<=1,
    M_h(R)=max(R-h,h-1)                   if R>=1 and h>=1.    (3)

All formulas agree at their common endpoints. M_h is continuous and
nondecreasing as a function of R. For h>=1, the equivalent formula is

    M_h(R)=max(R-h,(h-1)min(R,1)).

Write E(R)=R^2 for R<=1 and E(R)=2R-1 for R>=1. For a Huber derivative with
core curvature lambda in (0,1] and threshold radius r>0, the substitution x=ry
gives the unit-core maps (3) with steps lambda h. Its objective normalization
from initial interval radius rR is lambda E(R_n)/R^2. Thus optimizing the
threshold and the initial point can be done through the exact interval maps;
testing only one initial point of a fixed Huber function does not do this.

## 3. An exact full-class bound for the unit-curvature Huber subfamily

Put c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, and H_0=(), H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m).
Let s_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2). Write F_m for composition of the maps (3)
in the order H_m. Every threshold radius is allowed in this section, but the
core curvature is exactly one.

All steps in H_17 are less than one. Starting from R>=0, its interval radius
is nonincreasing. If its final radius is greater than one, every radius during
this block was greater than one, so F_17(R)=R-s_17. Conversely if R>s_17+1,
each partial sum is <=s_17, so all radii are >1 and the same formula holds.
Therefore, since M_1(R)=(R-1)_+,

    M_1(F_17(R))=(R-s_17-1)_+.

The following H_17 is radius-nonincreasing. Hence

    F_18(R) <= (R-v_18)_+,     v_18=1+s_17.                   (4)

For d=2/c=10/13, d^3<1/2 and d^2<4/5 imply d^17<1/40. Therefore
s_17/b_17=(1-d^17)/(c-2)>=13/8, and b_17=1. Thus v_18>=21/8>c=b_18.

Suppose F_m(R)<=(R-v_m)_+ and v_m>=b_m. For b>=1, equation (3) gives
M_b(y)<=max(y-b,b-1). Monotonicity and the inductive bound yield

    F_(m+1)(R)
      <= (M_(b_m)((R-v_m)_+)-v_m)_+
      <= (R-2v_m-b_m)_+,

because b_m-1-v_m<=0. Set v_(m+1)=2v_m+b_m. Since c<3,
v_(m+1)>=3b_m>=c b_m=b_(m+1), so the induction holds for all m>=18.
Explicitly v_m=s_m-2^(m-18)s_17.

For a unit-curvature symmetric Huber function with arbitrary threshold and
initial point, E(F_m(R))<=2(R-v_m)_+. Consequently

    sup_(R>0) E(F_m(R))/R^2 <= 1/(2v_m) <= 1/(2a c^m).       (5)

The scalar maximization uses max_(R>=v) 2(R-v)/R^2=1/(2v), attained at R=2v.
Thus all repeated-return trajectories of every unit-curvature symmetric Huber
function are controlled on H_m. This is not a bound for all core curvatures,
all scalar convex functions, or the unrestricted worst case.

## 4. A sharper quadratic-prefix calculation

The same H_m has every scalar quadratic prefix product bounded in absolute
value by one, uniformly in the curvature lambda in [0,1]. This strengthens an
earlier coarse bound of 27 in the recursive-family package, but is not used as
an accepted fact.

For lambda>0 choose k>=17 and y in (1/c,1] so that y=a lambda c^k. Let
P_m=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-a lambda c^j)^(2^(m-j-1)) and
R_u=P_u(1-a lambda c^u). Each ordered prefix is a product of R_u indexed by
the nonzero binary digits of its length. It suffices to bound every |R_u| by1.
For u<=k all relevant steps times lambda are <=1, so the assertion follows.

For u=k+r, r>=1, first bound whole blocks. Unless a factor is zero, put
d=2/c. Summing log(1-z)<=-z for negative scales and
log|1-c^j y|<=j log c+log y for positive scales gives

    log|P_u|/2^(u-k-1)
       <= -2y(1-d^k)/(c-2)+log[y(1-y)]+2log c
       <= -5/4+log(169/100)
       <= -14/25 < -1/2.

Thus |R_(k+r)|<=c^r exp(-2^(r-2)). For r>=4 this is <=1, since c<e and
2^(r-2)>=r. (The latter inequality holds at r=4 and is preserved by doubling.)

For r=1,2,3 put A=P_(k+1). Then |A|<=1-y, and all c^j y-1 for j>=1 are
nonnegative. Define L_j=(c^j-1)^2/(4c^j), the maximum of
(1-y)(c^j y-1) over real y. We have

    |R_(k+1)| <= L_1,
    |R_(k+2)| <= L_1 L_2,
    |R_(k+3)| <= L_1^2 L_2 L_3.

Here L_1=16/65<1/4, L_2=5184/4225<5/4, and
L_3=1073296/274625<4. The displayed three bounds are respectively <1/4,
<5/16, and <5/16. This proves the claim. Cases containing a zero factor are
immediate. Lambda=0 also gives prefix products equal to1.

In particular, for the unit-core maps with scaled steps lambda H_m, the entire
initial interval [-1,1] remains in the core during H_m, and its final radius is
|P_m(lambda)|. This justifies exact whole-block skipping in tiny interval-map
checks; it is not a computational proof of the all-curvature Huber bound.

## 5. Current boundary and failed simplifications

For arbitrary lambda, choose k so all steps of lambda H_k are <=1. Its radius
map obeys F_k(R)<=max(1,R-lambda s_k). If lambda s_j>=lambda b_j-2 at subsequent
levels, that inequality propagates with the exact sums: apply (3) between the
two copies and absorb the overshoot peak lambda b_j-1 into lambda s_j+1.
This bounds large input radii but leaves the small-radius objective ratio open.

A proposed two-line majorant max(qR,R-v) was not closed at full sum capacity.
The internal peak term q(b-1)min(F(R),1) survives composition; dropping it
incorrectly suggests faster contraction. In particular, at the input where
the first block reaches radius1 it produces q(b-1), and any new linear bound
must dominate that peak. This is the precise gap in the attempted simple
radius-potential induction, not an all-scalar stability theorem.

Tiny exploratory checks used three curvatures (.5,.75,.9), three initial radii
(10,100,10^6), and four depths (20,26,34,44). Whole-core and whole-outside blocks
were evaluated by their formulas; the largest call count was155, and times
were below .001 seconds per case. Logarithmic radii avoided underflow. Every
fixed tested interval eventually contracted very sharply. These 36 values
give neither a uniform-in-curvature nor a uniform-in-radius theorem.

The next construction problem is to choose a fixed multi-knot monotone gradient
for (1) with a quantitatively large terminal area. The interval formulation
removes chronology from the reachable-state description, but it does not remove
the global monotonicity/Lipschitz restrictions on the derivative. No comparison
of its optimized value with the exact ZJ benchmark has been proved.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000755 -->
# Bounded nonbasic primitive-seed discovery: completed at the authorized cap

Status: numerical discovery only, no improved seed found, no nonexistence theorem. The exact reversed-seed counterexample in Section3 is self-contained but unverified.

1. Model and calibration.
The code max4_primitive_pep.py minimizes the saturated intrinsic primitive energy
V_h=2 sum_i h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_n||^2
with s=sum h. It keeps the full gradient Gram matrix G positive semidefinite, trace(G)=1, f_0=0, and every ordered smooth-convex sample inequality Q_ij<=0. Thus it tests the full intrinsic primitive condition, not a quadratic-only or Huber-only relaxation. It records all primal data, all nonnegative interpolation multipliers, the dual PSD slack, and residuals.

The runtime is isolated in workers/max4/.venv-pep. Installed versions: cvxpy1.9.3, clarabel0.11.1, numpy2.5.3, scipy1.18.1. Each solve used one thread, max_iter150, time_limit10 seconds, and requested gap/feasibility tolerances1e-9. No core or verifier code was changed.

Six initial evaluations included known primitive blocks [sqrt(2)], [3/2,3/2], exact DP length3 and length7, and the two orderings of (17/10,2,6/5). The known primitive objectives were between -2.3e-9 and -1.7e-10, consistent with their exact zero optimum. The forward seed returned a negative value and the reversed seed returned a negative value. These calibration signs match the symbolic witnesses, rather than being counted as new mathematical conclusions.

2. Scope and complete budget.
The experiment then ran two local continuous SLSQP probes, of lengths3 and7, with all steps independently variable and constrained only to be nonnegative and to have shifted sum 1.002 times the corresponding exact-DP shifted mass (evaluated in floating point for discovery). Neither symmetry nor a recursive grammar was imposed. The starting points were small nonsymmetric perturbations of the DP blocks. The outer objective was minus the minimum primitive energy; its sensitivity used the computed primal and dual solutions. This is a local, nonsmooth numerical search, not a global optimization proof.

Total evaluations:60, including six calibration/candidate evaluations,24 local evaluations at length3 and30 at length7. The persistent counter in max4_pep_budget.json blocks further calls. Accumulated measured solve/build time was1.890137771 seconds. There were50 solver statuses `optimal` and10 `optimal_inaccurate`; no inaccurate output was promoted to a certificate.

Best sampled minimum energy at length3 was about -0.03623216044 (evaluation28), with h approximately
(1.4184351724,2.0071236092,1.4145251974).
Best sampled minimum energy at length7 was about -0.06114950110 (evaluation59), with h approximately
(1.4221824349,2.0110133066,1.4173278397,3.4184527329,1.4143778864,2.0014068997,1.4144488473).
Both were negative by amounts far larger than the recorded numerical residuals, and no credible improved primitive certificate appeared. The search stopped at the exact authorized evaluation cap. It did not test all nonbasic seeds, other initializations, lengths4 or5, or other target gains.

Across all60 evaluations, the largest positive interpolation residual was6.85e-8 and the most negative computed dual slack eigenvalue was-6.54e-7 (including inaccurate outputs). At the two best local samples, interpolation residuals were below4.3e-11; their slack eigenvalue minima were about-7.46e-11 and-1.36e-8. All full data and solver statuses are in max4_pep_evaluations.jsonl; the two local summaries are in max4_pep_local_summary.json. No exact feasibility or infeasibility assertion is inferred from these floating-point residuals.

3. Exact reconstruction for the reversed rational seed.
The rank-one numerical witness for h=(6/5,2,17/10) suggested gradients proportional to (1,1,-1,7/10). This was reconstructed without solver tolerances as follows.
Define f(y)=y^2/2 for y<=1 and f(y)=y-1/2 for y>=1. Its derivative min(y,1) is continuous, monotone and1-Lipschitz, so f is convex and1-smooth, with minimizer0. Starting at y_0=11/5, the three steps give exactly
y_1=1, y_2=-1, y_3=7/10,
with gradients1,1,-1,7/10. At all four points Q_(i,star)=0. With s=49/10 and R=59/10, the intrinsic primitive energy is therefore
V_h=(11/5)^2-(59/10)^2(7/10)^2=-122169/10000<0.
Its trace-normalized value is -122169/34900, explaining the numerical value near-3.5005444. The exact function, not a numerical rank determination, proves the seed's failure.
This also yields a useful specific test for schedules (H,2,C), with H>=0 and1<=C<=2: the same function starting at H+1 gives endpoints1,-1,C-1, so any primitive certificate must satisfy
(H+C+3)(C-1)<=H+1.
This is an additional witness condition, not a complete characterization of such schedules.

4. What remains.
The experiment found no nonbasic saturated seed with a proved positive gain and hence supplied no asymptotic negative construction. It does not establish that all primitive or all GD schedules are basic. The next analytic upper-bound attempt must change the invariant, rather than enlarge this scan or claim that numerical failure settles the seed question. In particular, a start/end gradient potential can retain cross terms omitted by a scalar primitive capacity; its one-step and two-step identities should be derived before any recursive closure is asserted.

Software references consulted for implementation only:
https://www.cvxpy.org/tutorial/constraints/index.html
https://clarabel.org/stable/api_settings/

# Exact cross-block moments and an endpoint-state collision

Status: unverified exact identities and an explicit counterexample to a proposed compression. No new optimization or verifier call. A competitive closed moment certificate is not yet available.

Work at smoothness1 with

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.

Define transformed sample coordinates and values

    z_i=x_i-g_i, F_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.

Then the actual interpolation slack is

    I_ij=-Q_ij=F_j-F_i-<g_i,z_j-z_i> >=0.           (1)

## 1. A finite exact moment tuple

For any nonnegative weights u on actual samples define

    D=sum u_i, U=sum u_i g_i, Z=sum u_i z_i,
    M=sum u_i F_i,
    N=sum u_i(<g_i,z_i>-F_i),
    C=M+N=sum u_i<g_i,z_i>.

The letters M,N here denote weighted function moments, not a PSD matrix or a horizon. N is the conjugate-value moment; this notation avoids reusing the primitive capacity K.

For a block beginning at x_start and ending at x_end with value f_end, its intrinsic objective energy satisfies exactly

    E=2<U,x_start>-||U||^2-2N-2D f_end.             (2)

For two weighted sets a,b of samples from the SAME function, rank-one sums of every directed cross edge satisfy

    T_ab=sum_(i in a,j in b) u_i v_j I_ij
        =D_a M_b+D_b N_a-<U_a,Z_b> >=0,
    T_ba=D_b M_a+D_a N_b-<U_b,Z_a> >=0.             (3)

These equations follow by expanding (1) and factoring the product weights. In particular the internal all-pairs sum is

    T_aa=D_a C_a-<U_a,Z_a> >=0.                    (4)

The moment tuple has an exact additive update. If the old head weights are scaled by alpha, the tail weights by beta, and extra weights eta,gamma are placed at the bridge point y and final point z, then

    D_new=alpha D_a+beta D_b+eta+gamma,
    U_new=alpha U_a+beta U_b+eta g_y+gamma g_z,
    Z_new=alpha Z_a+beta Z_b+eta z_y+gamma z_z,
    M_new=alpha M_a+beta M_b+eta F_y+gamma F_z,
    N_new=alpha N_a+beta N_b
             +eta(<g_y,z_y>-F_y)+gamma(<g_z,z_z>-F_z). (5)

Here z_y=y-g_y and z_z=z-g_z; the repeated z is a point name in the subscript and a transformed coordinate on the right. All positions and function values in (5) refer to their actual common global coordinates. There is no reset at the start of the tail. The actual final proof weights must be nonnegative. Equation(2) then computes the combined objective energy exactly.

This supplies a precise interface for the all-cross plan. It does not establish that the elementary inequalities(2)--(4) characterize all legal moments or provide a closed accelerated certificate.

## 2. Endpoint information, even including the dual slack, does not determine (3)

Consider the fixed physical schedule h=(1,1,1), start x_0=12/5, minimizer0 and proof weights u=(1,1,1,1). Construct two one-dimensional convex1-smooth functions A and B, each with f(0)=0. Their sample data are:

|i|x_i for A|g_i for A|f_i for A|x_i for B|g_i for B|f_i for B|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|0|12/5|1|293/200|12/5|1|293/200|
|1|7/5|7/10|123/200|7/5|23/30|47/72|
|2|7/10|1/2|39/200|19/30|13/30|283/1800|
|3|1/5|1/5|1/50|1/5|1/5|1/50|

Both columns satisfy x_(i+1)=x_i-g_i exactly.

For function A, linearly interpolate the displayed gradients between successive points. The three slopes from the first interval to the last are3/10,2/7,3/5, all in[0,1]. Set g(x)=x for x<=1/5 and g(x)=1 for x>=12/5. Integrating this continuous monotone1-Lipschitz derivative gives precisely the displayed values: its three drops f_i-f_(i+1) are17/20,21/50,7/40.

For function B, on each interval[x_(i+1),x_i] let L_i=g_i be its length, let delta_i=g_i-g_(i+1), and place a slope1 ramp of length delta_i after a lower flat piece of length a_i. Before the ramp the derivative is g_(i+1); after it the derivative is g_i. Choose

    (a_0,a_1,a_2)=(289/420,11/100,1/10).

The corresponding (L_i,delta_i) are(1,7/30),(23/30,1/3),(13/30,7/30). Each a_i lies in[0,L_i-delta_i]. The area under the derivative on such an interval is

    g_i L_i-delta_i^2/2-delta_i a_i.

It is respectively731/900,223/450,247/1800 for the three intervals, yielding the displayed function values from f_3=1/50. Extend the derivative by x below1/5 and by1 above12/5. This again gives one globally defined continuous, nondecreasing1-Lipschitz derivative, and its integral is minimized at zero. Thus both constructions satisfy the full function assumptions, not just adjacent interpolation tests.

## 3. All proposed endpoint data agree, but a needed reverse sum differs

For both trajectories,

    D=4, d=x_0-x_3=11/5, U=12/5, e=U-d=1/5,
    p=g_0=1, q=g_3=1/5,
    f_0=293/200, f_3=1/50, f_0-f_3=289/200,
    E=443/100.

The physical primitive energy, for S=3 and capacity4, is V=E-12q^2=79/20 on both instances. Their primitive dual expression is also the same:

    H=2(f_0-f_3)-6q^2-|p-(d+q)/4|^2=249/100.

These equalities use the direct definitions; they do not assume any unverified primitive theorem for the schedule.

Nevertheless their weighted proximal-point sums and primal moments differ:

    Z_A=23/10, M_A=281/200,
    Z_B=67/30, M_B=2497/1800.

The conjugate moment N is the same, as equation(2) already implies from the shared endpoint data. At the terminal point z_3=x_3-g_3=0 and F_3=f_3-q^2/2=0. Consequently the reverse-anchor sum is

    sum_i u_i I_(3,i)=M-qZ.

It equals189/200=1701/1800 on A and1693/1800 on B, a difference of1/225. Thus no formula depending only on the common tuple(D,E,d,U,e,p,q,f_0,f_3,V,H) can recover this reverse interpolation sum for all legal trajectories.

This is a counterexample to exact endpoint compression, not to inequalities that bound that sum using the tuple, and not to carrying the larger moment tuple from part1.

## 4. The open quantitative step

Equation(3) makes the missing quantities explicit. Substituting M=C-N trades the missing primal value moment for the within-block mixed moment C=sum u_i<g_i,z_i>. The inequality(4) provides only a lower bound on C. In a proposed energy certificate the coefficient of C after subtracting a positive cross-edge sum can have the opposite sign, so (4) cannot silently be used as an upper bound.

A successful all-cross join must retain a valid bound with the correct sign for this moment, or preserve C and its function-value counterpart through the next join. Additivity(5) alone is not that bound. This is the precise remaining mechanism in the moment plan; no exact-profile improvement is claimed.

# Endpoint cross-term fusion: concrete tests

Status: unverified candidate derivations; not a target solution and not a fact. Initial-wave verification allowance already used.

Cross-term fusion screen, beyond the earlier scalar-deficit rule: (i) the rational motif [3/2,3/2] has an exact saturated primitive certificate with nonlocal interpolation multipliers, proving that cross terms can repair scalar deficits; (ii) nevertheless pairing any known ZJ objective block of length n with its reverse cannot improve the primitive DP capacity at length 2n, because S_circle(2n)+1>2S_bullet(n)+1 for n>=1; (iii) an explicit asymmetric two-bridge rule promising a larger three-block capacity fails already on the primitive block [sqrt(2)] by a legal one-dimensional Huber example. These are unverified route-screening findings, not target results.

Notation. Put P_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, with exact DP definitions and normalized worst-case W_n as in fact 0be8b54e0a33381c. Define homogeneous scalar joins
F(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt(A^2+6AB+B^2))/2,
G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2
for positive A,B,D. The exact recurrences are
P_0=B_0=1,
P_n=max_{k+l+1=n} F(P_k,P_l),
B_n=max_{k+l+1=n} G(P_k,B_l).

1. A real cross-term repair, with no bridge.
For a nonnegative schedule h of length n and sum s, define the intrinsic potential
V_h(C)=2 sum_{i<n} h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_{i<n}h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-C||g_n||^2.
This is the same potential as in candidate 79da55e26c4a7406: the cross terms from the GD displacement cancel exactly.
Let Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0 for convex 1-smooth functions, with x_{i+1}=x_i-h_i g_i.
For h=[3/2,3/2], s=3 and C=s(s+1)=12, the following formal identity holds:
V_h(12)=(7/2)(-Q_10)+(-Q_01)+14(-Q_21)+(17/2)(-Q_12)+(-Q_20)+(1/2)(-Q_02).
All six multipliers are nonnegative. To check it, put u=g_0,v=g_1,w=g_2, x_1=x_0-(3/2)u and x_2=x_1-(3/2)v. Function coefficients become 3(f_0-f_1)+6(f_1-f_2); squared-gradient coefficients become -3||u||^2/4-3||v||^2/4-12||w||^2; and all three cross coefficients cancel. This is precisely V_h(12).
Thus a bridge-free two-block proof with retained history inequalities repairs the deficit of the one-step [3/2] seed. It is a concrete demonstration that the scalar-deficit obstruction does not forbid richer fusion. However its mass is 4, below P_2=F(1,1+sqrt(2)), so this test seed is not an improvement.

2. Exact capacity screen for pairing ZJ objective schedules with their reversals.
We prove the scalar inequality
F(A,F(D,A))>G(A,D) for A,D>0.
By homogeneity set A=1 and d=D/A. Let q=F(1,d), z=F(1,q), g=G(1,d). The defining quadratic identities give
d=q(q-1)/(q+1), q=z(z-1)/(z+1),
so d=z(z-1)(z^2-2z-1)/[(z+1)(z^2+1)] and z>1+sqrt(2)>2.
The larger root g>2 satisfies d=g-4+4/g. Direct expansion yields
(z-4+4/z)-d=4/[z(z+1)(z^2+1)]>0.
The function t maps to t-4+4/t is strictly increasing for t>2, since its derivative is 1-4/t^2>0. Hence z>g and the displayed inequality follows after restoring A.

We now prove P_{2n}>=B_n for every n>=0, strictly for n>=1. At n=0 both equal 1. For n>=1 choose a maximizing split k+l+1=n for B_n. By induction P_{2l}>=B_l. The primitive DP permits the two joins
P_{2n}>=F(P_k,F(P_{2l},P_k))
>=F(P_k,F(B_l,P_k))
>G(P_k,B_l)=B_n.
All indices are valid: the inner length is 2l+k+1 and the outer length is 2l+2k+2=2n. This is a pure algebraic property of the actual DP, not an assertion of unrestricted schedule optimality.

If h is a ZJ objective schedule of length n and sum S_bullet(n), the concatenated schedule [h,reverse(h)] has length 2n and shifted sum 2S_bullet(n)+1=B_n. Even if a new richer proof established that this concatenation is primitive, its capacity would still be strictly below P_{2n}, so every subsequent ordinary ConPP/ConPD construction using it can replace it by the existing same-length primitive DP block with a greater scalar capacity. Thus this fusion route using only existing ZJ objective blocks cannot furnish the requested strict improvement. General f/g-to-primitive closure was not assumed or claimed.
A nonbasic f/g seed of length n would need shifted pair mass strictly larger than P_{2n} even to escape this exact capacity screen, and then would still need a valid primitive certificate.

3. A hostile test of a different, direct three-block join.
Choose exact constants c=1/4 and d=(-5+5sqrt(17))/8-1=(5sqrt(17)-13)/8. Then 0<d<1 and
c d (3+c+d)=1.
Let M=3+c+d. A tempting homogeneous triple-primitive rule, on a primitive block a of shifted sum A=1+s(a), is
J(a)=[a, 1+cA, a, 1+dA, a].
Its shifted sum is M A. Because c d M=1, it balances the full-curvature quadratic endpoint product when the seed block has product 1/A; M exceeds the ordinary two-join factor F(1,F(1,1)) (numerically M is about 4.20194, versus about 4.01545). Quadratic/Huber balance alone therefore makes it an attractive but insufficient candidate.

It is not a universally primitive join. Take the exact primitive one-step block a=[sqrt(2)], write A=1+sqrt(2), and let
P=2sqrt(2)+1+cA
be the sum of all steps before the larger bridge b=1+dA. The tail is the one step sqrt(2). Define the convex 1-smooth function
f(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1,
f(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1,
with minimizer 0. Its derivative is min(x,1), continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz.
Start at x_0=P+1=(2+c)A. Every step before b occurs with derivative 1 and the point immediately before b is 1. The bridge sends it to -dA. The final sqrt(2) step is taken in the quadratic region and yields
x_final=(sqrt(2)-1)dA=d,
which lies in (0,1). At every trajectory point, Q_{i,star}=f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2=0: the identity holds in both pieces of this explicit function.
For a saturated primitive schedule of shifted sum R=1+s, its potential would require x_0^2>=R^2 x_final^2 whenever the final point is in the quadratic piece and all Q_{i,star}=0. Here R=M A, so it would require
(2+c)^2 A^2 >= M^2 A^2 d^2.
But Md=1/c=4, whereas 2+c=9/4; the inequality is strictly false. Hence the proposed primitive interface is refuted exactly, in dimension one.
This hostile test rules out that uniform join rule; it does not exclude a horizon-dependent rule valid only for a different restricted block family. Any such reopening must supply a new certificate and re-test the full smooth-convex class.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000758 -->
# The optimal three-step objective schedule needs more than endpoint proof-weight corrections

Status: complete unverified counterexample to a proposed certificate template. This is not a counterexample to the schedule, which is a standard optimal recursive objective schedule. No verifier call.

For a schedule h of length n and sum S, consider the restrictive proof-weight template

  u_0=h_0+a,
  u_i=h_i for1<=i<n,
  u_n=b.

Here a,b are real, and actual weights must be nonnegative. If the weight mass is the Huber-tight value D=2S+1, then b=S+1-a. Write d=x_0-x_n, U=sum_i u_i g_i, e=U-d. This template has the exact linear relation

  e=a g_0+b g_n.                                    (1)

The intrinsic objective certificate is E_h(u)>=0, where

  E_h(u)=2sum_i u_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_i u_i||g_i||^2
          +2sum_i u_i<g_i,x_0-x_i>-||U||^2.

For L=1 and minimizer0 it satisfies the exact identity

  E_h(u)=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)
           -2D f_n-||x_n-e||^2.                    (2)

The template is attractive because a one-step optimal schedule h=(3/2) has weights(2,2), hence a=1/2,b=2, and E=4(-Q_10)>=0. But it already fails on a basic three-step objective schedule.

Set t=sqrt(2), lambda=1+t, and

  h=(t,lambda,3/2),
  S=5/2+2t,
  D=2S+1=6+4t=2lambda^2.

This schedule is ConPD((sqrt(2)),(3/2)). The bridge is lambda, by direct substitution in psi. The argument below only needs the explicit steps and two legal equality examples; it does not assume its optimality among all schedules.

## Quadratic equality fixes the sole free endpoint parameter

Use f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1. All Q_(i,star)=0, and

  x_3=p=(1-t)(1-lambda)(1-3/2)
       =-1/(2+t)=-1/sqrt(D).

Thus2D f_3=Dp^2=1. From(1)–(2),

  E_h(u)=-[p-a-bp]^2.

Any valid nonnegative intrinsic energy must therefore have a+(b-1)p=0. Substituting b=S+1-a gives the unique value

  a=-Sp/(1-p)=S/(sqrt(D)+1)=lambda/2,
  b=S+1-a=(3/2)(2+t).                              (3)

The associated weights are

  u=((1+3t)/2,lambda,3/2,(3/2)(2+t)),

and all are positive.

## The middle-checkpoint Huber equality contradicts those weights

Put c=1/lambda and q=t/(4lambda). Define the one-dimensional convex1-smooth function

  f(x)=x^2/2 for -q<=x<=c,
  f(x)=cx-c^2/2 for x>=c,
  f(x)=-qx-q^2/2 for x<=-q.

Its derivative is x clipped to[-q,c], which is nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz; f is differentiable, f(0)=min f=0. Starting at x_0=1, the specified GD steps give

  x_1=c,
  x_2=c-1=-t/lambda=-4q,
  x_3=-4q+(3/2)q=-(5/2)q,
  (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(c,c,-q,-q).

Every sampled point is on an outer affine piece or its boundary. Therefore Q_(i,star)=f_i-g_i x_i+g_i^2/2=0 at all four samples. Also

  f_3=(5/2)q^2-q^2/2=2q^2,
  2f_3=4q^2=1/(2lambda^2)=1/D.

This is the middle-cut equality construction from accepted facts b98bb807d514e80d and1fca3e5dd555cb4a, with prefix sum t, checkpoint lambda, and tail sum3/2; all trajectory details are included here.

For the only possible template weights(3),

  U=(u_0+u_1)c-(u_2+u_3)q
    =(6+11t)/(8lambda),
  U-1=(3t-2)/(8lambda)>0.

Since e=U-(1-x_3), one has x_3-e=1-U. Equation(2) now yields

  E_h(u)=-[(3sqrt(2)-2)/(8(1+sqrt(2)))]^2<0.

Thus no choice of a,b in the endpoint-only template can certify the Huber-tight denominator D for this schedule: the quadratic forces(3), and a second legal equality witness refutes it. The original schedule remains valid with a different weight vector. In the usual ConPD construction, one may use the nonnegative weights

  (sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2)),

which have mass D and adjust internal indices as well as endpoints.

The scope is precise. This rules out modifying only the initial and terminal entries of the physical-step weight vector. It does not exclude all fixed-dimensional potentials, all gradient-aggregate residuals, or another certificate with internal anchor weights. It explains why retaining a single initial-gradient correction while insisting every internal weight equal the physical step is too narrow for the upper-construction program.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000759 -->
# A genuine finite objective residual and its exact block transport

Status: unverified exact identities and a scoped limitation. No solver or verifier calls. The eleven-step rule with bridge factors(7/10,1,1/5) is closed by max3's explicit counterexample, which was read and matches the present primitive energy definition. None of the constructions below attempts to repair that rule.

Use smoothness1 and Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0. For nonnegative proof weights u on a GD block write

    D=sum u_i, d=x_start-x_end, U=sum u_i g_i, e=U-d,
    E=2sum u_i(f_i-f_end)+sum u_i||g_i||^2
       +2sum u_i<g_i,x_start-x_i>-||U||^2.

For any minimizer, direct expansion gives

    ||x_start-x_star||^2+2sum u_i Q_(i,star)-2D(f_end-f_star)
       =||x_end-x_star-e||^2+E.                      (1)

Thus E>=0 yields the original last-iterate bound W<=1/D.

## 1. A nonprimitive proof-weight state with no loss of Huber mass

The physical schedule is the pair(3/2,3/2). Write its three gradients as p,m,q, its displacement as d=(3/2)(p+m), and define

    t=d-3q, r=p-(d+q)/4.

For 0<epsilon<=1/64, choose actual proof weights

    u=(3/2-epsilon, 3/2, 4+epsilon).

Then D=7 and

    e=4q-epsilon(p-q), v=e-4q=-epsilon(r+t/4).       (2)

These are not the primitive weights(h,4), although the physical block is primitive. All three weights are positive. The exact interpolation identity is

    E=(21epsilon/2)(-Q_01)+(1-5epsilon/4)(-Q_02)
        +(4+29epsilon/4)(-Q_10)
        +(9+29epsilon/4)(-Q_12)+(16+4epsilon)(-Q_21)
        +(r,t)^T M_epsilon (r,t),                    (3)

where the full symmetric matrix is

    M_epsilon =
      [ 2epsilon-epsilon^2, -31epsilon/12-epsilon^2/4 ]
      [ -31epsilon/12-epsilon^2/4,
                         1/9+7epsilon/36-epsilon^2/16 ].

Every actual interpolation coefficient in (3) is nonnegative. Equation (3) follows either by expanding E and the five Q terms directly, or by the following retained-slack derivation, which also explains the cross term.

Let V be the pair's physical primitive energy and H=2(f_0-f_2)-6||q||^2-||r||^2. Define the adjacent cycle quantities C_01=-Q_01-Q_10 and C_12=-Q_12-Q_21. They satisfy

    C_01=3<q,r>-<q,t>/4-||r||^2
                         +(7/6)<r,t>-13||t||^2/144,
    C_12=-(3/2)<q,r>+(5/8)<q,t>+||r||^2/2
                         -(5/12)<r,t>+25||t||^2/288.

The correlated identity in max4_joint_boundary_storage.md gives, more generally,

    V-epsilon H
      =(epsilon/2)(-Q_01)+(1-5epsilon/4)(-Q_02)
        +(4-11epsilon/4)(-Q_10)
        +(9-19epsilon/4)(-Q_12)+(16-8epsilon)(-Q_21)
        +(16-15epsilon)||t||^2/144.                  (4)

This can be checked using the one-parameter primitive identity there with
kappa=(16-15epsilon)/(64-28epsilon), so that
(1-7epsilon/16)kappa=(16-15epsilon)/64.

The exact change from V to E has q-cross terms 12epsilon<q,r>+5epsilon<q,t>. The nonnegative combination 10epsilon C_01+12epsilon C_12 has exactly those same q-cross terms. Subtracting its quadratic expression, and adding it to the actual interpolation coefficients in (4), yields (3). This is why keeping only the two scalar slack bounds would have missed the finite objective state: the adjacent cycles supply the required correlations with q.

Set nu=1/(2epsilon). The matrix after subtracting nu||v||^2 from (3) is

    M_epsilon-(epsilon/2)(1,1/4)(1,1/4)^T.           (5)

Its first diagonal entry is (3/2)epsilon-epsilon^2>0, and its determinant is

    epsilon[1/6-(1037/144)epsilon-(29/18)epsilon^2]. (6)

For epsilon<=1/64 the bracket is at least1321/24576>0. Thus (5) is positive definite, proving the concrete finite residual certificate

    E >= nu ||e-4q||^2, D=7, nu=1/(2epsilon).        (7)

The mass7 is exactly the physical Huber mass2sum(h)+1. No limiting argument is used to make (7) valid: every indicated positive epsilon gives an actual finite certificate. The family has nu tending to infinity as the weights tend to the primitive weights.

## 2. A transport with a nonprimitive input and an inherited quadratic term

Let a prefix have nonnegative weights, D_a=2K-1 with K>=1, and the actual finite certificate

    E_a >= nu||e_a-Kg||^2, nu>0,

where g is the gradient at its endpoint y. The pair construction above provides K=4 and any nu=1/(2epsilon), epsilon<=1/64.

Take an arbitrary tail block, starting at y'=y-tau g and ending at z. Its own nonnegative weights v have mass D_b>0, gradient aggregate U_b, energy E_b>=0, start gradient p_b and final gradient q_b. The four vectors g,p_b,q_b,U_b remain distinct. Nothing below identifies the tail start gradient with the bridge gradient, or replaces its aggregate by an endpoint gradient.

Put

    sigma=1/(nu+1), k=1+1/nu, eta=1-sigma=1/k,
    c=(2K-sigma)/D_b,
    beta=(1+sqrt(1+4k c))/(2k),
    tau=1+K/(k beta).

Thus k beta^2=beta+c. Add eta to the prefix terminal proof weight and scale every tail weight by beta. All new proof weights are nonnegative and

    D_new=2K-sigma+beta D_b=k beta^2 D_b.             (8)

Write v_a=e_a-Kg. The actual tail-to-anchor interpolation sum is

    A_y=-2sum_(i in tail) v_i Q_(i,y)
       =2D_b(f_y-f_z)-E_b-||U_b||^2
          -2(tau-1)<U_b,g>-D_b||g||^2.              (9)

Expansion of the new intrinsic energy and substitution of (9) gives exactly

    E_new=(E_a-nu||v_a||^2)+(beta+c)E_b+c A_y
          +(1/nu)||nu v_a-eta g-beta U_b||^2
          +sigma(2K-1)||g||^2.                      (10)

This is a finite-horizon certificate with actual multipliers c v_i on -2Q_(i,y). It preserves the squared boundary quantity nu v_a-eta g-beta U_b; resetting it at a join is not part of the identity. The tail's p_b and q_b have zero explicit rows in the quadratic remainder because (9) has eliminated the full tail value sum exactly, not because those gradients were equated.

For completeness, the full nonzero boundary matrix in(v_a,g,U_b) is

    [ nu,       -eta,                         -beta          ]
    [ -eta, eta^2/nu+sigma(2K-1),          eta beta/nu       ]
    [ -beta,   eta beta/nu,                  beta^2/nu      ].

It is the sum of the outer product (nu,-eta,-beta)(nu,-eta,-beta)^T/nu and diag(0,sigma(2K-1),0), hence PSD in every Euclidean dimension. If the independent gradients p_b,q_b are added to the boundary vector, their rows and columns are zero. Equation (1) now gives W<=1/D_new for the actual concatenated physical schedule.

To verify (10) without trusting a completion of squares, the raw quadratic coefficients before subtracting the square are

    ||v_a||^2: nu,
    <v_a,g>: -2eta,       <v_a,U_b>: -2beta,
    ||g||^2: sigma(2K-sigma),
    ||U_b||^2: c+beta-beta^2=beta^2/nu,
    <g,U_b>: 2[beta(tau-eta-K)+c(tau-1)]
               =2eta beta/nu.

The g-diagonal remainder equals sigma(2K-1), using eta=nu/(nu+1).

## 3. Calibration succeeds, but this closed subrule cannot improve ConPD

For fixed K and D_b, as nu tends to infinity the parameters in part2 tend to the ordinary ConPD parameters and

    D_new -> G(K,D_b).

There is no fixed calibration loss forced by a finite nonprimitive input: the actual pair family in part1 supplies arbitrarily large finite nu while retaining D_a=7. If desired, nu may be selected separately for each prescribed horizon.

Nevertheless, the specific transport in part2 always gives strictly LESS mass than the ordinary ConPD join. Let b_0>0 solve b_0^2-b_0=2K/D_b, so G(K,D_b)=2K+b_0 D_b. Since k>1 and c=(2K-sigma)/D_b<2K/D_b, its positive root beta satisfies beta<b_0. Hence

    D_new=2K-sigma+beta D_b < G(K,D_b).               (11)

Thus (10) is an actual finite-residual propagation and calibration test, not an improving upper-bound rule. Repeating this inequality with no further correlations cannot certify a strict gain over the corresponding primitive-prefix G operations.

There is also a legal hard-instance explanation for the lack of a free local gain. For a prefix with physical sum K-1, one bridge tau=1+r, and a tail with physical sum T, the accepted one-cut fact b98bb807d514e80d gives

    W >= r^2/[K^2(2T+1)].

If a proposed certificate were Huber-tight with mass2(K+T+r)+1, it would have to satisfy

    r^2[2(K+T+r)+1] <= K^2(2T+1).                  (12)

When the tail's own mass is2T+1, ordinary ConPD meets this condition at equality. The affine prefix and tail portions of this witness have t=H=0 for every embedded pair. Consequently the retained pair terms cannot by themselves permit a larger Huber-tight single bridge. This statement concerns this binary setting and does not exclude coordinated multi-bridge certificates or nonsaturated objective weights.

## Current next step

The candidate storage is now an actual finite-weight state with a full PSD transport, rather than just a formal five-variable test. Its unchanged scalar subrule is quantitatively dominated by ConPD. Further work must use additional cross-block interpolation or the explicit inherited vector nu v_a-eta g-beta U_b to produce a different closed state; retuning the parameters in (10) cannot give the required strict improvement.
